Original prompt: Review the delete decks piece, it appears to trigger on the first press but then it doesn't confirm the delete and afterwards it does not trigger at all. Once deleted it should be remove from the screen instantly

- August 24, 2026 Hardened the saved-deck Delete flow in `home.js`. Arming used to call `renderSavedDecks()`, which rewrites the whole `#customDeckGrid` innerHTML: with 13 saved decks that re-creates every tile and re-decodes each deck-art background, so the grid reflows and the scroll position moves out from under the finger — the confirming second tap lands on a different node, and nothing deletes. Arming and disarming now mutate only the one button (`paintSavedDeckDeleteButton`), so the tile nodes and scroll offset are untouched. Three further traps closed: the busy guard was global (`if (state.deckDeleteBusy) return`), so one request that never settled made *every* Delete button inert — it is now scoped to the deck being deleted and cleared in a `finally`, so an unexpected throw cannot leave a deck permanently undeletable; the armed state had no cancel other than its own timer, so a capture-phase document click listener disarms on any tap that isn't a Delete button; and the arm window went 4s -> 6s. Per the request, the confirmed tile is removed from `state.profile.savedDecks` and re-rendered **before** the request goes out, and restored in place (with the existing "Deck not deleted" notice) if the server rejects it. Cache pin: `home.js` 151 -> 152 on `home.html`.
- Verification: `node --check js/home.js` clean. New Playwright harness (`scratchpad/verify.js`, run against `./mvnw spring-boot:run` with `/api/auth/me` and `/api/profile/decks/delete` route-mocked to a 13-deck custom binder) drives the real bundle at 390x844 and 1920x1080: 13/13 checks green on both — two taps delete, the tile disappears within 50ms of the confirm tap (before the response), a second delete works immediately after, delete still works after an arm expiry and after a rejected request (which restores the tile plus notice), tapping elsewhere disarms, arming leaves the existing tile DOM nodes and scroll offset untouched, and a hung delete on one deck still lets another deck arm. Proved discriminating: the same suite on the pre-fix bundle fails exactly the two structural checks — "tapping elsewhere disarms" and "arming keeps the existing tile nodes (no grid rebuild)". Backend untouched.

Original prompt: Test those cases / fix any bugs and merge

- August 24, 2026 #683 and #691 shipped guarded only by source-text assertions in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` — `extractFunction(game.js, ...).contains("/api/match/forfeit")` and friends. That proves the code was *written*, not that it *runs*: a typo in adjacent logic, a wrong variable or an early return above the fix all still pass. Both sit on user-facing money/match paths, so they were the weakest-guarded of the ten fixes merged this session. New in-repo harness `tests/play-match-and-profile-check.cjs` (self-contained server + Playwright, matching the existing `tests/*.cjs` convention) drives the real bundles instead: for **#691** it seeds a live `gameState.multiplayer` + `multiplayerSession`, stubs `window.fetch`, and asserts a started match posts `/api/match/forfeit` (never the host-only `/api/match/close`), that the forfeit precedes the loadout reopening, and that an unstarted lobby still closes without forfeiting; for **#683** it seeds a prior same-user profile carrying `progression`, returns an authenticated `/api/auth/me` body with `progression` omitted, and asserts the shared `sieglingsAuthProfile` cache keeps the prior snapshot — and that a *different* user id does not inherit it. `leaveOnlineMatch`/`syncAuthProfileNow` are top-level declarations so they hang off `window`; `gameState`/`multiplayerSession`/`authState` are top-level `let` bindings reachable by assignment from page scope. No production code changed.
- Verification: 9/9 checks green against current `main`. Both bugs were reproduced to prove the harness discriminates rather than merely passing — against `44fb1dbc^` (pre-#691, which also predates #683) **4 checks fail**: the started match posts `/api/match/close` with no forfeit, the forfeit-ordering check fails with it, and the progression cache is written with `progression: null`; against `e26e2ecd^` (pre-#683, which *has* #691's fix) **exactly 1 check fails** — the progression one — with all six forfeit checks passing, so the two guards are independent and each detects only its own regression. Full `./mvnw test` on current `main` is **557/557 BUILD SUCCESS**. Correction recorded: an earlier claim in this session that #699 shipped was wrong — it is closed unmerged, and the live check that appeared to confirm it grepped `meta.timed`/`timed: true`, strings that already existed on `main` via the pre-existing `statusRounds` status-chip code; the discriminating marker is `unitEffects`, which is absent from `main` and from production.

Original prompt: There are two more handle th so how do we handle them / complete the job and clean my pr queue

- August 24, 2026 Closed out the last two unlanded PRs. **#702** (Siege end rewards lost after a failed progression save) merged as `4c2691e5`; the reported conflict was misleading — it looked like the branch deleted `GET /api/siege/run/active`, but that endpoint was added to `main` *after* the branch forked, so nothing removed it and `main`'s copy was simply kept alongside the branch's one real controller change (threading `Authorization` into `/api/siege/state`). The actual hazard merged **silently**: `SiegeService` came through with no conflict markers but two definitions of `state(String, String)`, because the branch and `main` had each added that overload in a different place — it does not compile, and git cannot flag it. The two bodies also did different jobs (the branch retried end rewards whose progression save failed; `main` adopted an orphaned device-local run for its signed-in owner), so they were merged into one method with **adoption first**, since `maybeRetryEndRewards` banks to the run's owner and a run adopted on that same call must still be able to pay out. Taking either side alone would have dropped a fix. The line-puzzle half of #702 (`st.drawing = null` in `lineMove` plus its guard) had already landed on `main` separately, so it is a no-op now. **#699** (live buffs and afflictions in the Siege unit detail popup) was closed as superseded rather than merged: `main` already ships the same feature as `battleUnitEffects` — shield, attack buff, speed-over-base, max-HP bonus and per-status rows reusing `STATUS_META.tip` — wired into two call sites. What `main` lacked was #699's one real insight, and it was a live display bug: the client appended the round count whenever `rounds > 0`, but `SiegeCombatEngine` gives BURN/POISON `BURN_ROUNDS = 99` for the whole battle and STUN/LEECH/SHOCK/DISORIENT/INSIGHT/BLIND a 2-round safety net they never actually spend, so a burning unit's popup read "1 damage at end of round · 99 rounds". Only SLOW/SOAK/RUST/CURSE/WITHER run a countable clock; those five are now marked `timed: true` in `STATUS_META` and the suffix is gated on `meta.timed && rounds > 0`. Extracting that one fix was worth more than force-merging a redundant feature. Cache pin: `adventure.js` 70 -> 71 on `adventure.html`.
- Verification: `node --check js/adventure.js` clean. New guard `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest#statusRoundsPrintOnlyForStatusesThatRunAClock` asserts the gate is present, that the five clocked statuses carry `timed: true`, and that BURN/POISON/STUN/BLIND do not — and it was **proved to reproduce the bug**: with the `meta.timed` gate removed the test fails ("The round counter must be gated on the status actually running a clock"), and passes again once restored, so it is not a vacuous assertion. #702 was verified at **552/552 BUILD SUCCESS** on the full suite in an isolated worktree, after an amend: the duplicate-method fix had been left unstaged, so `git commit --no-edit` captured the merge's broken version and the first pushed commit did not compile — caught by re-checking the committed tree rather than the working copy, then re-verified from a fresh worktree of the commit itself.

Original prompt: critical bug hunt (cron)

- August 11, 2026 Critical fix for Siege end-of-run payout loss. `grantEndRewards` always set `endRewardsGranted=true` even when the authenticated progression `save` threw, and `continueRun` returned early once `battle == null`, so a transient Firestore blip permanently stranded Siegecoins/Remnants/card prizes (UI showed the guest "Sign in…" copy for a signed-in player). The flag now seals only after a successful claim or for true guests; failed banks keep the already-shown spoils map for an identical retry. `state` and `continueRun` re-attempt the bank when a finished run is still in memory, `/api/siege/state` forwards `Authorization` (cookie-bridged), and `adventure.js` refetches state on the result screen when spoils are unclaimed for a signed-in player. Cache pin `adventure.js` 60 → 61.
- Verification: `node --check` on `adventure.js`; `./mvnw -Dtest=SiegeEndRewardsTest,AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest,GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#siegeRunMenuSavesBeforeQuitAndRestartsOnlyAfterServerAbandon,GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#siegeBattleMapsStayStaticSmallAndOrientationMatched test`.

- August 22, 2026 Critical: a leftover Siege client could steal the account resume pointer after a newer expedition started. `#710` made `/api/siege/run/active` the cross-device source of truth, and `deleteForUser` already refuses to erase a pointer that has moved on — but `saveCheckpoint` still did an unconditional `saveForUser`. Trigger: phone is mid-expedition (token T1 in memory + account slot); desktop taps "Start a new run" (does not abandon) and starts T2, which overwrites the account slot; phone then travels or hits Save, and T1 writes the account document back. Desktop refresh shows the old phone run; T2 is orphaned from account resume. Fix: ordinary checkpoints skip the account write when the slot already names a different token; `newRun` evicts the previous token from memory and Firestore before taking the pointer. Guarded by `SiegeRunSaveTest` (`aSupersededRunCannotStealTheAccountResumePointer`, `theSameRunCanStillUpdateItsAccountCheckpoint`, `startingANewRunEvictsThePreviousAccountSave`).
- Verification: `./mvnw -q -Dtest=SiegeRunSaveTest,SiegeContinueCheckpointTest,SiegeModeSaveSlotTest test` green (plain JUnit, no Spring context). No JS/CSS, no cache-pin bump.

- August 21, 2026 Battlegrounds starts never stamped the signed-in `ownerId` on the run (`newRun` does). `checkpoint()` therefore skipped the account document and only wrote the opaque token snapshot, so `/api/siege/run/active` — which the client prefers over `localStorage.siegeToken` whenever *any* account save exists — never listed the march. Concrete trigger: have an expedition save, start a Battlegrounds run, refresh `/siege`; the resume prompt showed only the expedition, and continuing it overwrote the device token so the march was unreachable. Same gap hid a Battlegrounds run from every other device. Fix: `newBattlegrounds` now sets `ownerId` before the first checkpoint, same as `newRun`. Guard: `SiegeModeSaveSlotTest#startingBattlegroundsWritesTheAccountSaveSlot`.
- Verification: `./mvnw -q -Dtest=SiegeModeSaveSlotTest test` (see run below). No JS/CSS; cache pins unchanged.

Original prompt: Hunt CRITICAL correctness bugs in recent areas (#678/#671/#680/#672, shop pack idempotency, Siege, auth, Keep)

- August 8, 2026 Critical correctness fixes from the bug hunt: (1) #680 Join With Code abandoned live matches by calling `openLoadoutSelector()` after confirm, which only clears local `multiplayerSession`/`gameState` — never `/api/match/forfeit` or `/api/game/forfeit` — so opponents were stranded with no forfeit win. `play-hud.js` now forfeits server-side first (`abandonActiveMatchForJoinCode`). (2) `PlayerProgressionService.purchaseDeck` (and other shop writes) did not share the pack-open per-user lock while `PlayerProgressionStore.save` is a full-document Firestore `set`, so a pack open concurrent with a deck unlock could drop cards or `purchasedDeckIds`; all progression write paths now use `progressionWriteLock`. (3) `SiegeService.continueRun` / `extract` had no per-run monitor, so a timeout retry on Claim Rewards could double-apply gold/XP/end-rewards; both paths now `synchronized` on the in-memory `Session`. (4) Consolidated the still-open #683 Play progression-cache wipe into the same PR: friend actions / `/api/auth/me` can omit `progression` after an isolated Firestore read failure; `play-hud.js` `applyProfileResponse` and `game.js` `syncAuthProfileNow` now keep the prior same-user progression before writing `sieglingsAuthProfile` (Home starter-gate lockout). Cache pins: `game.js?v=238`, `play-hud.js?v=3`.
- Verification: `node --check` on `play-hud.js` and `game.js`; `./mvnw -Dtest=PlayerProgressionServiceTest,SiegeContinueCheckpointTest,GameJavaScriptRegressionTest test` → **94/94 green** (includes `playHudJoinWithCodeForfeitsActiveMatchBeforeClearingLocalSession`, `playAuthProfileMergesPreserveProgressionWhenOmitted`, `shopWritePathsShareTheProgressionLockWithPackOpens`, `continueRunSerializesOnTheSessionMonitor`).

- August 20, 2026 Siegelings unlocked by finishing an expedition were pickable in warband select and then rejected when the run actually started. `#711` taught `roster()` to treat `siegeUnlockedSieglings` as starters, and the client keys off that flag, but `SiegeService.newRun` still gated on `content.isExpeditionStarter(s)` alone — so finding Spoutyl, banking the unlock, and tapping Start returned `"Spoutyl is locked — find them on the expedition path first."` Both the picker and the start path now share `isSieglingSelectable` (catalog flag OR account unlock).
- Verification: `./mvnw -Dtest=SiegeSieglingUnlockTest test` **8/8 green**. New `startingARunRejectsACatalogLockedSieglingUntilItIsUnlocked` spies the catalog flag off for the lead card (so the case exists even when this environment has not flagged any starters), asserts `newRun` 400s for a guest, then starts successfully once `PlayerProgressionService.isSiegeSieglingUnlocked` is true for that id.

Original prompt: Chain damage animation should strike the target first then bounce to to chained targets from the initial target

Original prompt: critical bug hunt (cron)

- August 9, 2026 Critical fix for active `energy_boost` overcharge spends. `#684` records spends as negative temporary adjustments so a mid-setup recalculation cannot refund them, but temporary debt survives into Battle while overcharge is cleared in `startBattlePhase` before the full restore. Spending the surge on a Setup cast therefore left a debt that stole board-link energy for the fight (board 2 fire + overcharge 2, spend 2 → Battle restored 0 instead of 2). `EnergyService.consumeEnergy` now drains `Player.consumeOverchargeEnergy` first and only books the remainder as temporary debt.
- Verification: `./mvnw -Dtest=GameServiceTest,EffectServiceTest,EnergyServiceTest,BattleServiceTest test` → **82/82 green**. Headline case `spendingOverchargeDoesNotStealBoardEnergyWhenBattleBegins` drives board links (2 fire) + overcharge (+2) + spend (2) through reflective `startBattlePhase` and asserts Battle still has 2 fire with no temporary debt; existing overcharge spend/recalculate / fade / AI / claim-temp cases stay green.

Original prompt: critical bug investigation

- August 6, 2026 Mission panel still blanked when day/week rollover persist failed. `DailyMissionService.loadProgress` still called `progressStore.save` inside the snapshot read; a Firestore hiccup threw out of `GET /api/missions/daily` and blanked all three mission tabs (controller only caught `IllegalArgumentException`). Separately, `homeDailyMissions` used `featured || missions`, and empty `featured: []` is truthy in JS. Made rollover persist best-effort (`persistRollover`), returned a client-handled error envelope for unexpected snapshot failures, and length-checked featured before falling through. Cache pin `home.js?v=139`. Same fix previously opened as #674 / #586 / #585 and remained unmerged; verified still broken on `origin/main` before re-applying.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `home.js`. `DailyMissionServiceTest#snapshotStillServesWhenRolloverPersistFails` and `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#emptyFeaturedMissionsFallThroughToFullDailyList` green via `./mvnw -q -Dtest=DailyMissionServiceTest,GameJavaScriptRegressionTest test`.

- Verification and two live-only defects found: Chromium cannot complete TLS to the live host through this environment's egress proxy (`ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`, with no proxy-side failure recorded for the host), so rather than disable verification the **deployed bundles themselves** were mirrored locally — live `home.html`, `home.js`, `card-binder-visual.js`, `home.css`, `style.css` fetched from Hosting and served from a local stub whose `/api/game/options` returns the real live catalog (153 cards: 83 SIEGLING all carrying `OVERLAY` art, 47 SPELL, 23 TRAP), with 12 real card-art PNGs pulled from Firebase Storage. Driving that at 390x844 and 1920x1080 reproduced everything the mock catalog had shown (tab strip `grid` on mobile / `none` on desktop with all 153 rows, no `Owned x` in the list, scroll held across an add, 4 wind notch emblems on Aerovane's cost pill at 12px on one line, no page errors, no sideways scroll) **and surfaced two defects the synthetic catalog could not, because it had no real art**: (a) row thumbnails inherited the designer's inline art transform — real cards sit around `scale(1.67)`, framing tuned for the card's tall art window — so a 36px square showed a zoomed crop of the creature's middle; `renderCardRowThumb` now emits the image without `buildArtTransformStyle` and the CSS switched `cover` to `contain`, since a transparent cutout loses nothing to letterboxing. (b) The Card-tab hero preview rendered a 120x326 sliver — `.binder-card` is content-sized and its stat/cost/description body forced the height in the hero's narrow column — and the `OVERLAY` art, scaled 1.67 with `overflow: visible`, painted 40px into the identity text beside it. The hero now hides `.binder-card-body` (every value in it is already in the quickstat pills or one tab away in Overview) giving a 120x191 card shape, and pins the overlay art to the card with `transform: none !important` — `!important` because `renderBinderCardPreview` writes the framing as an inline style that no stylesheet rule can otherwise outrank; both rules are scoped to `.deck-builder-card-hero` so the full-size card face everywhere else keeps its authored framing. Re-measured after the fix: art rect exactly equals the card rect (120x191, zero spill in either axis), zero thumbnails exceeding their 36px mark box across the first 24 real rows with every image `complete && naturalWidth > 0`, and screenshots read back showing Aerovane/Applehead as creature cutouts and Aquatic Conductor/Arc Lash/Ashen Air as electric and fire notch art. Cache pins for the fix: `home.css` 133 -> 134 on `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`, `card-binder-visual.js` 21 -> 22 on `home.html`, `card-dashboard.html` and `play.html`.

Original prompt: Check the placement of text and the bottom of the display on landscape here. Also on landscape try to make the map full screen while preserving or creating a space to view the player menu where they can view Siegelings, items and a key to see what emblems mean for specific points. Also on the end game screen some Siegeling names were cut off

- August 23, 2026 Three landscape faults in Siege mode, all reproduced at 932x430/844x390 before the fix. (1) **Map**: `MAP.landLaneGap` was 60px while a node's label prints at `r+18` and runs ~11px tall, so every label landed on the circle of the lane below it (23 label/circle overlaps measured); lanes are now 78px apart with a 44px pad, which the extra height below pays for. The map itself was a bordered card inset by the app gutters with a 100px HUD block under it in the flex flow — in landscape the scroll canvas is now `position:absolute; inset:0` (no border/radius, full-bleed) with the top bar and the HUD floating over it exactly like the battle screen, and scroll padding (34px top / 78px bottom) instead of margins so nodes never park under either band while the canvas art still paints edge to edge. The HUD collapsed to one ~75px row — gold/deck/Items/Key/hint on the left, the warband strip taking the rest and scrolling sideways — which is the "space to view the player menu": Siegelings (the chips, and 🎒 Items for the full warband panel) plus a new **🗝️ Key** button opening a map-key overlay built from `NODE_LEGEND`/`NODE_STATE_LEGEND` (nine emblems with the same tinted ring the map draws, plus the four node states). `.inv-panel` also got `position:relative` so its absolutely-positioned ✕ pins to the panel corner instead of the viewport. (2) **Bottom of the display**: `.location-overlay` carried `max-height:78%` in the short-landscape query. The overlay lays its copy/choices out along the bottom edge, so that cap did not trim the scene — it lifted every control ~22vh up, leaving the dead band of art under the event/camp buttons visible in the report. Cap removed (children keep their own height caps). (3) **End-game screen**: in the landscape reward rail (196-270px wide) `.xp-recap-row` put the name and the level/bar columns side by side, ellipsing names down to a bare "…"; the row now stacks in that rail so the name gets the full width and wraps. Cache pins: `adventure.css` 65 -> 66, `adventure.js` 68 -> 69 in `adventure.html` (the only page that loads them).
- Verification: `node --check js/adventure.js` clean, `./mvnw -q -DskipTests compile` clean (no Java touched). New guard `tests/siege-landscape-screens-check.cjs` drives the real page against a mocked `/api/siege/**` run over map / event / camp / reward at 932x430, 844x390, 390x844 portrait and 1920x1080: label/circle overlaps 23 -> 0 at both landscape sizes, map canvas measured full-bleed (0,0..vw,vh) with a 75px bottom HUD, event choices bottom 327 -> 422 of a 430px viewport (camp grid 382 of 390), and all four recap names ("Draco the Emberwing", "Clawkid Skitterclaw", "Ser Airek Stonewarden", "Blinky") measured untruncated where three of four were cut before. Portrait and desktop measurements are unchanged apart from the portrait HUD growing 31px for the new Key button. `tests/siege-map-hud-portrait-check.cjs` re-run and passing; its two landscape assertions were updated to the new intent (gaps are now measured inside the scroll padding, and the mode badge is asserted to float on top of the full-bleed map rather than sit above it). `tests/mobile-overlap` was not run — `@playwright/test` is not installed in this offline environment — but it fixtures battle tiles, which this change does not touch.

Original prompt: Display card cost using notch emblems in the tab

- August 23, 2026 The Card tab's hero pill showed cost as a bare number (`COST 1`) while the card face and the Overview block below it already drew notch emblems, so the same value was rendered two different ways a few pixels apart. The pill now renders `renderBuilderCostEmblems(cost, costElement)` — one notch token per energy, using the same `notchIconStyle` background the battle table and binder cost rows use — and the standalone "Energy cost" block was dropped from the Overview tab along with the spell/trap text `Cost` row in `.detail-grid`, since both are now duplicates of the pill; the grid renders only when it still has rows, so a Strategy/Deception no longer draws an empty box. A dedicated compact renderer rather than reusing `renderBinderCardEnergyCost`: that one wraps at 6 emblems per line and draws up to 12, which grew the hero row and pushed the add stepper down on a high-cost card. This one caps at `BUILDER_COST_EMBLEM_CAP = 5` with a `+N` overflow and is `flex-wrap: nowrap`, so the pill is a fixed one line whatever the cost; cost 0 falls back to a `Free` label instead of the block renderer's "No energy cost" sentence. Two CSS collisions fixed on the way: `.energy-token` in `style.css` (loaded by `home.html` alongside `home.css`) carries an 18px box and a 2px border, so the pill's token needed `min-width`/`max-width` as well as `width` to actually shrink; and `.deck-builder-card-quickstats span` was a *descendant* selector, so it applied the pill's own `padding: 4px 9px` to each nested emblem — a 12px token measured 20px wide and cost 9 spilled out of the hero. Scoped to `> span`. Cache pins: `home.js` 144 -> 145 and `home.css` 132 -> 133 on `home.html`, `home.css` 132 -> 133 on `card-dashboard.html`.
- Verification: `node --check js/home.js` clean; no Java touched. Headless Chromium at 390x844 and 1920x1080 against a mock catalog covering cost 1, 4, 9, 0 and a 2-cost SPELL. Per card: emblem count equals the cost (1/4/2), cost 9 caps at 5 emblems plus `+4`, cost 0 renders the `Free` label with zero tokens; each token's computed background resolves to that element's notch art (`notch-fire`, `notch-ice`, `notch-shadow`, `notch-water`); every token measures 12px wide (20px before the two CSS fixes); the pill stays 23-24px tall — one line — at every cost including 9, and its rect sits inside `.deck-builder-card-hero` at both viewports (cost 9 escaped the hero on mobile before the cap). `.detail-cost-block` count in the tab panel is 0, confirming the duplicate block is gone. No page errors. Re-ran the earlier suites unchanged: scroll held at 400 across an add, selection held across a recommendation Add, Moves tab renders 2 rows, inline filter 40 -> 8 -> 40 with the tray closed, row thumbnails still resolve (`card-row-thumb-notch` loaded, no `Owned x` in the list), and the replicated `tests/mobile-overlap` assertions still pass at 390x844, 375x812, 360x800 and 320x568.

Original prompt: Shouldn't need to display owned cards in builder since you will only be able to build decks intuitively with owned cards in the card tab instead of showing the element icon show the singalong cutout overlay art. The element can be inferred by the tab color and the text there as well. Make sure we use the notch art for strategies and deceptions

- August 23, 2026 Follow-up on the deck builder rework. The builder only ever lists cards the player owns (`builderCatalogCards` filters on `builderCardLimit(card.id) > 0`), so the "Owned xN" tail on every binder row was noise — dropped, leaving the caption as type / element with the rarity + evolution line below. Row thumbnails no longer show the element badge: a new `renderCardRowThumb(card)` in `card-binder-visual.js` (exported alongside `notchIconPath`) returns the Siegling **cutout illustration** for `OVERLAY`/`REPLACE` art modes, and the element's **notch art** for SPELL/TRAP (Strategies and Deceptions have no creature art). `FULL_CARD` art is deliberately excluded — it is a pre-composited card face and would read as an unrecognisable crop at 36-42px. The element badge stays only as the fallback for a Siegling with no uploaded art, since element is already carried by the row's accent colour and its caption text. `home.js` routes all three builder marks (binder row, deck row, compact preview head) through a local `renderBuilderRowThumb` delegate that falls back to `renderElementIcon` if the visual bundle is absent. The new `.card-row-thumb-art` / `.card-row-thumb-notch` images are pinned `position:absolute; inset:0` inside the already-sized `.builder-card-mark` box, matching the contract `.element-icon-art` relies on — that comment in `home.css` records a past bug where an absolutely positioned thumbnail sized against the viewport and filled the whole screen behind the builder, so the new classes were given the same explicit sizing rather than inheriting nothing. Cutouts use `object-fit: cover` (transparent-background art, framed to fill); notch art uses `object-fit: contain` with 4px padding, since it is a complete badge and must not be cropped. Original prompt: Building decks feels clunky and not user friendly makes me not want to build them we neee a better efficient system. A few issues , the evolution recommendations swapping the selected card, need filter buttons native to the screen outside of the hud but they trigger the same page. When adding a card the list resets to the top needing multiple scrolls. Too much displayed could gate behind buttons and tabs like moves. The usage of space on card tabs has a lot of unused space. I want to create a simple system that allows players to build decks intuitively with recommendations and efficient use of space and screen space

- August 23, 2026 Reworked the `/deck-builder` page in `home.js`/`home.css` around the five reported problems. (1) **Selection theft**: `adjustBuilder` set `state.builderPreviewCardId = cardId` on every add, so adding a copy — including tapping Add on an evolution recommendation — swapped the inspected card and re-flowed the recommendation list under the player; it now only claims the preview when nothing is selected, and the recommendation row's body is no longer a `data-select-builder-card` button (Add keeps you on the card you are building around, an explicit **View** button switches). (2) **Scroll reset**: every +/- rebuilt `#deckBuilderPage.innerHTML`, discarding list scroll. Lists carry `data-scroll-key`; `captureBuilderScroll`/`restoreBuilderScroll` bracket the render and restore binder/deck/card offsets plus window scroll. (3) **Native filters**: a `.builder-filter-bar` renders All/Siegelings/Strategies/Deceptions type chips inline on the page with a **More filters** button that opens the same `builderFilterTray` HUD tray (one source of truth — `syncBuilderFilterTrayControls` mirrors inline changes back onto the tray's persistent controls) and a Reset chip showing the active-filter count. (4) **Too much on screen**: on phones the builder is now one pane at a time behind a Binder/Deck/Card tab strip (`state.builderTab`, `data-builder-tab-active` gating pane display), and the Card pane splits into Overview/Moves/Evolution sub-tabs (`state.builderCardTab`) instead of stacking art, flavor, stats, every move, every ability and the recommendations in one column. Deck name + SiegeKnight collapsed behind a toggle. Desktop keeps all three columns; the tab strip is `display:none` there. (5) **Card-pane space**: the preview became a hero row — thumbnail art beside name/type/HP/SPD/Cost/Owned pills and the add stepper — so the stepper is above the fold; the mobile rules that hid `.detail-cost-block`/`.detail-grid`/`.detail-abilities` outright were dropped since the sub-tabs now carry them. Binder/deck rows put the stepper back inline (single-line rows, ~5 cards visible instead of 2) and the lists grew from a 22vh/30vh sliver to 58vh/52vh now that one pane owns the viewport. Two layout bugs found and fixed during verification: builder grid/flex children needed an explicit `min-width: 0` (default `min-width:auto` sized the track to content and pushed the panes 484px wide inside a 340px column, scrolling the whole page sideways), and the nowrap chip groups shrank below their own chips and collided, fixed with `display: contents` on `.builder-filter-chips` so every chip is a direct item of the scrolling row. Cache pins: `home.js` 142 -> 143 on `home.html`, `home.css` 130 -> 131 on `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`.
- Verification: `node --check js/home.js` clean; no Java touched (no backend change — deck rules and the 30-card cap are untouched). Headless Chromium against the real page served by `python3 -m http.server`, with `/api/game/options` stubbed to a 40-card mock catalog including evolution lines, at 390x844 and 1920x1080, asserting behavior rather than load: (a) binder list scrolled to 400, added a copy from a row already on screen (so Playwright's own scroll-into-view can't be mistaken for the bug) — `scrollTop` 400 before and 400 after, deck total 1; (b) selected "Card 10", opened Evolution, clicked Add on its recommendation — inspected card still "Card 10" and the Evolution tab still active (previously the pane jumped to the added card); (c) Moves sub-tab renders 2 move rows with 0 `.detail-grid` in the panel, confirming the overview is gated away rather than stacked; (d) the inline Strategies chip filtered 40 rows -> 8 with `#builderFilterTray` still `is-closed`, and Reset restored 40; (e) mobile shows exactly one pane (`binder` block, card/deck `none`) with the tab strip `grid`, desktop shows all three with the strip `none`. No page errors at either viewport. Replicated the `tests/mobile-overlap` assertions against the builder itself (its own runner can't execute here — `@playwright/test` isn't installed locally, only the global `playwright`; the shared fixture references none of the classes touched here): at 390x844, 375x812, 360x800 and 320x568, across all three tabs and after an add, zero text/button overlaps and zero horizontal overflow, with `#deckBuilderPage` and the document both non-scrolling sideways.

Original prompt: Improve the drawing animation when you use. Knight draw skill, card draw skills should create the same popup. The spacing and sizing and way cars overlay. Allow the player a 1 second to see the cards then place them in the hand. I did not see a draw for the card ability

- August 20, 2026 The draw-ability reveal built its cards by cloning `.hand-card[data-hand-index]` nodes out of the live hand DOM, so it silently did nothing whenever those nodes weren't rendered — which is exactly the battle-phase/SiegeKnight case, where the hand tray is in queue mode: `showDrawAbilityReveal` bailed at `cardNodes.length === 0` and no popup appeared for creature battle abilities or knight actives. `game.js` now renders the reveal from the drawn card objects in the returned state via `renderShowcaseCard(card, {compactSummary, bodyMode:'summary'})`, so every draw source (spell cast, battle ability, SiegeKnight active) gets the identical popup regardless of what the hand tray is doing. Layout: `.draw-ability-reveal` became a row grid (copy above cards in normal flow instead of an absolutely positioned band over a fixed `min-height` card strip, which clipped the stat pills), and the cards now sit in a gapped flex row — no `margin-left: -18px` overlap — with width/gap driven by `--draw-reveal-card-width`/`--draw-reveal-gap` stepped down by a `data-count` attribute (4/5/6 cards) so a wide fan tucks in rather than running off a phone. Timing is explicit: entrance ~620ms (440ms transition + 180ms stagger), then a full 1000ms hold with the cards still and readable, then a 460ms flight into the hand/hand-counter. Original prompt: The chain projectiles should come from the original target not the attacker. Like a rebound off the targeted card

- August 17, 2026 The rebound the previous entry described was correct in code and still wrong on screen, because in real play chain playback never ran. `parseDamageFromLog` stripped only **one** trailing parenthetical (`(?:\s*\([^)]*\))?\.?$`), but `EffectService.dealAbilityDamage` appends a group per bonus before the HP group — `"Statik deals 4 damage to Sundile (weakness +1) (HP: 6)"`. The lazy target group therefore captured `"Sundile (weakness +1)"`, `damagedByAbility` matched nothing, `buildChainSteps` hit its `return null` bail-out, and the action fell through to the generic simultaneous barrage — every projectile fired from the attacker at once. Electric chains almost always carry a weakness bonus (Statik/Zzzap vs the Fire board in the report), so the chain path was effectively dead. Fixed by making the trailing group repeat (`?` -> `*`) in `parseDamageFromLog`, and the same in `parseSiegeBountyFromLog` which had the identical shape. No change to the hop logic itself. Cache pin: `action-queue.js` 44 -> 45 on `play.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean. Regex probe: `"Statik deals 6 damage to Pylord (weakness +1) (soak +1) (rust +2) (HP: 2)"` now yields target `Pylord` (was `Pylord (weakness +1) (soak +1) (rust +2)`), and unannotated lines are unaffected. End-to-end through the real `enqueueFromStateDiff` path on `chain-order-fixture.html` (headless Chromium, 390x844) with a Monkwatt Statik log carrying two weakness annotations: projectiles are now `player 1,1 -> enemy 0,0 @263ms`, `enemy 0,0 -> 0,1 @1566ms`, `enemy 0,0 -> 1,0 @2283ms` — one strike to the named primary, then rebounds originating from that card. The same fixture run against the stashed pre-fix file produces a single projectile `player 1,1 -> enemy 0,1`, i.e. the barrage from the attacker that was reported. New guard `damageLogParserStripsEveryTrailingAnnotationFromTheTargetName` in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` pins the repeated group and pins the EffectService wording it depends on; suite 69/69 green, `EffectServiceTest` 32/32.

Original prompt: The battle queue pushes the arenas into each other, chain attacks are hitting connected targets but the order should be hitting the initial target then a electric shock hitting the secondary targets in sequence

- August 17, 2026 Two battle-table presentation fixes. (1) Chain-attack ordering in `action-queue.js`: `playChainAttack` already struck the primary before the arcs, but it played the whole link set as **one hop** — every connected Siegling got its projectile, border and impact on the same frame, so the effect read as a simultaneous splash rather than a shock travelling the links. The hop loop now fires the primary alone and then walks `step.links` one at a time (`await playHop(step.primary, [link], ARC_SPEED_SCALE)`), and `playHop` takes a speed scale so the arcs run at 55% of the opening strike's projectile/impact timings (floored at 90ms/70ms) — without that a 3-link chain would have tripled its playback length. Backend order was already correct (`EffectService.applyChainDamage` resolves the full victim list first, then damages primary-then-links), so this is presentation only. (2) Battle queue squeezing the arena: under `body.battle-inline-dock` on <=900px the queue dock was capped at `min(42vh, 300px)`, and a Siegling with five abilities (New Ton) filled it, leaving the battlefield too short for both 3x3 halves — the two boards ran into each other with the bottom row clipped. `.desktop-hand-battle-panel` is now capped at `min(34vh, 260px)` with `overscroll-behavior:contain`; the ability list scrolls inside the dock, which it was already set up to do (`overflow-y:auto`). Cache pins: `style.css` 231 -> 232 (`play.html`, `home.html`, `card-dashboard.html`), `action-queue.js` 43 -> 44 (`play.html`).
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/action-queue.js` clean. New harness `static/chain-order-fixture.html` builds both 3x3 grids, stubs `SieglingsFx.attackCell` to record `(from,to,timestamp)`, and enqueues a real ATTACK action carrying `chainSteps` with one primary and three links. Headless Chromium at 390x844 reports the arcs strictly sequential off the primary: `player 1,1 -> enemy 0,0 @382ms`, then `enemy 0,0 -> 0,1 @1685ms`, `-> 1,0 @2402ms`, `-> 1,1 @3119ms` (~717ms apart); before the change all three arc calls shared a single timestamp. CSS cap confirmed by computed style on the same fixture with `body.battle-inline-dock`: 390x844 gives `max-height 260px, overflow-y auto` (was 300px, so 40px returns to the arena), while 1920x1080 is outside the media query and is unchanged at 496.8px.

Original prompt: I standardized card size but now text is falling off the card the standard size should be large enough and more rectangular so all text can fit. Also holding a card should make it overlay on top of the others. Similar to the action when you tap the card

- August 17, 2026 Siege hand cards were standardized to a near-square box (94x98 in phone landscape, 128x152 base, 138x170 on wide screens), and at that size long cards ran their text off the bottom edge: `.playcard` keeps `overflow:visible` for the hung AP badge, so a name like "Ser Airek: Aero Sphere" wrapping to three lines pushed the effect line **past the card and onto the board** rather than being clipped. Measured on the failing tree at 995x460 (the reporter's viewport): the effect row ended **6px** below the card on that card and **21px** below on "Shellpack: Tidal Crush Barrage". Two fixes in `adventure.css`: every card box became a proper portrait rectangle with room to spare — base 140x196, portrait phone 112x152, phone landscape 100x128, tablet/desktop `clamp(116px,10.5vw,152px)` x `clamp(166px,25vh,216px)` — with `--arena-hand-h` raised to match (170px phone landscape, `clamp(212px,32vh,300px)` above 600px tall) and `.hand-fan` min-heights raised so the fan arc still is not clipped; and the fixed rows are now line-clamped (`.pc-name` 3 lines, `.pc-eff` 3 lines, `.pc-owner` single-line ellipsis) so no amount of text can push a row out of the box, leaving `.pc-desc` as the one elastic row it always was. Second ask, held cards: the fan overlaps cards by a negative margin, and pressing a card did nothing visually until the drag threshold was crossed, so the card under your finger stayed half-hidden behind its neighbour. `setupCardDrag()` in `adventure.js` now adds a `playcard-pressed` class on `pointerdown` (removed in `cleanup()`), and the new `.playcard.playcard-pressed` rule gives the same lift/scale a tapped `.selected` card gets plus `z-index:8` so the held card sits above the whole fan. Cache pins bumped on `adventure.html`: after merging `origin/main` (PR #716, which had taken `adventure.css` to 64) the pins were set above both branches at `adventure.css` 65 and `adventure.js` 68.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/adventure.js` clean; `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` + `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest` green (the CSS pin floor assertion is `>= 56`, satisfied by the pin). New guard `tests/siege-card-size-check.cjs` renders the worst realistic hand (long owner:ability names, wrapping effect lines, an evolution gauge card) against the real stylesheet at four viewports and asserts no row ends below its card's content box, a card ratio of at least 1.2, and that a `playcard-pressed` card carries a lift transform with `z-index >= 6`. Run before the change it reported the 6px/21px spills above; after: `phone-portrait 112x152 ratio=1.36 worstOverflow=-59px heldZ=8`, `phone-landscape 100x128 ratio=1.28 worstOverflow=-22px`, `tablet-landscape 116x192 ratio=1.66 worstOverflow=0px`, `desktop 152x216 ratio=1.42 worstOverflow=0px` — all pass (0px = the elastic description exactly filling the remaining room, which is the intended behavior).
Original prompt: Yeah the expedition type can be displayed at the top instead of the bottom / When rotating the map portrait and landscape it sometimes adjust the view and leaves blank space on the bottom and you can't see the top it's also messy fix that

- August 17, 2026 Two follow-ups on the Siege map HUD. (1) The expedition-type badge moved out of the resource row and into the top bar: `#mapMode` is now a sibling of `.siege-title` in `adventure.html`, shown only on the map screen (`body:not([data-screen="mapScreen"]) .siege-topbar #mapMode{display:none}`) and styled by id/position, since `renderMap()` rewrites its `className` each render. On phones the two-line `.siege-title` gives way on this screen — the badge already names the run — and the badge itself shrinks with an ellipsis at `60vw`. With the badge gone, gold + deck/save + Items now share one HUD line and the hint takes the second. (2) Rotation stranded the view: `onMapOrientationFlip` only re-rendered when the phone-landscape axis actually flipped, so a rotation on the same axis kept the old `scrollTop` against new scroll extents — blank space below the map with its top out of reach — and a map shorter than its scroll box sat pinned to the top edge with all the slack dumped underneath. `renderMap()` now stores its focus scroll as `mapFocusScroll` (a closure over the baked geometry) instead of running it once, the flip handler calls it whenever the axis is unchanged, and `.map-scroll` became `display:flex` with `#mapSvg{margin:auto}` so a small map centres on both axes — auto margins rather than `align-items:center` precisely because they never make an overflowing start edge unreachable. Cache pins: `adventure.css` 63 -> 64, `adventure.js` 66 -> 67.

All five viewports pass; e.g. 390x844 -> 844x390 lands `current=true` with gaps `T1/B-33`, back to 390x844 `scroll=16,75 current=true`, and desktop 1920x1080 centres at `T14/B14 L344/R344`. `siege-mode-saves-check.cjs` passes with the HUD order now `mapGold,mapDeckCount,inventoryBtn,extractBtn,mapHint` in both modes (its badge assertions read `#mapMode` by id and still hold), as do `siege-merc-at-stops-check.cjs`, `siege-merc-party-layout-check.cjs` and `siege-effect-timing-check.cjs`. `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` + `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest` green.
