Original prompt: Review the sequence of animations and effects in siege and battlegrounds. Health should reduce after projectiles strike. Heals after effects burns after effects etc check the clocks and timing for everything. Also when a merc is hired it should display at stops with the rest of the team until it leaves the team

- August 16, 2026 Siege's effect ordering was inverted at the root: **every HP bar reached its final value ~1.5s before the projectile that caused it even launched**. `adventure.js#applyRun` assigned the fresh post-turn server state and called `renderRun()` *first*, then handed the turn's events to `playEvents` — so the damage, heals and burn ticks were already painted before playback started, and the projectiles/auras/floats were decoration replayed over a settled board. Measured on the pre-fix bundle: HP settled at **6ms** while the first orb launched at 1514ms and the first burn aura at 2231ms. Fixed on both sides. Server: `SiegeBattle#event` now stamps every presentation event with a `vitals` array — the `hp`/`maxHp`/`shield`/`alive` of the combatants it names, as of the moment it was emitted. It reads them off `combatants` for the `targetId`/`aId`/`bId` keys only, deliberately **not** `sourceId`, because an attacker's own HP moves on its own event (leech, recoil) and stamping it into the hit would leak that change early. Client: `state.vitals` holds the pre-turn numbers for the duration of playback (captured in `applyRun` before `state.run` is replaced, cleared in the playback callback), `renderSpriteLine` and `renderKnightPlate` read through the new `heldVitals()`, and each event steps its own targets forward with `commitVitals` at the moment its effect lands — inside `fireProjectile`'s arrival callback for `hit`, +200ms for burn/poison/wither (after the element border catches), +180ms for heal/shield/knightHit, +380ms for `evolve` (which rewrites max HP, so the new bar belongs to the new form). `repaintVitals` patches the bar, the HP text and the shield chip in place rather than calling `renderBattle()`, which would rebuild the sprite DOM and orphan the very projectile the number is supposed to be following; the KO pose still trails the hurt flash by 460ms. A second, compounding timing bug was in `playEvents` itself: it compressed long sequences with `scale = 10 / events.length` floored at a flat 60ms, so a busy round cut a `hit` to well under the **340ms its projectile spends crossing the stage** — the next event and its HP change landed while the orb was still in flight. Compression is now clamped at half speed and each event carries a per-type floor (`EVENT_MIN_MS`) equal to the time its own animation actually needs. **Battlegrounds was reviewed and deliberately left unchanged**: `action-queue.js` already implements this exact discipline through `registerPendingHealth`/`registerPendingHeal`/`registerPendingLethalHold`, and every playback path releases the hold *after* its impact — `playStandardAttackImpact` and `playLethalAttackImpact` release after `sleep(t.impactMs)` following the projectile, `HEAL` releases only once the cross and `+N` are on screen, `AFFLICTION` waits `auraMs * 0.4` for the border to catch, and the direct-HP-bar path releases after the bar flash. Separately, a hired mercenary now travels with the warband at the stops. A rental lives outside `run.party` server-side (that list drives equipping, evolving and smith scrapping, none of which a merc is eligible for), and the stop renderers read `run.party` directly — so a merc vanished from the team everywhere except the battlefield until its contract ended. `SiegeService#serialize` publishes it as `run.mercenary` with its boon cards, kept out of `party` on purpose, and adventure.js appends it for **display only** via the new `displayParty(run)`, now used by the map HUD strip, the resume card and all six illustrated stops (camp, cache, broker, smith, caravan, event). It is badged rather than levelled — `partyLevelBadge`/`partyXpBar` are suppressed and a gold `Merc` chip takes their place, matching the treatment the battlefield plate already gives the `(Merc)` name suffix — and the detail modal reads "Mercenary - leaves after the next battle". Cache pins: `adventure.js` 64 -> 65 and `adventure.css` 58 -> 59 on `adventure.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `adventure.js`, `action-queue.js` and both new harnesses; full `./mvnw test` **529/529** green. The ordering fix is guarded by the new `tests/siege-effect-timing-check.cjs`, which drives the **real** code end to end — adventure.js is a closed IIFE, so it stubs `fetch`, lets `boot()` raise its own resume prompt, clicks Continue into the battle screen and clicks End Turn, then samples every unit's rendered HP text once per animation frame. **It reproduces the reported bug against the pre-fix behaviour** (with the hold disabled, all five units read final HP at 6ms, ~1.5s before the first projectile) and passes after, at 390x844 and 1920x1080 alike: first projectile 1517ms -> e1 HP drops 1868ms (the 340ms flight); burn aura 2240ms -> HP 2434ms; heal float 2834ms; enemy hit 4134ms; knight backlash 4683ms — each change after its cause, and in event order. The merc work is guarded by `tests/siege-merc-at-stops-check.cjs` across the same two viewports: the map strip carries knight + 2 warband + merc with a `Merc` badge and no raw `(Merc)` suffix, the rest camp stands three figures with the merc badged and fully in view, and with the contract gone the stop drops back to two figures with no merc. It also records a baseline that the map strip **already** scrolls horizontally at a full 3-strong warband with no merc (622/370px at 390x844), so the merc chip needing a scroll on a phone is the strip's existing behaviour, not a new clip. No regressions in the sibling siege guards: `siege-merc-party-layout-check.cjs` and `siege-mode-saves-check.cjs` both pass, and `tests/battle-timing/presentation-gate-check.cjs` was run against a live `spring-boot:run` and reports PASS on all six assertions at both viewports, confirming battlegrounds playback is untouched. (`siege-sprite-scale-check.py` could not run - no `PIL` in this environment, a pre-existing gap unrelated to these changes.)

Original prompt: Review the leaderboard issue again

- August 13, 2026 The hub's leaderboard panel was showing "Leaderboards are unavailable right now." with a Retry button on a load where **nothing had failed**. The backend was healthy the whole time — live `GET /api/leaderboards` answered 200 in 3.4s with populated `weekly`/`monthly`/`year`/`allTime` boards (daily legitimately empty) — and the deployed `home.js?v=140` matched the repo, so the break was the panel's own state machine. `init()` calls `renderHomeDashboard()` before `loadAll()` has requested anything, and `state.leaderboards` had no initialized value: `leaderboardListMarkup` treats a falsy payload as a failed fetch, so *"not asked yet"* rendered as the failure banner. On a Cloud Run cold start `loadAll()` awaits `Promise.all` with `/api/game/options` in it (20-30s, expected), so the false banner stayed on screen for the whole wait — and if any sibling promise rejected, `applyLeaderboardsPayload` never ran at all and the banner was permanent. This is the successor to the August 10 fix: that one stopped a `{ error }` payload rendering as an empty board, this one stops an *unset* payload rendering as an error. Three changes in `home.js`: `leaderboardsLoading` joins state (true until a fetch resolves) and drives the existing "Loading leaderboards…" emblem; `hydrateStaticCachesFromStorage` now seeds the board from the `leaderboards` cache within its 60s TTL, so a returning visit paints real rows on the first frame; and the leaderboards fetch settles on its own promise inside `loadAll`, applying itself and re-rendering the dashboard the moment it lands, so neither a slow nor a rejected sibling can strand or skip it. Two server-side hardenings went with it. `LeaderboardController` no longer lets a cold-instance failure escape as a bare 500 — when `getSnapshot()` throws with no snapshot to fall back on it answers **503** `{ error: "Leaderboards are warming up. Try again in a moment.", warming: true }`, which is the text the panel puts next to its Retry button instead of Spring's "Internal Server Error". And `warmOnStartup` now retries on a daemon thread at 0s/5s/20s/60s instead of swallowing one attempt: `MatchHistoryStore.findAll` scans every match doc under a 15s op timeout, which a cold instance racing Firestore auth can blow, and a single swallowed failure left the instance with no snapshot at all, so *every* read threw until one happened to succeed. Boot is never blocked. Cache pin: `home.js` 140 -> 141 on `home.html` (the only page that includes it); the four `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` guards that assert that pin literally move with it.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `home.js`; full `./mvnw test` **519/519** green, five of them new. Two `LeaderboardServiceTest` cases drive the real objects — a cold controller over a failing store returns 503 with `warming: true` rather than throwing, and `warmOnStartup` against a store that fails once then recovers builds its snapshot from the retry. A `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` guard pins the three client halves (the `leaderboardsLoading` state, the loading branch, the independent promise and the cache hydration). Behaviour was measured in headless Chromium against the real `home.html` at **390x844 and 1920x1080**, with `/api/game/options` stalled 9s to simulate the cold start and `/api/leaderboards` stalled 4s and answered with the payload captured from production: at 600ms the panel reads `Loading leaderboards…` with the pill on `Daily`, and once settled it reads the board with the pill on `Updated Aug 13, 7:34 PM` — one leaderboards call, zero page errors, identical at both viewports. The before-picture was captured by routing `/js/home.js` to `git show HEAD:` and replaying the identical timing: `{list: "Leaderboards are unavailable right now.\nRetry", pill: "Daily"}` at 600ms — the reported screenshot, reproduced exactly. The genuine failure path still works: a 503 renders the warming text plus Retry, and clicking Retry refetches (2 calls) and lands the board.
- August 13, 2026 (follow-up) Finished the leaderboard waiting state so a cold start never ends at a button the player has to press. The panel's wait now renders the hub's shared `panelLoadingMarkup` — spinner, label, sliding bar, `role="status" aria-live="polite"` — instead of a bare text emblem, with a new `.panel-loading.compact` modifier (20px padding, 26px spinner) because the browser-panel sizing made the loading panel taller than the panel is with rows in it. More importantly, `loadLeaderboardsWithRetry` retries the fetch on the player's behalf at 2.5s and 6s before the failed state is ever shown, keeping the panel in its loading state across all three attempts and caching the first good payload. That is exactly what tapping Retry did, and a warming Cloud Run instance recovers well inside that window, so the error banner is now the last resort for a genuinely down backend rather than the normal cold-start experience. Cache pins: `home.js` 141 -> 142 on `home.html`; `home.css` 129 -> 130 on `home.html` **and** `card-dashboard.html`, which also includes it.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `home.js`; full `./mvnw test` **519/519** green. The static guard grew to pin the retry ladder, the self-retrying loader, and the compact spinner markup/CSS together. Measured in headless Chromium at **390x844 and 1920x1080** against the real `home.html`, with `/api/game/options` stalled 9s to hold the cold start open and `/api/leaderboards` answering the production payload after N warming 503s. One 503 (the cold-instance case): the panel shows the compact spinner at 1.2s and has the board by 4.2s with **2 calls and no click** — the player never sees an error. Nine 503s (backend genuinely down): spinner at 1.2s and 4.2s, then after the third attempt the warming text plus Retry, so the fallback still exists. Panel height holds steady across the transition (107px loading vs 104px with rows, against 87px for the error state), and both viewports report zero page errors.
- August 14, 2026 Shipped the leaderboard loading-state fix to production. PR #708 was fast-forwarded onto `main` as `ec19f944` (no merge commit — `main` had not moved since `e7da730c`), starting Deploy run [31764517568](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/31764517568) at 02:41:59Z. Both jobs green: **Cloud Run Deploy** 02:42:01Z-02:47:02Z, **Firebase Deploy** 02:47:05Z onward with Hosting released at 02:48:16Z and Functions at 02:49:22Z. Hosting was polled every 45s and flipped to the new pins at **02:48:20Z** — `/home` now serves `home.js?v=142` and `home.css?v=130`. Health checks: `/api/cards/editor` reports `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true`; `/api/leaderboards` answers **200 in 0.74s** with a snapshot generated at 02:46:54Z (`allTime`/`year` wins Roc 19, lawz67 2, Tron Dreamer 2; `weekly`/`monthly` Roc 1; `daily` legitimately empty). The deployed bundles were grepped, not assumed: live `home.js?v=142` carries `leaderboardsLoading: true`, `LEADERBOARD_RETRY_DELAYS_MS`, `loadLeaderboardsWithRetry()` and the compact `panelLoadingMarkup` call, and live `home.css?v=130` carries `.panel-loading.compact`.
- Verification: the **deployed** assets were driven in a browser, not just pin-checked. Chromium still cannot egress to the live host through this environment's proxy, so live `home.js?v=142` and `home.css?v=130` were fetched by curl and served over an otherwise complete local page, with `/api/game/options` stalled 9s to hold a cold start open and `/api/leaderboards` answering the payload production returned at that moment. At **390x844** with one warming 503 first: the compact spinner shows at 1.2s and the board lands by 4.7s in **2 calls with no click** — the cold-start path a player actually hits. At **1920x1080** with an immediate 200: the board paints straight away, no spinner flash, 1 call. On both, the All Time tab reads `#1 Roc 19 / #2 lawz67 2 / #3 Tron Dreamer 2` with the pill on `Updated Aug 14, 2:46 AM`, and zero page errors.
Original prompt: Implement these updates

- August 13, 2026 Implemented the battle-timing fix from `docs/battle-timing-review.md`: the acting Siegeling's move buttons no longer appear before the Battle Phase banner that introduces them. Root cause was structural — `api()` in `game.js` assigns `gameState`, *enqueues* the animation diff without awaiting it, then calls `render()` synchronously, so the battle dock painted from the new BATTLE state while the `PHASE` action was still queued. **Step 1** gives `ActionQueue` a single presentation gate: all three writes to `this.processing` now route through `_setProcessing`, which toggles a `sgl-playback-active` body class and settles `onIdle()` waiters, plus `isPhaseBannerActive()` (backed by a new `window.isPhaseTransitionBannerActive` in `game.js`) and the combined `isPresentationBusy()`. **Step 2** holds the battle dock in its existing "Resolving" standby shell while that gate is set, and re-renders it from `_drain()`'s `finally` so the moves surface exactly when playback ends. The hold is scoped to the drawer and hand docks on purpose: the left inspector rail is only rewritten further down when the landscape dock is in use, so writing standby into it stranded "Queue is resolving" on that rail after playback — caught by a per-panel probe and fixed by giving the busy case its own early-return branch. The board, HP bars and hand deliberately keep updating live, because the queue's pending-placement/health layer is built around `render()` continuing to run. **Step 3** makes the banner an actual beat rather than decoration: `.phase-transition-banner.visible` gains `pointer-events: auto` and a new `#phaseTransitionScrim` (z-index 859, under the banner's 860) blocks taps on the board and dock for the hold, wired through `showPhaseTransitionBanner`/`hidePhaseTransitionBanner` and the new-game reset. **Step 5** routes the turn toast through the queue — `maybeNotifyTurnChange` now awaits `onIdle()` and is called *after* `enqueueFromStateDiff` (asking for idle before the actions exist would resolve immediately), with a `gameState !== nextState` guard so a stale turn is never announced. **Step 6** replaces `beginSoloAiEndTurn`'s 15s `while (this.processing) sleep(50)` spin-wait with `await this.onIdle()` and drops the ad-hoc `hidePhaseTransitionBanner()` beside it, since the `PHASE` action awaits its own banner and an idle queue already means the banner finished. **Step 4 of the plan was withdrawn, not implemented**: it proposed forcing PHASE-first ordering, but a closer read of `action-queue.js:2302-2311` shows the per-path ordering is deliberate and temporally correct — the `setupToBattle` placements belong to the SETUP phase that just ended, so replaying them before the BATTLE announcement is right, and enqueuing PHASE first would have made it worse; the `phaseTransitionQueued` latch is load-bearing rather than a smell. The review doc was updated to record that withdrawal. Two exact-literal cache pins in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` (`style.css?v=230`, `action-queue.js?v=42`) were converted to `>=` floors via a shared `assetPin` helper, extending the same fix the repo had already applied to `game.js` — a literal pin turns every unrelated asset edit into a red test. Cache pins: `game.js` 244 -> 245 and `style.css` 230 -> 231 on `play.html`, `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`; `action-queue.js` 42 -> 43 on `play.html` and `border-fx-fixture.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `game.js`, `action-queue.js` and the new harness; full `./mvnw test` run with the dev server stopped (an earlier run overlapped `spring-boot:run` and produced spurious "cannot find symbol" compile errors from concurrent `target/` writes — not test failures). The behavioural check is `tests/battle-timing/presentation-gate-check.cjs`, kept in-repo: it feeds a **real server-captured BATTLE state carrying `pendingBattle`** through the page's own `api()`/`render()` and the real `ActionQueue`, landing it three times — queue idle, a real `PHASE` action mid-flight, and after drain. **Against the pre-fix bundle it reproduces the reported bug at both 390x844 and 1920x1080**: during playback `{abilityButtons: 3, dockState: "Acting Now", bannerUp: true, queueBusy: true}` — the screenshot exactly. After the fix, both viewports report `{abilityButtons: 0, dockState: "Resolving"}` during playback and `{abilityButtons: 3, dockState: "Acting Now"}` once it drains, six of six assertions green. A separate per-panel probe confirms all three action panels cycle `Acting Now -> Resolving -> Acting Now` while `desktopInspectBattlePanel` is left untouched (`state=null` throughout). Worth recording for whoever revisits this: a naive "play a real match and time the events" harness was built first and **passes even on the broken build**, because the round-1 `SETUP -> BATTLE` endturn response carries no `pendingBattle` — the dock has nothing to paint until auto-advance fires `/battle`, and `shouldAutoAdvanceBattle` already gates on `isProcessing()`. The defect needs a single response carrying the phase flip *and* the next acting Siegeling, which is the solo-AI end-turn path; that is why the probe lands such a state directly instead of hoping the RNG produces one.

Original prompt: Timing is off, ability options appear before the battle Phase motivation pops up, doa. Full review of game state sequences and provide a report and update plan to make sure everything works in order

- August 13, 2026 Review only, no behavioural change: `docs/battle-timing-review.md` documents why the Battle Phase banner arrives after the UI it is meant to introduce. Root cause is structural rather than a tuning miss — `api()` in `game.js` assigns `gameState`, *enqueues* the animation diff without awaiting it, and calls `render()` synchronously, so the battle dock, the ACTING NOW header and the ability buttons paint from the new BATTLE state while the `PHASE` action is still queued behind every placement action (each carrying up to 1000ms of `minActionMs`/`gapAfterMs`). The banner is also `pointer-events: none` with no scrim, so it never gates input, and `shouldAutoAdvanceBattle` is the only consumer of `ActionQueue.isProcessing()` — the game holds its own turn-taking for playback but lets the player act into it. Five further ordering defects are catalogued with file/line references, including the turn toast jumping the queue and the phase banner landing in a different position in the `setupToBattle` and `soloAiEndFlow` paths. The plan is six steps: a shared `presentationBusy` gate on the queue, gating only the battle dock (not the board or HP bars, which the queue's pending-state layer needs `render()` to keep driving), making the banner block input, deterministic PHASE-first ordering, routing the turn toast through the queue, and then retiring the three existing workarounds that each patch this same root cause.
- Verification: documentation only — no JS, Java or CSS touched, so no cache-bust bump, no `node --check` and no test run applies. Every code reference in the report was read directly from the current bundle at `e7da730` rather than recalled. The report specifies the assertion that would actually catch the bug for whoever implements it: a headless-Chromium timeline at 390x844 and 1920x1080 recording when `phaseTransitionBanner` gains `.visible` versus when the first ability button renders and becomes enabled, asserting the banner both precedes and clears before it.

Original prompt: 2 things, one my fire damage did not burn on the opponents turn. It should have burned 3 stacks and killed them. Two, when the ai or other person turn is active disable knight button, draw button and make sure cards cannot be handled by the other player. Electric attacks should still use particle projectiles then the chain effect that bounces from the target card etc right now they are only doing border effects. Keep those and add projectiles

- August 12, 2026 Three Battle Table fixes: Burn never ticking on AI-owned Sieglings, live controls during the opponent's turn, and electric attacks losing their projectiles. (1) Setup-tick afflictions (Burn, Wither, Chill thaw) were only fired from `GameService.draw`, and the solo AI does not go through it — `AIService.executeAITurn` writes its own draw/setup sequence. Burn stacked onto an enemy Siegling therefore sat on the card forever: it never damaged, never cleared, and a card the player had set alight three times survived at 2 HP. `executeAITurn` now calls `elementalAfflictionService.tickOwnerSetup(state, false)` in the same position `draw` uses — after the phase flips to SETUP and *before* `recalculateEnergy`, so a Siegling that burns to death stops feeding links in the same pass; `finishSetupTurn` already re-checks the win condition, so a lethal burn bounty ends the match. (2) The Knight button, the Draw button and card handling stayed live while the opponent (AI or the other player) held initiative. The server rejected those actions, so nothing illegal landed, but a control that looks armed and silently no-ops reads as a bug. New `isOpponentControlLocked()` in `game.js` is the single predicate — during BATTLE it keys off `battleWaitingOn === 'ENEMY'`, otherwise off `activeSide`, and MULLIGAN is deliberately exempt — and it now gates `playerDraw`, `placeCard`, `activateActionCard`, `openTrainerAbilityPopup`, the Knight button's disabled/ready state and title, the Draw button's disabled state, and `getSelectedLegalPlacements`, which is what kills both the click-to-place highlights and the drag-drop landing zones in one place. (3) Electric attacks played as border-only with no projectile. `resolveAttackerFromLogs` recovers the attacking cell by matching the damage line's ability name against a `"<Card> uses <Ability>."` line, and any failure drops the whole attack into the sourceless playback branch reserved for traps and auras — for a chain ability that also splits the ricochet into unrelated single hits. Two gaps closed: `parseAbilityFromLog` stripped only a trailing `.`, so an exclamation-terminated line yielded the ability name with a `!` glued on and never matched; and `findCellByAbilityName` never looked at abilities at all — it searched for the *card's* name inside the ability string. Board cells already carry their serialized `abilities` list (`GameController`), so it now matches ability names outright before falling back to the old substring scan. Spells and traps still resolve to nothing, because they are never on the board, so their borders are untouched. Chain hops additionally light the element on each victim's border (`variant: 'chain'`, no new CSS — variants are styling hooks) so the effect reads as projectile plus conducting card, which is what was asked for. Cache pins: `action-queue.js` 41 -> 42 on `play.html` and `border-fx-fixture.html`; `game.js` 242 -> 243 on `play.html`, `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`. `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` asserts those pins literally, so its expectations move with them — note that two of its pin assertions (`game.js?v=240`) were already failing on `main` before this change, because the preceding hand-placement fix bumped the bundle to 242 without moving the guard; both are repaired here.
- Merge note: `main` moved under this branch (#705, the evolution-card hand freeze) before it landed, and both branches shipped a `game.js?v=243` with *different* contents — a returning browser that had already fetched #705's 243 would never pull the merged bundle, so the pin moves to **244** on `play.html`, `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`. The `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` conflict resolved in favour of #705's `gameJsPin(markup) >= 240` helper over the literal pins this change had introduced: the helper removes this whole class of conflict, which is exactly what left those two assertions stale in the first place. `game.js` itself auto-merged, keeping #705's `getHandCardLockReason` try/catch wrapper around the renamed `computeHandCardLockReason` alongside the new turn lock.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `game.js` and `action-queue.js`; full `./mvnw test` green. New `ElementalAfflictionServiceTest#aiTurnTicksBurnOnItsOwnSieglings` drives a real `AIService.executeAITurn` over an enemy Siegling left at 2 HP with Burn x3 and asserts it dies, its stacks clear, and its bounty lands on the AI's own health — it fails on the pre-fix `AIService`. FX behavior was measured, not inspected: the real `ActionQueue` was driven through `border-fx-fixture.html` in headless Chromium at **390x844 and 1920x1080** with `SieglingsFx` stubbed to count `attackCell` calls and a `MutationObserver` counting the transient border overlays. Against the pre-fix bundle the three chain cases split: `uses …` with a period → 2 projectiles, but `uses …!` and a batch with no uses-line at all → **0 projectiles and 2 sourceless borders each** — the reported symptom, reproduced. After the fix all three play identically: **2 projectiles** (attacker→primary `E00→P11`, then primary→link `P11→P12`, both ELECTRIC) plus 2 `sgl-element-border-chain` borders. Regression cases unchanged at both viewports: single-target electric 1 projectile, plain fire attack 1 projectile, sourceless trap damage still **0 projectiles / 1 `sgl-element-border-effect`**. Zero page errors. The turn lock was exercised on the real `play.html` at both viewports with `api()` stubbed to record calls: on your own SETUP the Knight button is enabled with its READY badge, the popup opens, and forcing `playerDraw` + `placeCard` emits `draw` and `place`; during the opponent's SETUP and during BATTLE waiting on ENEMY the Knight button is `disabled` with no READY badge, the popup refuses to open, Draw is `disabled`, and the same forced calls emit **no API traffic at all**.

Original prompt: Had another issue when placing a card down to evolve, the evolution card did not get removed from my hand

- August 12, 2026 Fixed the hand freezing whenever an evolution card sat in hand with its base form already on the board — the state where a card the player had just played stayed on screen. `getEvolutionBaseCells` returns board **cells**, but `getHandCardLockReason` destructured them as `[row, col]` pairs (`baseCells.some(([r, c]) => cellHasAffliction(playerBoard[r][c], 'CURSE'))`) to word the "base is Cursed" message. Destructuring a non-iterable object throws, so the moment an evolution card's base was on the board and the evolution was not yet legal — the base had not finished a battle phase, the common case the turn you play a precursor — the lock check raised `TypeError: object is not iterable`. That throw escaped `renderHand`, which builds the whole hand as one string and assigns `container.innerHTML` only at the end, so the assignment never happened and **the hand stayed frozen on its previous markup**, still showing the card that had just been played. It escaped `render()` too, so `placeCard`'s `await api(...)` rethrew and the interaction state was never reset. The cursed check now reads the cell it was handed. `getHandCardLockReason` was also split into a thin wrapper over `computeHandCardLockReason` that catches and treats the card as playable, so no future throw in one card's lock check can strand the entire hand on stale markup again. The server was ruled out first: a scratch JUnit test drove `GameService.placeSiegling` through a real evolution and confirmed `removeFromHand` empties the hand, including when the player holds two copies of the same evolution card (exactly one leaves). Two pre-existing cache-pin assertions in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` pinned `game.js?v=240` exactly and had been red on `main` since the 241 bump; they now assert a floor via a `gameJsPin` helper, since the pin only ever moves forward. Cache pin: `game.js` 242 -> 243 on `play.html`, `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean; `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` green at 65 tests (was 64 with 2 failures, both pre-existing and now fixed). New `evolutionLockCheckReadsBaseCellsAsCellsAndCannotFreezeTheHand` pins all three halves — that `getEvolutionBaseCells` still yields cells, that the caller no longer destructures them as pairs, and that the lock check stays wrapped. Behaviour proved in headless Chromium at 390x844 against a real match from the running Spring Boot app, with an evolution card in hand whose base form on the board has `battlePhasesSeen: 0`: before the fix `getHandCardLockReason` returned `TypeError: object is not iterable`, `render()` rethrew it, and a sentinel written into `#playerHand` survived the render (hand frozen); after, the lock returns "Basemon must complete a full battle phase in its current form before it can evolve", `render()` completes, the sentinel is gone, and the hand DOM matches the state hand exactly with that copy shown on the locked card. A 60-turn driver looking for the same precondition to arise naturally was also run four times against the live rules engine — it never dealt that hand/board combination with the default preset deck, so the injected-state check above is what covers it, and those runs finished with zero page errors.

Original prompt: When a card is used it sometimes does not disappear from the hand it bugs out and gets stuck while also being placed

- August 12, 2026 Fixed the Battle Table hand-placement race where a Siegeling stayed visible (and re-grabbable) in the hand while already sitting on the board. Three defects in `static/js/game.js` fed the same symptom. (1) `placeCard` had no in-flight guard and no optimistic hand update, so between the drop and the server's reply — 200ms on a desk, seconds on phone data — the card kept rendering in the hand as a live, drag-placeable slot. A second drop in that window fired a duplicate `place` POST; whichever request the server happened to process first won, so the card the player dropped *second* could be the one that landed while the first bounced back into the hand with an error toast. `placeCard` now refuses to start while `placementRequestInFlight`, hides the committed slot through `pendingHandRemovalIndex` (tracked by hand index, not card id — hand cards carry definition ids, so three copies of a Siegling share one id and hiding by id would blank all three), and clears both plus the selection in a `finally`, so a rejected placement restores the card instead of wedging the UI. `canHandCardDragPlace` refuses the hidden slot too, and `api()` drops the optimistic hide the moment authoritative state arrives. (2) `pointercancel` was wired to the same handler as `pointerup`, so a gesture the browser took over — which iOS Safari does readily around a scrolling hand fan — was treated as a drop and placed the card at the last known coordinates, with no release from the player. Cancel now aborts the drag through its own `handleCardDragPointerCancel`. (3) `activateCardDragSession` selects the dragged card, and that selection re-renders the hand and destroys the very element `pointerdown` captured; WebKit answers the implicit capture release on a removed node with `pointercancel`, which — via defect 2 — killed or mis-fired the drag it had just started. The capture is now released explicitly before that render (the drag tracks pointer events on `document` and never needed capture), and `cleanupCardDragSession` sweeps any stray `.card-drag-ghost` so an interrupted gesture cannot leave a card floating over the hand. The drop path also tears the drag down before submitting, so the ghost is gone before `placeCard` re-renders. Cache pin: `game.js` 241 -> 242 on `play.html`, `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean; no Java or CSS touched, so no service tests and no `tests/mobile-overlap` run. Both defects were reproduced and then re-tested against a **real match** driven headlessly through the running Spring Boot app (loadout -> mulligan -> player SETUP), with the `place` route delayed through a Playwright route handler to open the in-flight window on demand. Duplicate placement, 390x844: firing a second `placeCard` inside the first request's window used to end with `board: [Draco]` and `hand: [Emberpup, ...]` — the first card back in hand, the second on the board, `placementUsed: true`; after the fix the same script ends `board: [Emberpup]` with Emberpup gone from the hand and the second call suppressed. Cancelled drag, 390x844: dispatching `pointercancel` mid-drag over a legal cell used to place the card (`board: [Pylook]`, `placementUsed: true`); it now leaves `board: []`, `placementUsed: false`, the card still in hand and selected, and zero `.card-drag-ghost`/`.lifted-card-clone` nodes. Happy-path drag-and-drop re-checked at 390x844 and 1920x1080: the card leaves the hand DOM immediately on drop while the server state still lists it (`handNames` has it, `handDom` does not), the board shows it once the response lands, no orphan ghosts or `is-drag-source` leftovers, and no page errors at either viewport.

Original prompt: design change for Siege mode, battle should always be against 2 or 3 enemies , even boss fights should have "minion" enemies, that way the attacks are diverse. Scale the health and damage accordingly now that there are 3 enemies

- August 11, 2026 Siege encounters are squads of 2-3: every battle now fields two or three foes instead of one or two, and bosses/elites bring an escort of minions rather than standing alone, so the telegraphed attack pattern each round is varied. `SiegeContentService.generateEnemies` picks the count first (2 when the warband is down to its last Siegeling, otherwise 3 for BOSS/ELITE and 2-3 for plain battles from floor 3), then scales each foe by a role share of the encounter budget instead of the old flat per-type multiplier. Splitting a budget across more bodies makes an encounter *easier* at equal totals - focus fire removes attackers as the fight runs, costing a squad of n roughly (n+1)/2n of the damage it would otherwise land - so the shares sum above the old totals and land within ~15% of the old encounters once that decay is folded in: rank-and-file are 0.62 hp/dmg of the old solo foe, an elite leader keeps its 1.5/1.15 block beside two 0.85/0.70 minions, and a boss keeps 0.85x its HP and 0.90x its damage beside two 0.38/0.35 escorts. The pinned opening fight (`SiegeTuning.OPENING_FIGHT_*`) became a pair for the same reason - the first battle should teach the real shape of a fight - with the old single foe's 30 HP / 5 damage split and then raised for the same focus-fire decay, landing on 2x17 HP / 2x3 damage against the same yardstick. Squad-wide guards came with it: at most one foe per squad gets the party-wide Sweep (three sweepers would multiply line damage by the squad size), escorts run one fewer ability than the unit they guard, squadmates take distinct elements and names, and `SiegeCombatEngine.ENEMY_STATUS_CHANCE` dropped 20 -> 14 because it is rolled per hit and the extra attackers would otherwise raise Stun/Burn uptime by half again. Escorts draw their shade art from the rank-and-file stage rather than their leader's, so a boss still towers over its minions through the authored size band. `Combatant` gained a `leader` flag (serialized live and through checkpoints, so a resumed boss does not read as one of its own minions) and `.sprite.enemy.leader` lights the plate - deliberately NOT a size bump, because sprite height is the card's authored band from #697 and overriding it would shrink a GIGANTIC boss to spite its escorts. Only the boss itself keeps its title; its escorts are ordinary shades. Score and `enemiesDefeated` are still counted per foe, so both rise with the larger squads. Cache busting advanced to `adventure.css?v=55` and `adventure.js?v=60`.
- Verification: `./mvnw.cmd -q -DskipTests compile` exit 0 and the full JUnit suite green. New `SiegeEnemySquadTest` (8 tests, Spring-wired because generateEnemies reaches the catalog for shade art) pins the design: every type/floor/party combination fields 2-3 foes, the pinned opener is a pair whose total HP stays on the old yardstick, BOSS/ELITE have exactly one leader that out-HPs and out-hits its minions, plain battles have none, each rank-and-file foe is under 0.85x the old solo foe while the squad total stays inside the old 1-2 foe band, no squad has more than one sweeper, and squadmates never repeat an element or a name. `SiegeShadeEnemyTest.bossesKeepTheirTitleButStillGetArt` was narrowed to the leader and now also asserts the escorts ARE shades - the boss keeps its title, its minions do not. Client verified against a real Spring Boot instance with a fetch-stubbed copy of `adventure.html` driving a canned 3-foe boss encounter through the real renderers: `#enemyRow` renders three sprites, only the boss carries `.leader`, and the row fits with no horizontal overflow at 375x812 and 1280x800 with no console or page errors.

Original prompt: The Generoot is a tier 3 large siegeling but it appears small as a shade, is this because hes listed as a squire or do we need to update the logic for these siegelings / fix the scaling so siegelings always appear at the correct size / another issue is the Kilokong rental, its showing as the size of a tier 1 but its a tier 3 in the dashboard, with a larger size

Original prompt: This puzzle is unsolvable, the blue can't reach the other blue; fix the puzzle system. Follow-up: matching runes should never spawn next to each other when no interior path block can be built.

- August 10, 2026 Rune Circuit solvability/spacing release: completing a path now ends its drag immediately at the matching rune, preventing pointer drift from extending a valid connection past its endpoint. New LINE boards reject adjacent matching runes and require every carved solution to contain at least one interior path tile; the emergency fallback follows the same rule. Validation remains compatible with adjacent-pair puzzles already active during deployment. `adventure.html` advances `adventure.js` from v59 to v60. Tests cover generated-board solvability and endpoint distance, foreign endpoint blocking in the exhaustive test solver, legacy-board completion, client endpoint locking, and the cache pin.

- August 10, 2026 Siege battlefield sprites are now sized by the card's **authored size band** — the dashboard field the Keep already sizes residents by — instead of by evolution depth, and units drawn from a card they do not "own" can resolve that card at all. Two reports, one root cause chain. The boss "Squire Odo" wearing stage-3 Generoot art rendered starter-sized, and a rented Kilokong (stage 1 in the catalog but LARGE in the dashboard) did the same. The Squire title was a red herring: `SiegeContentService#bossName` only swaps the plate text. (1) Identity — sprite scale came from `evoStage`, which `SiegeService#serializeCombatant` derived from `sourceCardId`; shades and mercs deliberately carry none (foes have no evolution chain, mercs must not be offered evolution cards), so every one of them reported stage 1. Added `Combatant#artCardId`, appearance-only, set from the shade card in both enemy builders (`generateEnemies`, `generateOpeningEnemies`) and from the merc's card in `toMercCombatant`; `getDisplayCardId()` prefers `sourceCardId`, so player units and battle evolutions are untouched. Bosses set it regardless of `bossTitle` — they are the one case with no `shadeOf` to fall back on — and it round-trips through `snapshotCombatant`/`restoreCombatant` so a run resumed mid-battle does not shrink the boss again. (2) The wrong field — evolution depth is only one input to `SieglingSize#defaultFor`, and a designer can pin a band per card, so depth can never answer "how big is this creature". `serializeCombatant` now also serves `size` via a new `sizeBandOf`, resolved exactly as `KeepService` resolves residents (pinned band, else the rarity/depth default). `adventure.js#renderSpriteLine` sets `sp.dataset.size` (mirroring `keep.js`) and `adventure.css` renames `--evo-scale` → `--sprite-scale` and maps the bands: SMALL 1 / MEDIUM 1.19 / LARGE 1.48 / GIGANTIC 1.93 — keep.css's `.84/1/1.24/1.62` renormalised so SMALL is 1.0, identical proportions to the Keep but anchored so the common small Siegeling keeps the size it has today rather than shrinking. Evolving mid-battle still grows the sprite, now by the evolved card's authored band. `evoStage` stays in the payload as real data; it just no longer decides size. (3) CSS — `.sprite .sp-art img` capped with `max-height` only, so any upload shorter than the scaled box rendered at intrinsic size and the band never reached the pixels; it now takes `height:100%` with `object-fit:contain`. Cache pins `adventure.css?v=54` and `adventure.js?v=59` in `adventure.html`.
- Verification: `./mvnw -o compile` BUILD SUCCESS and the whole `com.sieglings.adventure` package green at **119/119** (was 114), including the checkpoint/run-save suites that exercise the new snapshot field. New `SiegeSpriteSizeTest` drives a real run through `enterNode` and asserts off the serialized battle payload that every unit carries a valid band, that the band served equals the one its own card resolves to (`getSize()` else `defaultFor`), and that a merc resolves back to its card — each case counts its assertions so it cannot pass vacuously. `SiegeShadeEnemyTest` gained `shadesCarryTheCardTheirArtCameFrom` (12 seeds x battle/elite/boss/opening: every foe's `artCardId` resolves in the catalog, equals `getDisplayCardId()`, and points at the card whose art it is wearing) and `mercsAreSizedFromTheirCardWithoutBecomingEvolvable` (gains the art id, keeps a null `sourceCardId`). Layout proved with a new `tests/siege-sprite-scale-check.py` (Playwright Chromium 1217 over `python -m http.server`, no backend — `window.fetch` stubbed to a pending promise) at 390x844 portrait, 844x390 landscape and 1920x1080: all four bands compute their expected `--sprite-scale`, a LARGE foe measures 1.48x a SMALL one's art box (127x95 vs 86x64 phone; 296x281 vs 200x190 desktop), a LARGE ally matches a LARGE foe to within 1px, and GIGANTIC still fits inside the `overflow:hidden` `.battle-stage` on every viewport with no page scroll and no console errors. That check is what caught the `max-height` gap — desktop art measured 200px tall inside a 428px box before the CSS fix. Screenshots: `output/web-game/siege-sprite-scale/`.

Original prompt: Two updates, one keep a consistent card size in the hand selector do not change the size based on number of cards. Also. When in battle phase show the acting card not a random card. This is all on desktop

- August 10, 2026 Two desktop-dock fixes in `game.js`. (1) The Hand Selector sized every card by dividing the rail by the **live** hand count, so a card's footprint tracked how many you were holding: measured at 1920x1080 the same card rendered **261px wide with one card in hand, 229 at two, 166 at three, 127 at four, 99 at five, 81 at six and 58 at eight** — and because the rail is content-sized, the whole hand section grew with it (tray height 151px → 379px), shoving the Menu & Info panel around every time a card was drawn or played. `syncDesktopHandSelectorCardScale` now divides by a fixed `HAND_SELECTOR_DESKTOP_CARD_SLOTS = 6` whenever `isDesktopSidebarLayout()` is true, so the card keeps one size and the rail scrolls (it already had `overflow-x: auto`, `flex-wrap: nowrap`, wheel and pointer auto-scroll) once the hand outgrows six. Six slots rather than five because the common turn-loop hand is five-to-seven cards and sizing for five would push a routine sixth card behind a scroll; the cost is that a card is 81px instead of the old 99px five-card case. Phone and compact-landscape keep the live-count path untouched — the change is gated on the desktop sidebar, and phone-portrait/phone-landscape were confirmed unchanged. (2) During BATTLE the Menu & Info **Card** pane showed whatever hand card was left selected back in Setup — the reported screenshot had the pane on `Chain Lightning` while the Battle Action panel read `Simicircuit · ACTING NOW`, because `getFocusedPreviewCard()` fell through to the stale `selectedCard` and `renderDesktopCardPreviewPanel` backstopped that with `hand[0]`, neither of which the player can act on while the hand is docked away. The preview now resolves the acting Siegeling from `gameState.pendingBattle.instanceId` (falling back to the previous actor while the opponent resolves, since the server only hands us a `pendingBattle` for our own side, so the pane holds steady between actors instead of flicking back to a hand card), skips `selectedCard`/`hand[0]` entirely while `isHandHiddenForPhase()`, and gets a battle-specific empty state. Board hover and a deliberate board click still win over the acting default; `syncActingPreviewFocus` in `render()` releases that manual focus when the queue advances to a new actor, so an arena selection made during Setup cannot pin the preview to a bystander for the whole battle. Cache pin: `game.js` 240 → 241 on `play.html`, `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean; no Java touched, so no service tests were rerun, and no CSS changed, so the `tests/mobile-overlap` guard (which fixtures shared tile/button CSS) was not exercised. Both fixes were measured against a **real match** driven headlessly through the running Spring Boot app — loadout → mulligan → placement → battle phase — not a mocked state, and both were replayed against `git stash`ed baseline code in the same harness to confirm the before-picture. Hand sizing at 1920x1080, hand set to 8/6/5/4/3/2/1 cards: after, the card measures **81px at every count** with the tray fixed at 151px and `scrollWidth` 722 > 541 at eight cards (it scrolls, as intended); before, the same sweep read **58, 81, 99, 127, 166, 229, 261px** with the tray ballooning to 379px. Phone-portrait 390x844 and phone-landscape 844x390 report `isDesktopSidebarLayout() === false` and identical card sizes across 3/5/8 cards (74x104 and 78x109), i.e. untouched, with no page errors. Battle preview: with the bug's exact setup reproduced — a spell deliberately left in `selectedCard` — the Card pane's title reads **`Sundile`, matching `gameState.pendingBattle.name` and the ACTING NOW header**, and clicking a different board Siegeling still switches the pane to `Shellpack`. The same script on baseline code renders **`Backfire Sigil`** (the stale trap) while `Sundile` is acting, reproducing the screenshot exactly.

Original prompt: The bottom bound of pop ups (motivations , friends) etc should all be above the bottom hud

- August 10, 2026 Every hub popup now anchors to the docked HUD's **measured** height instead of a constant. An audit of all eight overlays at 390x844 found the constants were each shorter than the nav actually is: the notification panel's `bottom: 200px` overlapped by 5px, `.shop-card-preview-modal`'s `194px` by 11px, and the Friends card — whose bottom padding only covered the home indicator, never the dock — by **93px**. Signing in makes it far worse: the extra action buttons take the nav from 205px to 255px, and because `--bottom-hud-height` was only re-measured on resize/orientation/minimize/route-change (none of which fire when buttons appear), the value went stale and even the Card View and Filter trays, which *did* read the variable, fell 42px under the dock. So the fix is two-sided. CSS gained one shared token, `--hud-clearance: calc(var(--bottom-hud-height, 205px) + 8px)`, and the notif panel, Friends modal, profile modal, shop card preview, shop purchase confirm, and both trays now derive their bottom bound and their `max-height` from it — no popup guesses the HUD's height any more, and the 170px/194px/200px/216px/240px stand-ins are gone. JS gained `observeBottomHud`, a `ResizeObserver` on `.home-nav`, so the measurement tracks *any* height change rather than the ones we thought to enumerate. The Field Guide is deliberately left alone: it is a full-screen takeover (`height: 100dvh`, no radius or border) that covers the HUD entirely rather than being cut by it, so nothing of it is obscured — worth revisiting only if that pattern itself is unwanted. Pins: `home.js` 139 → 140, `home.css` 128 → 129 on `home.html` (plus `home.css` on `card-dashboard.html`).
- Verification: `node --check` clean; full `./mvnw test` **498/498** green with one new static-asset guard asserting the shared token exists, that none of the removed constants are still present, and that the `ResizeObserver` is wired — the half that a CSS-only fix would silently miss. Behaviour was measured across a matrix: an eight-overlay audit at **390x844 and 430x932**, each at a signed-out HUD (205px) and a signed-in HUD (255px, reproduced by revealing the hidden action buttons the way authentication does). After: every popup reports a **positive gap** to the nav's top edge — 8px for the panels and trays, 28–58px for the centred Friends card — at all four combinations, and the measured variable follows 205 → 255 with no resize event, which is the ResizeObserver doing its job. Before, replayed against `git show HEAD:` for both files: notif −5px, Friends −93px, shop preview −11px signed-out, degrading to −55px, −143px and −61px signed-in with the trays at −42px. A third run — **new CSS against the old JS** — still fails everything at −42px, confirming the observer is load-bearing rather than belt-and-braces. The top-inset fix from the previous entry survives the rewrite: the notif panel's top edge still computes to 99px with a 59px inset and 40px with none.

Original prompt: Next update on this branch, leaderboard should have some data for all time make sure this system is working / Also the notification popup extends to far into the top safety zone

- August 10, 2026 The all-time leaderboard was never empty — the hub was rendering a **failed fetch** as an empty board. Probing live `GET /api/leaderboards` proved the backend healthy and populated (`allTime.wins` = Roc 18, lawz67 2, Tron Dreamer 2; 25 matches, 37 spells, 67 Siegelings across three players), so the break was entirely client-side: `fetchJson` returns `{ error }` on failure, `loadAll` stored that truthy object as `state.leaderboards`, and the error banner's guard `state.leaderboardsError && !state.leaderboards` could then never fire — so a 503 or a network blip fell through to `No leaderboard results yet.` with the header pill showing the period label instead of `Updated …`, which is exactly the state in the reported screenshot. New `applyLeaderboardsPayload` keeps the payload and the error apart (only a real payload becomes state), `leaderboardListMarkup` renders three distinct states — loading, failed-with-Retry, genuinely-empty — and `retryLeaderboards` refetches past the 60s cache and re-renders, so a Cloud Run cold start no longer leaves the panel wrong until a reload. Two backend hardenings went with it: `refreshIfStale` now keeps serving the last good snapshot when a rebuild throws (one Firestore blip past the TTL used to turn a populated board into a 500 for **every** player, since `getSnapshot` had no catch), and the wins board switched to `requirePositive` — live `monthly.wins` was ranking Roc #1 with **0 wins** because losing a match still put you on the wins board. Matches-played keeps its zero floor, where every counted match contributes 1 by definition. Pins: `home.js` 138 → 139, `home.css` 127 → 128 on `home.html` (and `home.css` on `card-dashboard.html`, which also includes it).
- The notification popup is bottom-anchored to the docked HUD and grows upward, so its `max-height` was the only thing holding it out of the status bar: with `bottom: 200px + inset-bottom` and `max-height: 100vh - 240px - inset-bottom`, the top edge lands at a fixed **40px** from the top of the display regardless of device — inside the ~59px top inset on a Dynamic Island iPhone. The rule now subtracts `env(safe-area-inset-top)` as well, so that 40px is measured from the bottom of the safe area instead of the bottom of the display, and uses `100dvh` (with a `100vh` fallback line) because with browser chrome visible `vh` is the taller number and would push the top edge back under the inset. Phones without a top inset are unchanged at 40px.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `home.js`; full `./mvnw test` **497/497** green, five of them new. Three cover `LeaderboardService` through the real `refreshSnapshot`/`getSnapshot` path rather than its pure helpers — an all-time board aggregated from matches too old for any other bucket (2 matches, 5 spells, 9 Siegelings for the leader, every period key present), a wins board that excludes a player who only lost while still counting their match as played, and a failing store that keeps serving the previous snapshot. Two are static-asset guards: that `state.leaderboards = leaderboards || null` is *gone* (the exact line that masked the error) alongside the retry markup, and that the notif-panel ceiling subtracts the top inset. Behaviour was measured, not inferred. Leaderboard: headless Chromium at 390x844 booted the real `home.html` with `/api/leaderboards` stubbed to **the payload captured from production**, and drove the All Time tab through three routes — 200 renders `#1 Roc 18 / #2 lawz67 2 / #3 Tron Dreamer 2` with the pill reading `Updated Aug 10, 5:14 PM`; 503 renders `Leaderboards are warming up.` plus a Retry button and pill `All Time`; and clicking Retry after a 503 refetches (2 calls) and lands the same three rows. The before-picture was captured by routing `/js/home.js` to `git show HEAD:` and replaying the identical 503: `{pill: "All Time", empty: "No leaderboard results yet.", error: null, hasRetry: false}` — the reported screenshot, reproduced. Notif panel: at 390x844 and 430x932 with the list overfilled so the cap binds, the top edge moves from **40px to 99px** once a 59px inset is in the calc (measured in-browser by substituting the inset, since Chromium reports zero insets and *supports* the env var, so an `env(x, 59px)` fallback probe reads 0 and proves nothing), the zero-inset case stays at 40px, and the panel stays inside the viewport with no page errors.

Original prompt: Keep more, team should show 3/3 and reduce as teams are put in use instead of the other way around

- August 10, 2026 The Keep header's Teams pill now reads as crews **on hand** rather than crews busy. `renderHeaderCapacityCounters` in `keep.js` computed `${activeTeams}/${teamCapacity}`, so a keep with three idle construction slots showed `0/3` — the number climbed as the player *lost* the ability to start projects, which reads backwards next to the Materials and Ready pills, where a bigger number is always more to spend. It now derives `availableTeams = teamCapacity - activeTeams` and renders `${availableTeams}/${teamCapacity}`: `3/3` at rest, dropping to `2/3`, `1/3`, `0/3` as crews are put on projects. The hover title and `aria-label` moved with it (`3 free · 0 building`, "3 of 3 teams free, 0 building") so the assistive reading matches the glyph, and the static placeholder in `keep.html` went `0/1` → `1/1` so the pre-snapshot frame does not flash the old polarity. Server-side numbers are untouched — `KeepService` still serializes `constructionSlots` plus the active-construction list, and the Projects panel's crew note still says "Construction teams: N/M active" because that line labels its own polarity explicitly. The Siegelings pill is deliberately left as `15/16` (residents assigned out of capacity): it counts a roster, not a spendable pool. Cache pin: `keep.js` 52 → 53 on `keep.html`; `keep.css` unchanged at 54.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `keep.js`. Focused `./mvnw -Dtest=GameJavaScriptRegressionTest test` green at **61/61**, one of them new — `keepTeamsPillCountsDownFromFullCapacity` asserts the available-teams computation is present, that the old active-teams render string is *gone* (the form that would silently flip the polarity back), and that the HTML placeholder matches; the existing `keepBuildingsAndRoomsUseLayeredPaperTreatments` pin moved to `keep.js?v=53`. Behaviour was measured, not inferred: headless Chromium drove the real `keep.html` shell through `tests/keep-test-server.py` with a 3-slot snapshot at three occupancies, at **390x844 and 1920x1080** — 0 active → `3/3` (title `3 free · 0 building`), 1 active → `2/3`, 3 active → `0/3`, with no text clipping inside the pill, no horizontal page overflow, and zero page errors at either viewport. Worth knowing for the next person using that harness: test mode runs `completeMockConstructionIfReady` off the *real* wall clock, so fixture `completesAt` values must be genuinely in the future or the mock silently completes every project and the pill honestly reports an empty yard.

- August 9, 2026 Chain damage now plays as a ricochet instead of a barrage. Every victim of a `chain_damage` ability shares one attacker, so `action-queue.js` grouped them into a single multi-target ATTACK and fired all projectiles from the attacker simultaneously — a chain looked exactly like an AoE and never showed the links it travels. The board diff arrives in row/col order and carries no notion of "the one that was targeted", so the ordering is recovered from the log lines `EffectService.applyChainDamage` already writes: new `parseChainArcFromLog` reads `"<Ability> arcs through <Primary>'s links to N connected Sieglings!"` (and `"… finds no links on <Primary>."`), and `buildChainSteps` turns a damage group into `[{ primary, links }]` hops. A card is only accepted as a primary when a damage line in the same batch names the *same ability* hitting it, and a bounce victim is only attached to a primary whose cell it touches (Chebyshev 1 — notch links reach one step in any of the eight directions, diagonals included); any victim the chain cannot explain drops the whole group back to the old simultaneous barrage, so an unrelated attack in the same batch cannot reorder someone else's hits. Playback (`playChainAttack`) fires attacker → primary, lands that impact and drops the primary's HP, then fires primary → every link at once and lands theirs; a multi-primary chain repeats the pair per primary. A victim killed by its hop takes its impact immediately but keeps its card on screen through `applyLethalImpactHealth` + the pending lethal hold, and only comes apart after the last arc has left — otherwise a dead primary would be an empty cell for the bounce to originate from. Sourceless chains (trap/spell/trainer active) keep the elemental border on the primary, since nothing crossed the board to reach it, but the arcs off it still fly, because those really do travel the links. Cache pin: `action-queue.js?v=41` on `play.html` (and the `border-fx-fixture.html` harness); no `game.js`/`style.css` change, so those stay at 240/230.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `action-queue.js`; full `./mvnw test` **491/491** green (up one — the new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#chainDamageStrikesItsTargetBeforeArcingToTheLinkedSieglings`, which asserts the hop order in `playChainAttack`, that the chain branch is reached before the generic barrage, that the destruction runs after the hop loop, and — the coupling that actually breaks silently — that `EffectService` still logs `" arcs through "` / `" finds no links on "`). Behaviour was measured, not inferred: headless Chromium at **390x844 and 1920x1080** drove the real queue through `border-fx-fixture.html` with `SieglingsFx.attackCell` wrapped to timestamp every projectile. Chain case (Voltcub at player 2,1 → Pylme at enemy 1,1, linked to Sleaf 1,0 and Mossling 0,1): **3 projectiles — P21→E11 at t=83ms, then E11→E01 and E11→E10 both at t=604ms**, i.e. the bounces leave the struck card ~520ms later and fire together. Lethal-primary case: primary struck at 74ms, the arc still departs E11 at 595ms. Regression case — a two-target AoE with no arc line — still fires both from P21 at the same timestamp, so the barrage path is untouched. Re-ran the identical harness against `git show HEAD:…/action-queue.js` for the before picture: all three chain projectiles left P21 at t=82ms. Zero page errors at either viewport. `tests/mobile-overlap` not run (no `node_modules` here); no tile or button CSS moved, and the change adds no persistent DOM.

Original prompt: When energy boost targets another card with the card element selected make sure it adds energy of the target cards type

- August 8, 2026 `energy_boost` now reads the card it names. With `targetElement` left unset (the dashboard's "Card element" row) the generated energy follows whichever card the ability points at rather than always the source: aim it at an ally and it generates *that ally's* element, once per named card, so `ALL_ALLIES` over a Fire + Ice + Earth board generates one of each. `SELF`/`PASSIVE` resolve to the carrier through the same path, so the existing shipped moves are unchanged. An explicitly picked energy type still wins, and - important - it is the *energy type*, never a target filter: `EffectService` resolves the targets for this key **unfiltered**, deliberately skipping `filterExplicitTargetElement`, so picking Water does not restrict the ability to Water cards. The continuously-applied passive follows the same rule for the shapes it can resolve without a player choice (`ALL_ALLIES`, `ROW_ALLIES`); `SINGLE_ALLY` has nobody to pick it every turn, so a passive with that shape stays on the carrier, and the dashboard blurb says so too rather than promising an ally's element. If every named card turns out to be poolless (Poison/Light/Neutral) the boost falls back to the source instead of silently fizzling. Authoring surfaces: the effect's target hints grew from `PASSIVE`/`SELF` to include `SINGLE_ALLY`/`ALL_ALLIES`/`ROW_ALLIES`/`ROW_SELECT_ALLIES` and its description explains what "Card element" resolves to - **in both `CardOverrideEditorService.buildEffectTypes` and `functions/editorMetadata.js`**, since the live host serves that endpoint from the Function (see the previous entry). `card-dashboard.js` 58 -> 59; no `game.js`/`style.css` change, so those pins stay at 240/230.
- Verification: `./mvnw test` green at 490 tests, six of them new. `EffectServiceTest` covers the rule from both directions - a Fire source tapping an Ice ally generates Ice and no Fire; `ALL_ALLIES` over Fire/Ice/Earth generates one of each; an explicit Water pick beats the target's element; an explicit Water pick does **not** filter which allies are named (2 water from 2 non-water allies, the regression that `filterExplicitTargetElement` would have caused); and a `SELF` boost still generates the source's element. `EnergyServiceTest` covers the passive equivalent: an `ALL_ALLIES` passive on a Fire carrier next to an Ice ally reports 1 passive Fire and 1 passive Ice. Then the real app on :8080: `/api/cards/editor` serves the six target hints and the new description, and headless Chromium at 390x844 and 1920x1080 drove the Shared Abilities editor - opening `fire-energy-boost`, clearing the energy type to "Card element" and switching the target writes "Passively generates 1 energy of each ally's element each turn" for `ALL_ALLIES` and "…of this card's element…" for a passive `SINGLE_ALLY`, which is exactly what the engine does. No page errors at either viewport.

Both jobs green: Cloud Run 14:57:09-15:01:21Z, then Firebase Hosting 15:02:15-15:02:31Z and Functions 15:02:31-15:03:48Z. Live `/play` flipped to the new pins at 15:02:46Z. Full checklist green: `/play` serves `style.css?v=230`, `game.js?v=240` (action-queue stays `v=40`, play-hud `v=1`, card-binder-visual `v=20`, unchanged as expected); `/home` serves `style.css?v=230`, `home.css?v=127`, `game.js?v=240`, `home.js?v=138`; `/siege` serves `adventure.js?v=58`, `adventure.css?v=53`, `siege-maps.js?v=3`; `/js/config.js` keeps `apiBaseUrl: ''`; `/api/cards/editor` reports `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true` on **Hosting and on Cloud Run directly**, now with **21** effect types including `energy_boost`; `/api/game/options` 200 with 6 decks, 153 cards, 13 trainers, 6 live elements (cold start ~18s, expected).
- Two things worth writing down for the next deploy. First, `/api/cards/editor` is **not** served by Cloud Run on the live host - `firebase.json` rewrites that exact path (and `/api/cards/editor/**`) to the Firebase **Function** `api`, which builds its metadata from `functions/editorMetadata.js`. Immediately after Hosting flipped, Cloud Run direct reported 21 effect types with `energy_boost` while Hosting still reported 20 without it; that was not a half deploy, just the Functions job still running - it landed at 15:03:52Z and Hosting matched. Any change to the effect registry has to be made in **both** `CardOverrideEditorService.buildEffectTypes` and `functions/editorMetadata.js` or the dashboard and the engine will disagree. Second, the four new classpath pool moves (`fire-/earth-/wind-/ice-energy-boost`) do **not** appear in the live dashboard's ability list, and that is pre-existing behaviour rather than a deploy fault: the Firestore snapshot already carries its own 181-move `moves` array, so `CardOverrideEditorService` uses it verbatim and never falls back to `movesPoolService.allMovesSorted()` - even the classpath baseline `fire-spark` is absent from the live list. The engine still resolves the new ids (MovesPoolService loads the classpath pool then overlays Firestore), and designers get the feature through the dashboard's new Energy Boost effect type rather than through those four ids.
- Verification: not just pins - the **live production bundle was driven in a browser**. Chromium cannot egress to the live host through this environment's proxy, so a threaded localhost mirror relays every request to `siegelingstcgtesting.web.app` through curl, the way the #679 and #680 checks did. At 390x844 and 1920x1080 against the live bundle: the energy view renders `Overcharged - +3 Fire / Lasts through Setup - fades when the battle phase begins` with a live `overcharge-pulse` animation and glow, the flagged Fire row reads `5 - 1 internal + 1 external + 1 passive + 3 overcharge`, the portrait HUD energy number resolves to `rgb(255, 230, 60)` with the pulse and the tooltip `Overcharged (+3) - extra energy until the battle phase begins`, and a side with no overcharge renders no banner - no page errors at either viewport. The deployed `game.js?v=240` and `style.css?v=230` were also fetched and grepped: `energyDetailOverchargeBlock`, `syncOverchargeCue`, `getOverchargeEnergyAmount`, `.energy-overcharge-banner`, `.energy-detail-row.is-overcharged`, `.m-counts strong.is-overcharged`, `@keyframes overcharge-pulse` all present, and the intermediate build's `is-pending` / `Charging -` markers absent, confirming the final build shipped rather than an earlier commit. Live `POST /api/game/new` (squire-bob / deck_fire_earth) returns `overcharged: false`, `overchargeEnergy: {}`, `passiveEnergy: {}` and **no** `pendingOverchargeEnergy`, which is the same proof on the backend side.

Original prompt: Yes, the passive ability applies immediately, the active version activates increasing energy immediately and lasting through your next setup phase. It deactivates before the battle phase starts

- August 7, 2026 Corrected the overcharge window to the owner's stated rule, replacing the delayed-by-a-turn model shipped in the previous entry. The passive form was already immediate and is unchanged. The **active** form is now immediate too: `EnergyService.grantOverchargeEnergy` turns the buff on the instant the card resolves and tops the live pool up directly (trainer actives never trigger a recalculation, so the pool has to be touched as well as recorded), the surge rides through the owner's next Setup phase, and `GameService.startBattlePhase` drops it - before the full energy restore - as the Battle phase opens. That single expiry point is what makes the rule hold however late in the round the buff was used: cast in Setup it lasts the rest of that Setup, cast during Battle it carries through the next Draw and Setup and dies at the next Battle. `Player` lost the pending bucket entirely (`addOverchargeEnergy`/`clearOverchargeEnergy` replace `addPendingOverchargeEnergy`/`promotePendingOverchargeEnergy`), `GameService.draw` no longer promotes anything (it just reports a surge carried in from the last battle phase), the `AIService` promotion mirror is gone because there is nothing to promote, and `pendingOverchargeEnergy` is off the serialized player. The energy view's muted "Charging" banner went with it - there is no waiting state left - and the live banner now reads "Lasts through Setup - fades when the battle phase begins", with the HUD tooltip matching. Pins: `style.css` 229 -> 230, `game.js` 239 -> 240 on play/home/card-dashboard, assertions moved with them.
- Verification: `./mvnw test` green at 484 tests. The headline test drives the real phase machinery rather than poking state: an active buff resolved during Setup is spendable immediately (2 electric before any recalculation), survives a mid-setup `recalculateEnergy`, still stands after the next `draw`, and is gone with `Overcharge fades as the battle phase begins` in the log once `endTurn` carries the round into `startBattlePhase` (asserted on the real phase, not a reflective call). A second test spends 2 of 3 overcharged fire and confirms a later recalculation keeps it at 1 instead of refunding; a third confirms the AI's own draw/setup phase does not wipe a surge on its side. `EffectServiceTest` now asserts the immediate grant and the chosen energy type landing in the pool. `node --check` clean. Real app on :8080 with headless Chromium at 390x844 and 1920x1080: the banner reads "Overcharged - +3 Fire / Lasts through Setup - fades when the battle phase begins" with a live `overcharge-pulse` animation and glow, the flagged Fire row still reads `5 - 1 internal + 1 external + 1 passive + 3 overcharge`, the portrait HUD number is `rgb(255, 230, 60)` and pulsing with the updated tooltip, a side with no overcharge renders no banner and keeps its class and title clean, and a stale `pendingOverchargeEnergy` payload from an older client now renders nothing rather than a ghost banner. No page errors at either viewport.

Original prompt: There should be an ui effect when you energy is overcharged. In the energy view and on the energy number in the portrait hud. If a card has an active energy buff effect. It applies on the players next setup and battle Phase.

- August 7, 2026 Active `energy_boost` became a real **overcharge** with its own timing window and a visible cue. Timing: resolving an active energy buff (Siegling battle move, spell, trap, or trainer active) no longer drops energy into the pool on the spot - it banks a pending overcharge on `Player`, which goes live when that side opens its next turn and rides the pool through that **Setup and Battle phase**, then expires at the following Draw. `EnergyService.applyEnergyTotals` adds the live overcharge on top of board energy and claim-style temporary adjustments, so it survives the full energy restore that opens the battle phase while a spend still stays spent (spends are recorded as negative temporary adjustments, so the arithmetic nets out). `GameService.draw` is the turn boundary (expire, then promote, with a log line naming the elements); `AIService.executeAITurn` runs its own draw phase and never calls `GameService.draw`, so the promotion is mirrored there or an AI-side buff would bank forever. Serialization gained `overcharged`, `overchargeEnergy`, and `pendingOverchargeEnergy` per side. UI: the energy view shows a pulsing "Overcharged - +N Fire" banner with the phase window spelled out and marks the boosted element rows in electric yellow (the per-element breakdown now reads `5 - 1 internal + 1 external + 1 passive + 3 overcharge`), and the portrait HUD's energy number glows and pulses with a matching tooltip. Energy banked but not yet live renders as a muted "Charging" banner instead, so the delay is legible rather than looking like nothing happened. The pulse is dropped under `prefers-reduced-motion`. Long breakdowns now right-align their wrapped remainder under the value column instead of drifting under the label. Pins: `style.css` 228 -> 229 and `game.js` 238 -> 239 on play/home/card-dashboard, with the `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` assertions moved to match.
- Verification: `./mvnw test` green at 484 tests. New coverage: `GameServiceTest` drives the real phase machinery - an active buff used during BATTLE changes nothing for the rest of that phase, goes live on the next `draw` (2 electric, enemy still 0), survives the Battle-phase `recalculateEnergy`, and is gone at the draw after that; a second test spends 2 of 3 overcharged fire and confirms the restore keeps it spent at 1 rather than refunding it; a third pins the AI-side promotion through `executeAITurn`. `EffectServiceTest` now asserts the active form banks *pending* rather than live energy and honours the chosen type after promotion. A new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` case pins the whole cue - both overcharge banners in `renderEnergyDetailPanel`, the marked rows, `syncOverchargeCue` on the HUD energy number, the four CSS hooks, the exact reduced-motion block, and the paired cache pins. Then the real app on :8080: `POST /api/game/new` serializes `overcharged: false`, `overchargeEnergy: {}`, `pendingOverchargeEnergy: {}` on a live match. Headless Chromium at 390x844 and 1920x1080 - computed styles confirm the banner actually animates (`overcharge-pulse`) and glows, the flagged Fire row renders `5 - 1 internal + 1 external + 1 passive + 3 overcharge`, the portrait HUD number turns `rgb(255, 230, 60)` with a text shadow, the pulse animation and the tooltip "Overcharged (+3) - extra energy this Setup and Battle phase", and a side with no overcharge keeps its class, title, and colour untouched; screenshots of the energy view at both viewports and of the lit `E: 5` in the real portrait HUD confirm it visually. No page errors at either viewport.

Original prompt: Add an ability call energy boost, where a card can generate energy without a connection. So passive ability can give ap amount of energy. You need to select the energy type.

- August 7, 2026 New `energy_boost` effect key: a card generates elemental energy on its own, with no reciprocal notch link and no perimeter socket involved, and the designer picks which energy type it makes. As a **passive** it is computed inside `EnergyService.analyze` alongside links and call wells, so the owner's pool carries `effectValue` extra energy of the chosen type for exactly as long as the card is alive on the board (placement pays out immediately; the Draw-phase clear of claim energy does not touch it, and the grant disappears with the card). The type comes from the ability's `targetElement`, falling back to the card's own element; Poison/Light/Neutral have no pool, so an explicit poolless pick generates nothing rather than quietly paying into another element. `EnergyBreakdown` gained a `passiveEnergy` map (folded into the per-element totals, kept out of `internal`/`external` so those keep meaning "link"/"socket"), `GameController.serializePlayer` ships it as `passiveEnergy`, and the energy panel now reads `4 - 1 internal + 1 external + 2 passive`. Because the engine keeps the passive applied continuously, `BattleService` no longer offers it as a battle action, and a loadout made of nothing but auto-applied passives now falls back to the generated battle options instead of leaving the Siegling with no action at all. Written non-passive the same key resolves once through `EffectService` and banks the energy as a temporary adjustment (spendable in the same phase, cleared at that side's next Draw) so spells, traps, and trainer actives can use it. Siege translation is `GAIN_AP` - AP is Siege's energy - capped at 2 per card, targeted at the caster, with an `actionPoints` playback event and banner. Authoring surfaces: `targetElement` now threads through `ManualAbilityDefinition` (so `siegling-overrides.json` abilities can name the energy type), the dashboard effect list gained Energy Boost, and its element select turns into an "Energy type" picker whose unset row reads "Card element". Four shipped pool moves (`fire-/earth-/wind-/ice-energy-boost`, passive, 1 energy, 0 cost). Docs updated in `ABILITY_EFFECT_KEYS.md` (both tables) and `CARD_TUNING.md`; `game.js` 237 -> 238 on play/home/card-dashboard, `card-dashboard.js` 57 -> 58, `adventure.js` 57 -> 58 (with the pinned assertions in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` moved to match).
