- Verification: not just pins - the **live production bundle was driven in a browser**. Chromium cannot egress to the live host through this environment's proxy, so a localhost mirror relays every request (pages, bundles, `/api/**`) to `siegelingstcgtesting.web.app` through curl, the way the #679 binder investigation did. One wrinkle worth recording for the next person: `curl -D -` dumps *every* header block it sees - the proxy's `HTTP/1.1 200 Connection established` for the CONNECT tunnel **and** Hosting's `103 Early Hints` - so a mirror that splits on the first blank line serves the real headers as page text; consume header blocks while the remaining bytes still start with `HTTP/`. At 390x844 and 1920x1080 against the live site: `window.SieglingsPlayHud` present, the coin pill, Join With Code, Friends, bell and `?` all render with non-zero boxes, nav overflow 0. The phone dock's first action row is Siegecoins / **Play** / Lobbies with Play centered, desktop keeps its original order. The notification tray opens; the Friends popup opens and measures as a popup (370x620 on phone, 520x640 on desktop) showing the signed-out "Sign in to add friends" state; the Field Guide loads `/help.html?embed=1`; and **Join With Code against the live backend** leaves `matchMode: online`, `onlineRoomMode: join`, the Join Room tab `aria-pressed="true"`, the online panel visible and `document.activeElement` = `roomCodeInput`. Zero page errors at both viewports. The only failed requests are three third-party CDNs (howler, two gstatic firebase modules) that the mirror does not proxy - an artifact of the harness, not of the live site.

- August 7, 2026 Merge of #680 (play HUD: Siegecoins, Join With Code, Friends, notifications, Field Guide; hub Friends modal as a popup). One pre-merge fix was needed: `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` string-matches the exact `?v=` pins on `home.html`/`play.html`/`card-dashboard.html`, so the bumps to `style.css?v=228`, `game.js?v=237` and `home.css?v=127` had to land in its assertions or `main` would have gone red on the merge commit. **The deploy did not ship on the merge push.** Run [31121957794](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/31121957794) never got a runner: Cloud Run Deploy queued 17:03:37Z and was cancelled 17:18:38Z, Firebase Deploy queued 17:18:39Z and was cancelled 17:33:40Z, both with `runner_id: 0` — GitHub's 15-minute queue-then-cancel. The cause was external and confirmed on GitHub's status API: **Actions was in `major_outage`** from an incident opened 15:22Z ("workflow runs are failing or delayed in starting, and some queued jobs may time out"), recovering to `operational` at 00:05Z. Nothing half-shipped — neither surface moved, so live stayed internally consistent on the previous build (`style.css?v=226` / `game.js?v=236`) rather than landing a new frontend in front of an old API the way #604/#605 did. No manual deploy was possible from the agent environment: no `gcloud`, no `firebase-tools`, no service-account credentials. Re-running the workflow from the API was also unavailable — the GitHub App token has no `actions: write`, so both `run_workflow` and `rerun_workflow_run` return 403 — so this commit is the re-trigger: `deploy.yml` fires on push to `main`, and merging this record starts a fresh run against the same tree.

- August 6, 2026 The Battle Table HUD (`play.html`'s `.play-hub-nav`) now carries the same five controls the hub HUD has: a Siegecoins pill, Join With Code, Friends, a notification bell, and the Field Guide "?" button. The hub owns those controls inside `home.js` - a 10k-line IIFE wired to hub routing and state that `play.html` does not load - so the play side got its own small bundle, `js/play-hud.js`, loaded after `game.js` and reading the account snapshot `game.js` already keeps in `authState` (the `/api/auth/me` payload carries `friends`, `incomingFriendRequests`, `outgoingFriendRequests`, and `progression.gold`). Join With Code does better than the hub's version (which just routes to `/social`): it opens the loadout, flips it to Online -> Join Room, and focuses `#roomCodeInput`, re-applying once if the catalog fetch is still in flight, and confirming first if a match is live. The single hook into `game.js` is one line at the top of `renderPlayHubAuth` calling `window.SieglingsPlayHud?.syncAuth()`, so every auth refresh repaints coins and the request badge; on `home.html`/`card-dashboard.html` (which also load `game.js`) the namespace is absent and the call is a no-op. Original prompt: update the autofill accross the dashboard to understand and update on target, ability changes for all skills

- August 6, 2026 The dashboard's ability-description autofill now tracks target/effect edits on **every** skill surface. Two defects, one root cause. First, `syncTrainerAbilityAutoDescription` bailed out on `kind !== "passive"`, so a SiegeKnight's **active** ability never got a generated blurb at all — the reported case was Isaac Tesla's "Coil Cage" (Speed Zero → Single Enemy) sitting with an empty Description while the passive above it was filled in. Second, every surface decided "is this text mine to rewrite?" by comparing against a *last generated* string stashed in a DOM `dataset.autoDescription`. That memory dies on reload and does not follow record selection, so any description loaded from Firestore was classified as hand-written and then frozen: retargeting or reassigning the effect left text describing the old skill (the same trainer's passive still read "All Electric allies gain +5 Speed" — `speed_boost` wording — under a `connected_allies_speed_boost` effect). Replaced the dataset bookkeeping with shape matching: `resolveAutoDescription(current, generate, cacheKey)` asks the generator what the blurb would read under every target × effect × row × passive combination reachable from the current one, folds digits to `#` so an Effect Value edit cannot strand a sentence, and rewrites only when the stored text matches one of those shapes. Anything a designer actually wrote matches nothing and is returned untouched; a combo the generator no longer covers clears rather than lying. The sweep is memoised per `cacheKey` (which names the inputs it holds fixed — required energy, source element, move target element) because `renderAll` runs on every keystroke. Added `buildAutoTrainerActiveDescription`: an active resolves on the targets the designer picked, so it reads like a Siegling move and must not inherit the trainer's element prefix the way a passive aura does. All three surfaces — spell/trap ability editor, SiegeKnight passive **and** active, Shared Abilities move draft — now share the one resolver. Cache pin: `card-dashboard.js?v=57`.
- Verification: no Node in this environment, so the bundle was exercised in a browser instead of `node --check`. Served `src/main/resources/static` with `python -m http.server` and drove the **real** `card-dashboard.html` (temporary copy with a `window.fetch` stub returning a canned `/api/cards/editor` payload: the Isaac Tesla trainer from the report, an auto-described spell, a hand-written spell, and one pool move). Zero console errors across the whole session. On load the active filled in as `Reduce 1 enemy speed to 0 (1 turn)` and the stale passive corrected itself to `Connected allies gain +5 Speed`. Live edits then tracked on all three surfaces: trainer active `ALL_ENEMIES` → `Nullify All enemies' speed (1 turn)` → effect `freeze` → `Freeze All enemies for 1 turn` → value 2 → `…for 2 turns` (plural switch) → `heal`/`ALL_ALLIES` → `Heal all allies for 2 HP` → `draw`/`SELF` → `Draw 1 card`; spell ability `Deal 4 damage to 1 enemy` → `Deal 4 damage to all enemies` → `Destroy all enemies` → `Heal the selected allied row for 7 HP`; pool move `Deal 6 damage to 1 enemy` → `Deal 6 damage to the selected enemy row` → `Chain 6 damage to …` → value 9 → `Reduce 1 enemy speed by 9`. Both hand-written guards held: the custom spell blurb and a manually typed move description survived retargeting **and** an effect swap unchanged. Harness page and server removed afterwards.

Original prompt: Logged in and while the shop is showing a loading screen the cards page still is not / I am not seeing that on live after several resets (screenshot: Card binder reading "0 owned cards - No owned cards match these filters" with 1029 Siegecoins in the HUD)

- August 4, 2026 A signed-in binder could report **"0 owned cards"** over a collection the browser already had on disk. The cause was not the loading gate that the first pass at this assumed - it was `applyGameOptions(null)`. `fetchGameOptions()` deliberately **skips the cache for signed-in players** (so a signed-in load never serves the guest catalog) and returns whatever `/api/game/options` gives back; on a failure - offline, a cold-start timeout, or the request aborted because the player reloaded while it was in flight - that is `null`, and `loadAll()` passed it straight to `applyGameOptions`, which substituted `{ decks: [], trainers: [], cardCatalog: [], liveElements: [] }`. That object is **truthy**, so `state.options` looked loaded to every consumer: `binderCatalog()` was empty, `filteredCards()` returned nothing, and the binder rendered its authoritative "No owned cards match these filters" empty card while gold, the HUD and navigation all rendered normally - which is exactly what the reported screenshot shows. Being signed in is what exposes it: the guest path returns its cached catalog before ever touching the network. Three changes: `fetchGameOptions` falls back to the cached snapshot for its own identity when the fetch fails instead of resolving null; `applyGameOptions` refuses to replace a populated catalog with a catalog-less payload; and `renderCards`/`renderDecks` now treat an empty catalog as *not loaded* (`hasCardCatalog`) rather than as an empty collection, since the game always has a catalog. `visibilitychange` already re-runs `syncCatalogIfVersionChanged()`, which refetches whenever the catalog is empty, so the loading state stays recoverable. Cache pin: `home.js?v=136`.
- **Found by driving production, not by reading the code.** Chromium cannot egress to the live host through this environment's proxy, so a localhost mirror was built that relays every request - pages, bundles and `/api/**` - to `siegelingstcgtesting.web.app` via curl; the browser then talks only to localhost while running production's own bytes against production's own API. A throwaway account was registered on the live site (`/api/auth/register` then `/api/player/starter-pack` with `pack_fire`) to have a real signed-in session: the server side proved healthy - `/api/auth/me` returns `ownedTotal: 5` with all five ids present in the 127-card live catalog - and a cold visit rendered 6 owned cards correctly. The **reload** pass is where it broke: `/api/game/options` was aborted by the reload, and the binder dropped to "0 owned cards" with `sieglingsAuthProfile` in localStorage still holding all 5. One caveat on that first mirror run: `spawnSync` made the relay serialize, which produced spurious `ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`s and a 20s stuck loader; the mirror was made concurrent before any conclusion was drawn from it.
- Reproduced deterministically before the fix and re-run after, at 390x844 with a seeded signed-in cache (catalog + profile holding 3 owned cards): with `/api/game/options` aborted, `?v=135` gives `0 owned cards / No owned cards match these filters`, and the fixed bundle gives `3 owned cards`. A slow-then-failed fetch and a successful fetch both give 3 either way, and a **first visit with no cache and a failed fetch** now shows the binder and deck loaders rather than a false empty state. Full Maven suite green (465 tests); new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#failedCatalogFetchNeverEmptiesTheBinder` pins the `hasCardCatalog` guard in `applyGameOptions`, the cache fallback in `fetchGameOptions`, and both render guards.

Original prompt: Not seeing chain attacks in dashboard

- August 4, 2026 "Chain Damage" is now offered by the card dashboard's Effect dropdown. It was missing because **`/api/cards/editor` is not served by Spring in production** — `firebase.json` rewrites both `/api/cards/editor` and `/api/cards/editor/**` to the Firebase Function `api`, and `functions/editorMetadata.js` hand-maintains its *own* copy of the effect catalog. That copy had 18 entries; `CardOverrideEditorService` has 19. `chain_damage` was the only drift, so the dashboard — which builds every Effect `<select>` from `state.metadata.effectTypes` — never saw it, while every backend test and the Cloud Run service kept reporting the effect as fully supported. Confirmed against production: Hosting's `/api/cards/editor` returned 18 effect types ending `…destroy, move_link`, exactly the tail in the report's screenshot, while the Cloud Run URL directly returned 19 including `chain_damage`. The dropdown was only half the damage: `functions/index.js` derives `SUPPORTED_EFFECT_TYPES` from the same array and line 386 runs `validateAbility` over **moves** as well as abilities, so a move hand-authored with `chain_damage` would have been rejected on save with "uses an unsupported effect type." No live card or move used the key yet, so nothing was already broken. Added the `chain_damage` entry to `functions/editorMetadata.js` in its Java position (right after `damage`) with the same label, description and `targetHints` (`SINGLE_ENEMY`, `ROW_SELECT_ENEMIES`, `ALL_ENEMIES`) as `CardOverrideEditorService`. No static asset changed, so no cache pins moved.
- Verification: new `FunctionsEditorMetadataParityTest` pins the two catalogs to each other in both directions — every `AbilityEffectKeys.all()` key must appear in `functions/editorMetadata.js`, and the Function must not offer a key `EffectService` cannot resolve (which would write an override the engine silently ignores). The guard was proved to bite: with the fix reverted it fails naming `[chain_damage]`, and passes with it restored. Full `./mvnw test` **464/464 green** (up from 462). Browser proof against the **real live payload** (164 cards, 158 moves, downloaded from Hosting) driving the real `card-dashboard.html`, with only `metadata.effectTypes` swapped, at **390x844 and 1920x1080**: before → move draft panel opens with **18** options and no `chain_damage`, reproducing the report; after → **19** options with `chain_damage` labelled "Chain Damage". Zero page errors at both viewports. `functions/` has no `node_modules` in this environment so the CI `npm run lint` require-check could not be run locally, but the edit is a dependency-free data literal and `node --check` plus a real `buildMetadata([])` call both pass.

- August 4, 2026 Integrated #654, #646 and #648 into `main`. **#626 was deliberately not merged — it is already on `main`.** Its `chain_damage` work was ported in `f35a1267` ("Rebase chain_damage onto main with affliction-compatible hits", via `cursor/chain-damage-compat-d230`) so chain hits route through `dealAbilityDamage` and pick up Blind/Soak/Rust and per-victim weakness; `main` carries the effect key, `getLinkedBoardCells`/`findBestChainPreviewCell`, the `.chain-target` CSS and all four `chainDamage*` tests. Merging the original branch would have replayed a pre-affliction implementation over the ported one, so #626 is superseded, not pending. #654 (projectiles reserved for attacks; elemental border for afflictions, traps, auras, statuses and heals) merged with five conflicts, every one a collision on shared append points rather than on logic: four cache-pin blocks and one add/add of new test methods in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest`, resolved as the union so `main`'s Leech/Settings-Guide assertions and #654's border assertions both survive. The pins are the part that could have shipped a silent regression — both sides had independently advanced `style.css` and `game.js` to the *same* numbers for *different* content, so taking either side would have left every browser that already cached `main`'s build serving the pre-#654 bundle forever. Pins therefore move past both sides: `style.css?v=224`, `game.js?v=233` (play/home/card-dashboard), `adventure.css?v=53`, `adventure.js?v=57`; `action-queue.js` keeps `?v=40` (`main` was still on 38) and `home.css` stays at `main`'s `?v=125` since #654 never touched it. #646 and #648 are documentation only — the `progress.md` deploy records for #644 and #647 — and git dropped each into its own chronological slot above the work it documents.

Original prompt: Traps and other auras need border effects as well projectiles should be for attacks only

- August 4, 2026 Projectiles are now reserved for attacks; every other way a card can be affected plays the elemental border. Extends the affliction work below to the rest of the queue's playback paths, and generalises its visual: `spawnAfflictionAura` → `spawnElementalBorder(isPlayer, row, col, {element|color, variant, durationMs, sigil})`, CSS `.sgl-affliction-*` → `.sgl-element-border` / `.sgl-border-*` with a `sgl-element-border-<variant>` hook per cause (`burn`, `toxin`, `effect`, `status`, `heal`). Four playback paths changed: **(1)** sourceless single-cell damage — traps, auras, effect ticks, and AI log lines the parser can't pin to a card — dropped `attackBetween` from `_getFallbackProjectileOrigin` (helper deleted outright) for the border; **(2)** multi-target damage now branches on whether an attacker exists, so a trap or board-wide aura lights one border per struck card — previously a sourceless AoE fell past every branch and played *nothing* at all, no projectile and no impact VFX; **(3)** `STATUS_APPLY`, which the code's own comment describes as the aura case, lights the status's element instead of firing a bolt; **(4)** `HEAL` blooms a green (`#5eff8e`, ✚) border rather than launching a green projectile — a heal is not an attack, so it falls under the same rule. The three surviving projectile calls are all gated on a resolved attacker cell: single-target attack, multi-target attack barrage, and the direct-HP-bar `attackPoint`. Traps and spells never sit on the board, so the queue could only reach for the caster's *knight* element to colour anything; added `window.SieglingsCardElements` in `game.js` — a lazily-indexed name → element lookup over `gameOptions.cardCatalog`, keyed by card name **and** ability name (card name wins, since ability names repeat across cards) because the damage line names the ability while the "casts X!"/"springs trap X!" line names the card. `parseEffectCardFromLog` + `effectElementForTarget` feed it, so an Ice trap now burns an ice-blue border instead of its owner's knight colour. Siege mode's `status` events got the same treatment via a `STATUS_ELEMENT` map mirroring `ElementalAfflictionCatalog`, and its `afflictionAura` helper was renamed `elementBorder`. Cache pins (rebased onto `main` at merge time): `style.css?v=224` + `game.js?v=233` (play/home/card-dashboard), `action-queue.js?v=40` (play), `adventure.js?v=57` / `adventure.css?v=53`.
- Verification: `node --check` on `game.js`, `action-queue.js`, `adventure.js`; full `./mvnw test` **456/456** green. New `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#projectilesAreReservedForAttacksAndEverythingElseGetsTheElementalBorder` slices each playback branch out of the queue and asserts the sourceless-damage, status and heal blocks contain `spawnElementalBorder` and no `attackCell`, that `_getFallbackProjectileOrigin`/`attackBetween` are gone from the file entirely, and that the catalog bridge + trap-element wiring ship on both sides. Headless Chromium at **390x844 and 1920x1080** against `border-fx-fixture.html` (renamed from `affliction-fx-fixture.html`; `SieglingsFx` stubbed to count every projectile call) across 7 cases: trap damage → **0 projectiles**, ice-blue `#76e6ff` border resolved through the catalog stub, not the knight; row-wide aura → 0 projectiles and **3** borders, one per struck card; status-only application → 0 projectiles, ICE border from `statusProfile`; heal → 0 projectiles, `#5eff8e` border; burn tick → 0 projectiles, `#ff501e` border; and the two regression cases — a Siegling attack still fires 1 projectile, a Siegling AoE still fires 2 — with zero borders. Zero page errors at both viewports. The `SieglingsCardElements` IIFE was also `eval`'d out of `game.js` in node and exercised directly: card-name lookup, ability-name lookup, case/whitespace normalisation, card name winning over another card's identically-named ability, rebuild on catalog swap, and null/empty/unknown all returning null — 10/10. Screenshots of the ice trap and green heal borders captured at 390x844.

Original prompt: When effects like burn or poison happen don’t send a projectile do a border effect matching grab Element type that triggers around the card

- August 4, 2026 Affliction damage no longer fires a phantom projectile — it burns an element-colored border around the afflicted card instead. Burn/Wither ticks resolve at the owner's Setup with no attacker anywhere on the board, so `action-queue.js` saw only an HP drop it could not attribute, fell into the sourceless branch, and flung a bolt at the card from `_getFallbackProjectileOrigin` (a fabricated point on the "attacker's" side of the board). Added a full affliction layer to the queue: `AFFLICTION_PROFILES` mirrors `ElementalAfflictionCatalog.java` (Burn→FIRE, Toxin→POISON, Wither→UNDEAD, …), `parseAfflictionTickFromLog` recognises `"<name> takes N burn damage (HP: X)."` (with `poison`/`toxin`/`venom` and the other badge words as aliases) plus `"<name> withers (clamped by N; HP: X)."`, and matching damage entries are split out of the diff **before** source resolution so they can never reach the projectile path. They become a new `AFFLICTION` action whose playback calls `spawnAfflictionAura` — a fixed-position overlay (same survives-a-re-render pattern as `spawnHealCross`) drawing two rings, an edge-in wash, twelve motes seeded around the perimeter so their staggered delays read as a wave circling the card, and the element sigil — then lands the damage floater 40% into the burn, so border and HP loss read as cause and effect. Lethal ticks keep the ghost/destroy ordering and defer their toast like a lethal attack. The same aura also fires when a badge *lands* on a real hit (`"<name> is afflicted with Burn (Burn x1)."` → aura on that attack's impact); that attack keeps its projectile, since something did attack. Toast reads "Pylme suffers Burn −3", sided with the card's owner rather than a nonexistent attacker. `ELEMENT_HEX`/`ELEMENT_SIGIL` gained the missing POISON (`#7ecb4d`, ☣) and LIGHT (`#ffe59a`, ☀) rows that Toxin and Blind need. Siege mode's `burn`/`poison`/`wither` events never fired projectiles, but got the matching `.sp-aura` border on the sprite for consistency. Original prompt: I am logged in I know my cards are loading, the shop is currently loading but i dont see a loading bar or spinner in cards? It looks like a bug but without the loading

- August 4, 2026 Card binder no longer flashes a false empty collection while the hub is still booting. Root cause: `ownedDataLoading()` treated any present `ownedCards` object — including an empty `{}` left in the cached auth profile — as binder-ready, so a returning signed-in visit with a cached catalog but a still-in-flight `/api/auth/me` (and shop packs) painted "0 owned cards" / "No owned cards match these filters" with no spinner, while `/shop` correctly showed its loader behind `shopPacksLoading`. Readiness now only skips the loader before the first authoritative sync when the cache actually has owned cards to paint; after sync, a present (even empty) map means the account truly owns nothing, and a missing map stays on the spinner. Shared by decks. Cache pin: `home.js?v=134`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/home.js`; focused `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#binderWaitsForOwnedCardSnapshotWhenCachedProfileIsPartial` plus home.js pin assertions. Headless Chromium against real `home.html` at 390x844 and 1920x1080 with a signed-in cached profile (`ownedCards: {}`, gold present), cached game options, and held-open `/api/auth/me` + `/api/shop/packs`: `/cards` shows `.panel-loading` ("Loading your card binder…", spinner + bar, `aria-busy=true`, count "Loading cards…") in parallel with `/shop`'s loader — no "0 owned cards" empty state — then both flip to real content once the stubs answer. A second case with cached owned cards still paints the binder immediately from cache.

Original prompt: Evolution should not cleanse or remove badges

- August 4, 2026 Evolving no longer wipes a Siegling's badges. `PlacementService.createPlacedInstance` built the evolved form as a brand-new `CardInstance` and only carried the permanent max-HP boost and accumulated damage across, so every affliction stack, the Chill freeze, `FREEZE`/`SPEED_ZERO`, and the shield/damage buffs the buff pills read from were silently dropped — evolution was a free full cleanse (and, with Curse the only affliction that blocks evolving, the cheapest answer to any other debuff on the board). Added `carryBadgesForward(existing, evolved)`: it copies the affliction map (via `setAfflictionStacks`, which deep-copies into a fresh `EnumMap` rather than sharing the reference), the new `chillFrozen` flag, the status-effect set, and re-applies `temporaryShield`/`temporaryDamageBuff` through `addShield`/`addDamageBuff` so their backing statuses land consistently. Trainer-passive and aura buffs are deliberately *not* copied — `GameService.placeSiegling` calls `recalculateTrainerPassiveStatBuffs` and `recalculateBoardAuraDamageBoosts` for the whole board immediately after placement, so copying them would double-count. The fix sits in `createPlacedInstance`, which both `GameService.placeSiegling` and `AIService` route through, so player and AI evolutions behave identically. Curse continues to block evolving outright (unchanged, documented function). Added the invariant to the shared inflict rules in `docs/ELEMENTAL_STATUS_EFFECTS.md`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/game.js`; full `./mvnw test` **454/454** green. New `PlacementServiceTest#evolutionCarriesBadgesForwardInsteadOfCleansingThem` seeds Burn x3, Soak x2, a Chill freeze, `FREEZE`, a 4-point shield and a +2 damage buff on the precursor, then asserts every one of them lands on the evolved instance with `HEALTH_BOOST`/`DAMAGE_BOOST` set, and that clearing the precursor's afflictions afterwards leaves the evolved card untouched (proves copy, not shared map). The pre-existing `evolutionCarriesPermanentMaxHealthBoostAndExistingDamage` still passes, confirming the HP math is unaffected — `addShield` touches only `temporaryShield`, never `currentHealth`.

Original prompt: Review this for all badges make sure they remove correctly according to their function

- August 4, 2026 Audited all 12 elemental afflictions and the 5 ability statuses against their documented contracts, enumerating every mutation site (`addAfflictionStacks` / `clearAffliction` / `clearAllAfflictions`) — exactly six afflictions had a clear path (Burn, Wither, Rust, Toxin, Insight and now Chill) and six had none (Stagger, Disorient, Soak, Shock, Curse, Blind). Five of those six are correct: Soak's doc says "persists while stacks remain (no auto-clear on hit)", Shock's and Curse's say "persist until cleansed / design TBD", and Disorient and Blind are continuous modifiers on every ability with no documented expiry. **Stagger was the one genuine defect** and the exact twin of the Chill bug: its whole function is the queue demotion it buys at 2 stacks — documented as "acts after everyone else *that round*" — but nothing ever cleared it, so `isStaggeredToBack` stayed true forever and two Earth hits benched a Siegling at the bottom of the battle queue for the rest of the match. Added `ElementalAfflictionService.clearSpentBattleAfflictions(state)`, called from `GameService.completeBattleIfFinished` right after `clearTempEffects`, which drops a full 2-stack Stagger at the end of the Battle phase it fired in and logs "recovers its footing (Stagger clears)". A lone 1st stack persists — like Chill 1–2 it is still counting up to its threshold. Stagger's catalog row is now `clearsOnTick=true` with the updated contract, and the `STAGGER` effect-key copy in `game.js` plus `docs/ELEMENTAL_STATUS_EFFECTS.md` gained the clear rule. Also verified the ability statuses: `FREEZE` is one-action (removed in `BattleService` after the skip) except chill-freeze, `SPEED_ZERO` clears at end of battle, `HEALTH_BOOST`/`DAMAGE_BOOST`/`SPEED_BOOST` are removed by `takeRawDamage`/`clearTemporaryEffects`/`setTrainerPassive*` when their backing value reaches 0, and the shield badge renders off live `shieldHp` so a stale status cannot paint a phantom chip. Noted but not changed: evolution builds a fresh `CardInstance`, so evolving silently cleanses every affliction (Curse excepted — it blocks evolving).
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/game.js`; full `./mvnw test` **453/453** green. New `ElementalAfflictionServiceTest#staggerClearsAfterTheBattleItBenchedTheCardIn` stacks Earth damage to 2 on one card and 1 on another, asserts `isStaggeredToBack` splits them, then drives the private `GameService.completeBattleIfFinished(GameState)` by reflection (not the helper directly, so the call wiring is covered) and asserts the benched card is back to 0 stacks and no longer demoted while the 1-stack card keeps its badge.

Original prompt: Once the badge effect has triggered, remove the badge for the abilities that apply , this card was already frozen as soon as it freezes remove the chill badges.

- August 4, 2026 Chill badges are now spent by the freeze they trigger. Previously the 3rd Chill stack added `StatusEffect.FREEZE` but left `CHILL x3` on the card, and because `GameService.clearTempEffects` strips `FREEZE` at the end of every battle phase, the stack count *was* the durable freeze — `isChillFrozen` read `getAfflictionStacks(CHILL) >= 3`. That is why a frozen Siegling showed a thermometer "3" and no Frozen badge (see the reported Spoutyl board). `ElementalAfflictionService.tryInflictFromDamage` now clears the CHILL stacks at the threshold and sets a new durable `CardInstance.chillFrozen` flag alongside `FREEZE`, matching how Insight already clears itself on its draw payoff; `isChillFrozen` reads that flag, `clearTempEffects` skips the `FREEZE` removal while it is set, and `thawChillOnSetup` clears flag + status + any partial stacks at the owner's next Setup (unchanged timing). Further Ice hits on an already-chill-frozen card no longer start a fresh badge stack underneath the Freeze. Net player-visible change: the card reads **Frozen**, not Chill x3, and its Speed number stops carrying the −3 slow it can no longer use. Chill's catalog row is now `clearsOnTick=true` with the updated contract text; the FREEZE/CHILL effect-key copy in `game.js` and `docs/ELEMENTAL_STATUS_EFFECTS.md` were rewritten to match. Cache pins: `game.js?v=230` (play/home/card-dashboard).
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/game.js`; full `./mvnw test` **452/452** green. `ElementalAfflictionServiceTest#chillSlowsThenFreezesAtThreeAndThawsOnSetup` extended to assert 0 CHILL stacks at the freeze, that a 4th Ice hit does not restack under the Freeze, and that the thaw restores full printed Speed (5); new `GameServiceTest#chillFreezeOutlivesTheBattlePhaseWithoutItsBadges` drives the private `clearTempEffects(GameState)` by reflection and asserts a chill-frozen card keeps `FREEZE` with zero stacks (the pre-existing `freezeExpiresWhenBattleTempEffectsAreCleared` still proves ability-freeze is one-phase). Headless Chromium at 390x844 against the real `play.html`, calling `renderStatusBadgesForCell` with the exact payloads the server now emits: old shape `{afflictions:[CHILL x3]}` → `CHILL` badge; new shape `{statuses:['FREEZE'], afflictions:[]}` → `FREEZE` badge only, no CHILL; partial `{afflictions:[CHILL x2]}` → still `CHILL`, so the slow badges are untouched.

Original prompt: Ok Here are my changes — Electric drains energy use from the targeting card; Toxin blocks heals (heals strip stacks); Wind raises lowest-cost ability; Curse blocks claim/evolve; Soak +dmg per stack on attacks; Earth 1 stack nothing / 2nd bottom of queue; Ice slow then freeze at 3 until Setup; Psychic→Insight draw at 3; Blind reduces AP values; Rust vulnerable to next Metal attack then clears.

- August 3, 2026 Elemental affliction design lock-in: rewrote the battle contracts in `docs/ELEMENTAL_STATUS_EFFECTS.md` and `ElementalAfflictionCatalog` to the owner's rules — Shock is a per-card spend cap (−1 energy usable per stack), Toxin blocks healing and converts heal amount into stack removal, Disorient raises only the lowest-cost ability (ties by ability order), Curse blocks claim **and** evolve, Soak is +1 damage from any attack per stack, Stagger is inert at 1 and bottom-of-queue at 2, Chill slows then Freezes at 3 until owner Setup, Psychic's badge is **Insight** (at 3 stacks the inflicter draws and stacks clear), Blind reduces ability effect values per stack, Rust only amplifies the next **Metal** hit then clears. Wind's Siege map is now `DISORIENT` (no longer shared with Electric `SHOCK`); `DAZE` renamed to `INSIGHT` across battle + Siege enums/UI. Cache pins: `game.js?v=224`, `adventure.js?v=52`.

Battle runtime adds stack storage on `CardInstance`, `ElementalAfflictionService` (inflict after HP damage; Burn ticks flat damage × stacks at owner Setup then clears), board serialization of `afflictions`, and Burn badge rendering in `game.js`. Only Burn is `battleEnabled` today; other rows are designed placeholders. Original prompt: Cards in siege mode should mimic their function in battle as close as possible. Example change the card fisher is a draw effect but in siege it's dealing damage to the caster. Review card effects and create siege variations. Connected allies effects all allies. Shields grant shield that last until the beginning of your next turn. Chain attacks attack enemies. Etc. Review and apply updates.

- August 3, 2026 Siege card-effect parity: Siege translated battle-table effect keys through `SiegeContentService.effectFor`, a short substring chain that recognised seven shapes and returned `Effect.DAMAGE` for everything else — so `draw`, `slow`, `destroy`, `player_damage` and `connected_allies_slow` all resolved as attacks, and because `targetFor` kept the card's board target, a `SELF`-targeted draw card (the reported "fisher" case) resolved as the caster damaging itself. The resolver now matches every registered `AbilityEffectKeys` value exactly and falls back to substring matching only for keys the live dashboard has invented but the registry hasn't caught up with. Four new Siege effects carry the keys that had nowhere to land: `DRAW` (pull up to 3 cards), `STUN` for `freeze` (the board's freeze skips a turn, so it stuns rather than merely slowing), `MAX_HP_BOOST` for `health_boost` (raises max HP for the battle and heals the same amount, rather than being flattened into a shield), and `EXECUTE` for `destroy` (an outright kill, capped to 25% of max HP against elites/Siegelords and floored at 3 AP so a 0-energy printing isn't a free kill each turn). `connected_allies_*` cards are authored as `SELF` on the board because they walk the source's notch links; Siege has no links, so the warband *is* the linked network and those cards now resolve at `ALLY_ALL`. Targeting also gained a polarity guard: a damaging effect can no longer be aimed at your own side and a healing/shielding/buffing effect can no longer be aimed at the enemy line, which is what permanently closes the fisher class of bug — even an unregistered key that falls back to `DAMAGE` gets retargeted at the enemy instead of the caster. Two behaviour corrections came with it: shields are now temporary, lapsing when the shielded side opens its next turn (matching `clearTempEffects` wiping `temporaryShield` at the end of the board's battle phase), tracked per-combatant as `shieldExpiryRound` with battle-start grants (knight Bulwark passive, carried items, ambush foes) pinned to expire on round 2 so they still cover the turn they were meant for; and `BUFF_ATK` now buffs only the Siegelings the card named instead of always sweeping the whole party, so a `SINGLE_ALLY` damage boost is single-ally and only the connected-allies version is party-wide. The played card leaves the hand before its effect resolves, so a draw card refills the slot it vacated rather than being blocked by its own presence, and the hand's `boostedValue` preview now reads the buff of the Siegeling that will actually swing rather than the party maximum. Client side, `adventure.js` gained faces, drag palettes and effect classes for the four new effects plus a `shieldExpired` playback beat, and "(all)" now appears on any card that sweeps its whole side rather than only on damage/heal/shield. `ABILITY_EFFECT_KEYS.md` gained a Siege-translation table. Cache pin advanced to `adventure.js?v=50`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/adventure.js` passes; full Maven suite green at 414 tests (2 new files: `SiegeCardEffectParityTest`, 10 tests pinning the key-to-effect and key-to-target mapping, the no-self-damage guard, shield expiry, `health_boost` max-HP behaviour, single-ally attack boost, draw-not-damage, and destroy's boss cap; `SiegeShieldWindowTest`, 2 tests driving a real run through `SiegeService.endTurn` to prove the lapse and its `shieldExpired` event reach the serialized battle the client reads). `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest`'s two `adventure.js?v=` pins were advanced to 50. A live end-to-end pass ran the real Spring Boot app: a STANDARD run was started through `/api/siege/run/new` with a warband deliberately chosen to carry the newly-mapped effects (Gymstone `health_boost`, Clawkid `freeze`), and `/api/siege/roster` now reports those moves as `MAX_HP_BOOST` and `STUN` instead of `SHIELD`/`SLOW`. Headless Chromium then resumed that exact run at 390x844 and 1920x1080 and rendered the hand as ["10 dmg (all)", "+4 max HP", "Stun", "6 dmg", "Stun", "Evolve!"] at both viewports — no raw enum names leaked onto a card face, no horizontal overflow, no page or console errors.

Merged as `498b4701`; Deploy run [30767676722](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/30767676722) green on both jobs — Cloud Run 21:21:41–21:24:34Z, Firebase Hosting published 21:25:44Z with Functions completing at 21:27:10Z. `/home`, `/cards` and `/shop` all serve `home.js?v=129` / `home.css?v=121`; `/play` still serves `style.css?v=218` / `game.js?v=222`. `/`, `/play`, `/siege`, `/home`, `/cards`, `/shop`, `/keep` all 200. `/api/cards/editor` returns `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true` both through Hosting and against Cloud Run directly; `/js/config.js` keeps `apiBaseUrl: ''`; `/api/game/options` 200 with 5 decks / 127 cards, and `/api/shop/packs` returns 9 packs, 5 daily card offers and 4 daily title offers — so the live shop has real content behind the loader.

Against those exact bytes, at 390x844 and 1920x1080: `/shop` renders `.panel-loading` with `role="status"`, `aria-busy="true"`, "Loading the shop…", a live spinner (`animationName: panel-spin`) and bar (`panel-loading-slide`) and a `Loading…` gold label while `/api/shop/packs` is held open, then flips to `aria-busy="false"` with real pack tiles; `/cards` does the same with "Loading your card binder…". No console or page errors.
- Note for the next deploy from a remote agent session: the live-site Playwright path is unavailable here. Downloading the deployed assets and serving them locally is the workable substitute and is strictly better than asserting from a hash alone — it exercises the shipped bytes.

Original prompt: When a player acces the cards or shop page and items have yet to load the page should show a loading bar and spinner saying loading the content

- August 2, 2026 The Shop now shows the same spinner + loading bar the Cards binder already had while its content is still in flight. Previously `/shop` painted its "No packs available — Pack groups will appear here once the catalog loads" empty card during the boot fetch, which reads like a dead shop rather than a load in progress; the gold total also rendered a confident `0 Siegecoins` before the signed-in progression snapshot arrived. `state.shopPacksLoading` starts true (the pack catalog is fetched on boot), is cleared by `applyShopPacksPayload` whichever way the attempt resolves, and is re-armed by `ensurePacksLoaded` before any refetch. `renderShop` returns the loader early when `shopDataLoading()` — either the pack catalog has not landed yet, or the player is signed in with no progression snapshot, since prices, Owned title badges and starter mode all key off it. Packs, the daily card rotation and the daily titles all arrive in the one `/api/shop/packs` payload, so a single loader covers all three rows. The synchronous `hydrateStaticCachesFromStorage()` pass already applies a cached pack payload before first paint, so returning visitors clear the flag before rendering and never see a flash. The Retry button on the pack-catalog error state now shows the loader while the retry is in flight instead of leaving the stale error on screen. The shared markup helper and its CSS moved from `binderLoading*` / `.binder-loading*` to `panelLoading*` / `.panel-loading*` — it is used by cards, decks and now the shop, so the binder-specific name had stopped being true. Cache pins: `home.js?v=129`, `home.css?v=121` (also bumped in `card-dashboard.html` and `arena-mobile-fixture.html`, which include the same stylesheet).
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/home.js`. Headless Chromium against the real `home.html` at 390x844 and 1920x1080, served by a local static server that rewrites `/shop` and `/cards` and holds `/api/shop/packs` and `/api/game/options` open for 4s: both routes render `.panel-loading` with `role="status"`, `aria-busy="true"`, the copy "Loading the shop…" / "Loading your card binder…", a live spinner (`animationName: panel-spin`) and bar (`panel-loading-slide`), and the head labels read "Loading…" / "Loading cards…" — then, once the stubs answer, `aria-busy` flips to `false` and the real pack and card tiles mount. Two further scenarios: a signed-in player with `/api/auth/me` held 4s keeps the shop loader and only then shows `4200 Siegecoins` plus its packs (proving the loader is gated on progression too, not just on packs); and a `{error: …}` pack payload lands on "Could not load packs" + Retry, where clicking Retry swaps straight back to the loader and then to the loaded pack tile. Captures in the session scratchpad.

Original prompt: merge and deploy (shade enemies)


- August 2, 2026 Production deploy of #622 (Siege foes as corrupted Siegelings). Merged as `443380bb`; Deploy run [30760074158](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/30760074158) green on both jobs — Cloud Run 17:59:04-18:03:13Z, Firebase Hosting + Functions 18:03:17-18:05:42Z. `/siege` serves `adventure.css?v=46` / `adventure.js?v=45`, the page carries the `#paper-siegling-shadow` `<defs>` block, and both deployed bundles are byte-identical to the merged repo files once CRLF is normalised (CSS 135469 vs 137739 bytes, JS 180125 vs 184081 — identical after normalisation). `/`, `/play`, `/siege`, `/home`, `/keep` all 200; `/api/cards/editor` returns `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true`.
- **The shade path was exercised against production, not inferred from the bundle.** The live roster reports **37 of 37** Siegelings carrying uploaded art, so the fallback branch is not what production takes. A real guest expedition was started (`POST /api/siege/run/new`, Squire Bob + Applehead/Squirebud — note the body field is `sieglingIds`, and a non-starter pick like Cacty is correctly rejected with "is locked — find them on the expedition path first") and its row-0 battle entered: the foe came back as **`name: "Shade of Bearby"`, `shadeOf: "Bearby"`**, with a Firebase Storage `artUrl` that itself resolves `200 image/png` (2.1 MB). Its shape was `maxHp 30, speed 8, Strike 5` — exactly `OPENING_FIGHT_HP` / `OPENING_FIGHT_SPEED` / `OPENING_FIGHT_DAMAGE`, so the pinned opener survived the change end to end on the deployed instance.
- Process note worth keeping: this change was verified twice because the `siege-fullscreen-desktop` worktree was repointed to another branch mid-run by a concurrent session, which stashed the work (`stash@{0}`) and reset the tree underneath a running `mvn test` — producing a confusing "the cache-bust pins failed again" result after they had already been fixed. The work was recovered into its own worktree (`siege-dark-enemies`) and re-verified from scratch. Both Playwright harnesses had hardcoded that worktree's path and would have silently verified a different checkout; they now derive the repo root from their own location.

Original prompt: enemy siegelings in siege should appear as dark colored Siegeling overlay cutouts, but they should be actual creatures from the game with their overpowered dark siegeling moves in tact, copy the dark look from The Keep (screenshot of a "Mire Beast" rendering as a plain water droplet glyph)

- August 2, 2026 Siege foes are now **corrupted Siegelings from the real card catalog**, drawn as the violet-black cutout Akhar's raiders wear in the Keep. They used to be invented silhouettes — a name from `ENEMY_NAMES_BY_ELEMENT` ("Mire Beast", "Gale Shrike") and `artUrl = null`, so `renderSpriteLine` fell through to the `sp-art-fallback` element glyph and every foe in a run was the same emoji droplet at 56px. `SiegeContentService#shadeCard` now picks a catalog Siegeling for each foe, mirroring `KeepService#akharsFrontRaiderPool`: only cards with uploaded art qualify, the search prefers the element the run's palette rolled and widens to any element before giving up, and it walks down evolution stages from a per-encounter floor (BATTLE 1, BATTLE at floor ≥5 and ELITE 2, BOSS 3) so the silhouette escalates with the fight. A catalog with no uploaded art falls back to the old themed names, which is exactly what the classpath catalog does — art only ever arrives from the dashboard, so local/test runs are unchanged.
- **Nothing about the fight changed.** HP, speed, ability count and damage still come from the encounter scaling in `generateEnemies`; the shade supplies name, art and nothing else, so the overpowered enemy movesets and the whole difficulty curve are untouched — that parity is pinned by a test rather than asserted in prose, and the test earned its keep: picking a shade consumes a roll only when the catalog has art to offer, so drawing it from the encounter's own `Random` let **the first foe's appearance shift the second foe's HP, speed and damage**. Shades now draw from a separate stream seeded once per encounter (`new Random(rng.nextLong())`, a draw that happens whatever the catalog holds), which makes the independence structural instead of coincidental. The fallback themed name is also rolled unconditionally even when a shade replaces it, for the same reason. Enemies deliberately do **not** get a `sourceCardId`: that field drives `evoStage` (and the client's 1.5×-per-stage sprite scaling) and evolution lookups, so setting it would silently resize foes and open them to evolution paths they should not have.
- `shadeCard` filters on **art before stage**, which is a performance decision rather than a stylistic one: `sieglingsAtStage` calls `stageOf` for every card in the catalog, and `stageOf` walks the evolution chain through `findAnySiegling`, which rescans `getDeckBuilderCatalog()` (a full rebuild + copy) each time. Calling it once per stage per element pass — up to six times per foe, on every battle entry — would have been the expensive way to ask a cheap question. One walk collects the art-bearing cards, and only those pay for `stageOf`; a catalog with no uploaded art costs a single walk and no `stageOf` at all.
- Bosses are the one exception: they keep their own titles (Squire Bram, Ser Malachar, Siegelord Vareth — named antagonists, not corrupted Siegelings) but still take a stage-3 cutout, because a boss was otherwise the one fight in the run still rendering as a bare element glyph.
- The dark look is copied from the Keep rather than reinvented: `#paper-siegling-shadow` is lifted verbatim from `keep.html` into `adventure.html` (a filter resolves by document-local id and the two pages share no markup, so it is duplicated on purpose — retune both together), and `.sprite.enemy .sp-art img` mirrors `keep.css`'s `.front-raiders i .raider-art img`, drop-shadow fallback chain and `@supports` override included. The artless glyph fallback gets a matching violet crush so it still reads as a shade. One addition the Keep did not need: a foe's name is "Shade of X", which at a 92px phone plate truncated every enemy to "Shade of Shell…" — so `Combatant.shadeOf` carries the bare creature name and the plate renders a compact `SHADE` badge plus the readable name, with the badge dropped entirely on phone-sized plates where the cutout already carries the message. `shadeOf` is serialized in both combatant serializers **and** restored in `restoreCombatant`, or a run resumed mid-battle would come back with the art but no badge. Cache bumps: `adventure.css?v=46`, `adventure.js?v=45`.
- Verification: new `SiegeShadeEnemyTest` (6 cases, all green) injects a stub catalog with art through `ReflectionTestUtils` — the classpath catalog has none — and asserts foes carry real card art with `Shade of` names, that the opening fight does too, that bosses keep their title while still getting art, that an artless catalog falls back to the themed names with a null `artUrl`, that 20 seeded runs meet a varied cast rather than one repeated silhouette, and — the load-bearing one — that the encounter shape (foe count, total HP, top speed, ability count, top damage) is **identical** across 8 seeds with and without shades. Full Maven suite green. Frontend proved by pixel rather than by eye: a headless-Chromium harness renders the same bright test art on an ally and a foe at 1920x1080 and 844x390, and asserts the foe's computed filter is `url("#paper-siegling-shadow")` while the ally's is `none`, the foe is still mirrored, the `<defs>` carrier occupies a 0x0 box and the page does not scroll, no console/page errors (which is also the syntax gate on `adventure.js` — no node on this machine), and the sampled art pixels come back `rgb(37,18,74)` for the foe against `rgb(210,179,128)` for the ally — a 5.6× luminance drop, hue-shifted to violet — with the enemy plate name no longer overflowing at either size. Captures in `output/web-game/siege-shade-enemies/`.
Original prompt: fix the concurrency issue (deploy record)

- August 2, 2026 The deploy serialization from #620 is live and was confirmed **behaviourally, on the real workflow**, not just by reading the YAML. #620 merged as `eb6d816a` and its own push-triggered run [30754758150](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/30754758150) went green (both jobs). While that run was `in_progress`, a second deploy was deliberately launched against the same ref (`gh workflow run deploy.yml --ref main`, run [30754777269](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/30754777269)) — exactly the collision that produced the #604/#605 split-surface deploy. It reported status **`pending` with 0 jobs started**, which is the concurrency-blocked state (a run merely waiting for a runner reports `queued` and still holds jobs), so nothing of it touched Cloud Run or Hosting. When run 30754758150 completed, the blocked run flipped to `in_progress` on its own and finished green (Cloud Run 15:43:30Z, Firebase 15:47:32Z). Before the fix both would have deployed simultaneously.
- Site re-verified after both runs settled: `/siege` still serves `adventure.css?v=45`; `/`, `/play`, `/siege`, `/home`, `/keep` all 200; `/api/cards/editor` returns `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true`. The second run was an idempotent redeploy of the same commit, so no content changed between them.

Original prompt: fix the concurrency issue

- August 2, 2026 `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` now serializes deploys. It had no `concurrency` block, so two pushes to `main` landing minutes apart each deployed Cloud Run and Firebase Hosting independently and whichever job finished last won **per surface** — which is how #604/#605 left a new frontend talking to an old API with two green workflow runs and no signal that anything was wrong. The block is `group: deploy-production`, `cancel-in-progress: false`, declared at **workflow level**: a job-level group would still allow run B's Cloud Run job to overlap run A's Hosting job, which is the same race in a smaller window. `cancel-in-progress` stays false because killing a half-finished deploy is worse than queueing behind it; GitHub still cancels a *pending* run when a newer one joins the group, so a burst of pushes deploys the first and then the newest and never lands an older commit last. The group is a fixed string rather than `${{ github.ref }}` so a `workflow_dispatch` redeploy queues behind a push deploy — both target the same production project, whatever ref they were launched from. This was the standing recommendation recorded in the July 31 #604/#605 recovery entry below; nothing else in the workflow changed.
- Verification: the workflow parses (`yaml.safe_load`) with `concurrency` at top level as `{group: deploy-production, cancel-in-progress: False}`, no job-level `concurrency` key competing with it, and `deploy-firebase` still `needs: deploy-cloud-run`. Behavioural confirmation is in the deploy record entry above/next — a second run dispatched while the merge's own deploy was in flight has to sit queued rather than start.

Original prompt: Desktop and tablet formats should use the mobile landscape fullscreen views instead of the battle arena view (screenshots of the siege battle in phone-landscape fullscreen vs the desktop boxed arena)

- August 2, 2026 The Siege battle screen's full-bleed arena is now the layout for **every** landscape viewport, not just landscape phones. It was gated to `(orientation: landscape) and (max-width: 979px) and (max-height: 600px)`; desktop and tablet-landscape fell through to the boxed `.battle-stage` inside the 1120px `.siege-app` reading column, so a 1920px screen played the fight in a centred box with the hand fan stacked under it. The gate is now plain `(orientation: landscape)`, and the sizes that were hardcoded for a 390px-tall phone became custom properties on `body[data-screen="battleScreen"]` — `--arena-chrome-top`, `--arena-sprite-w`, `--arena-sprite-art`, `--arena-name`, `--arena-plate-w`, `--arena-log-w`, `--arena-hand-h`, `--playcard-w` — so a second block, `(orientation: landscape) and (min-height: 601px)`, scales the same structure up for tablet/desktop by overriding variables rather than duplicating rules. Height, not width, is the split: a short wide desktop window keeps the compact phone values, and 1024x768 upward gets the roomy ones. `.siege-app` drops `max-width` and its gutters on the battle screen (otherwise "edge to edge" stopped 400px short of each edge), `.battle-top`'s right edge is derived from `--arena-log-w` instead of a hardcoded 160px, and on tall viewports the stage takes a `6vw` inline inset so the two formations don't sit ~1700px apart at the extreme edges, plus the card rules text (`.pc-desc`, hidden on phones) comes back. Portrait — phone or tablet — is untouched and keeps the stacked column. The phone-only map and page-scrolling-screen compaction stayed behind the original narrow+short query, since tablets and desktops have the height to render those normally; `adventure.js`'s `isPhoneLandscape()` map transposition is deliberately unchanged for the same reason. `adventure.css?v=45` in `adventure.html`.
- Verification: headless Chromium (Playwright, `python -m http.server` over `src/main/resources/static`, `fetch` stubbed so boot idles on the loading screen) with an injected mock battle DOM, at **7 viewports** — 1920x1080, 1366x768, 1180x820, 1024x768, 844x390, 390x844, 820x1180. All four landscape desktop/tablet sizes and the phone-landscape regression assert `battle-stage` is `position: absolute` and within 40px of covering the viewport in both axes; knight plate, speed track, ledger pill, hand fan, HUD and End Turn all inside the viewport; plate and track clear of the ledger pill; hand fan below the plate; End Turn below the hint; and no page scroll in either axis. Both portrait viewports assert the opposite — stage still static-flow and `.siege-app` still capped at 1120px — so portrait is provably unchanged. Screenshots per viewport in `output/web-game/siege-desktop-landscape/` were visually inspected: desktop and iPad landscape render the plate/track/ledger band across the top, allies left, foes right, centred hand fan, AP column bottom-left, End Turn bottom-right, matching the phone landscape composition at scale.
