Original prompt: Create a dashboard section under shop for activating and deactivating available packs. I don't see the water packs for draw? (screenshots of the dashboard Shop Prices panel and the hub shop's "Elemental and type pulls" row)

- August 1, 2026 The card dashboard's **Shop** page now has a second Store panel, **Pack Availability**, that switches individual shop packs on and off. Packs were defined entirely in code (`PackCatalogService#listPacks`) with a hardcoded `active: true`, so the only way to remove one from the shop was to deactivate its whole element in the Live Elements roster — which takes the element out of the deck builder, matchmaking, and every other pack that uses it. The new `PackAvailabilityCatalogService` stores per-pack toggles (`{"packs":[{"id":"pack_water","active":false}]}`) in Firestore `appConfig/packAvailability`, falling back to `resources/cards/pack-availability.json`, following the same load/save/cache shape as `ShopPriceCatalogService`. A pack with no stored row stays active, so an absent or malformed document can never take the shop offline. `listPacks()` now resolves each pack's `active` flag through that service and a new `listAvailablePacks()` filters to the live ones: `serializePacks()` (the `/api/shop/packs` and progression payloads) drops deactivated packs entirely, while `serializeAllPacks()` and `findPack()` keep them so the dashboard can switch them back on and `openPack` still answers "That pack is not currently available." rather than "Pack not found.". Admin endpoints are `GET/POST /api/shop/packs/availability` behind the same `X-Card-Editor-Token` guard `ShopPriceController` uses; the POST refuses a toggle that would leave zero active packs or zero starter-eligible packs, since the shop cannot render an empty list and a new account cannot finish onboarding without a starter pick. New files: `PackAvailabilityCatalogService`, `PackAvailabilityController`, `static/js/pack-admin.js`; `card-dashboard.html` gained the panel plus a `pack-availability` info topic (`card-dashboard.css?v=38`, `dashboard-info.js?v=7`). `#shopWorkspace` is now a single full-width column instead of inheriting the three-column browser/editor/preview grid, which had been squeezing the 640px-min price table into a 320px track.

- **The missing Water packs were not a backend fault — the phone was showing a day-old cached list.** `/api/shop/packs` in production returns `pack_water` and `pack_stormtide` today, and all five live elements (FIRE, ICE, WATER, EARTH, WIND) are active in the roster. The hub caches that payload in `localStorage` under `sieglingsHomeCache:shopPacks`, and `fetchCachedJson` returns a cache that is still inside its TTL **without revalidating** — the pack catalog was sharing `STATIC_CACHE_TTL_MS`, a full **24 hours**. So any window where Water was toggled off (or the catalog was fetched before the element went live) sticks to the device for a day, which is exactly what the screenshot shows: Fire, Ice, then Earth. That TTL would also have made the new activation toggle look broken, so packs now use their own `PACK_CACHE_TTL_MS` of 10 minutes (`home.js?v=127`) — still instant on repaint, but a dashboard toggle lands within the same session instead of the next day.

- Verification: full Maven suite green (exit 0), including a new `PackAvailabilityCatalogServiceTest` (unset packs read active; one deactivation leaves others alone and reverses cleanly; ids match case/whitespace-insensitively; blank/null ids rejected; an unknown id resolves active so a stale row can never hide a pack) and a new `PackCatalogServiceTest` case asserting a deactivated pack leaves `listAvailablePacks`/`serializePacks`, stays in `findPack`/`serializeAllPacks` with `active:false`, makes `openPack` throw the "not currently available" message, and comes back on re-activation. `node --check` clean on `pack-admin.js`, `dashboard-info.js`, and `home.js`. A headless-Chromium harness against the static bundle with a stubbed availability API passed at **390x844 and 1920x1080**: all four pack rows render, the deactivated row carries `is-inactive` with an unchecked box labelled "Off", the active rows read "Active", the page never scrolls horizontally, and clicking a toggle POSTs `{packId, active:false}` and re-renders from the response (a rejected save snaps the checkbox back rather than showing a state the server refused). Desktop captures of the two stacked Store panels were visually inspected. A second harness pinned the cache change against the real `home.js`: a 30-minute-old `shopPacks` entry triggers exactly one refetch and the fresh catalog is written back, while a 1-minute-old entry triggers none.

Original prompt: Only two cards display in the deck list preview of an [expedition] (screenshot of the hub deck preview showing DECKLIST + a near-empty CARD GALLERY)

- August 1, 2026 The hub's deck preview modal (`#deckPreviewModal` on `home.html`, filled by `showDeckPreview` in `home.js`) was building **every** card tile correctly — 16 tiles for a 40-card Frostmarch preview — but only the two whose art falls back to the legacy `binder-card-shell` markup had any height. The other 14 measured **0px tall**, which is why the gallery showed a stray yellow bar (the `preview-focused` outline on a collapsed tile), a large dead gap, and then exactly two readable cards. Cause is track sizing, not rendering: `.deck-preview-grid` is the one `.card-grid` in the app that is a scroll container with a *definite* height (`flex: 1 1 auto` inside the fixed-height `.deck-preview-panel` column, plus `overflow-y: auto`), so its `auto` rows resolve to each tile's **min-content** contribution rather than max-content. A framed card tile carries its whole height through `aspect-ratio: var(--card-aspect-ratio)` on `.mulligan-card-slot`, whose width is a percentage and whose innards (`.hand-notches`, `.hand-card-shell`) are absolutely positioned — so its min-content contribution is zero and the row collapses. The binder at `/cards` uses the same tiles and the same `.card-grid` but sits in a page-scrolled container with an auto height, which is why it never showed the bug and why this looked like a data problem rather than a layout one. Fix is two declarations on `.deck-preview-grid`: `grid-auto-rows: max-content` and `align-content: start`, mirroring the pattern `.deck-preview-stack` already uses directly above it. `home.css` advanced to `?v=120` in `home.html`, `card-dashboard.html`, and `arena-mobile-fixture.html`.
- Desktop was hit by the same rule and had been reading as "ugly" rather than "broken": at 1920x1080 the framed rows were squashed to an aspect of **0.32** (name banner only, art clipped) except the one row that happened to contain a shell tile. Both surfaces now render at **1.40** — exactly the intended 7/5 — so the two viewports agree for the first time.
- Verification: reproduced first against a locally running `spring-boot:run` at 390x844 with a headless-Chromium harness driving the real flow (`/decks` → click `[data-preview-deck="deck_ice"]` → measure `#deckPreviewGrid`), which reported `14/14` framed tiles at height 0 and `gridTemplateRows: 0px 0px 0px 0px 0px 0px 318px 0px`. The cause was isolated by toggling candidate properties live in the page — `align-content: start` and forcing `height: auto` on the slot both changed nothing, while `grid-auto-rows: max-content` alone restored all 14 (rows `212.094px`, scrollHeight 417 → 1929), confirming the row-sizing diagnosis rather than an art or markup fault. Post-fix the same harness reports **0 collapsed tiles** on Frostmarch (16 tiles) and the Fire deck (17 tiles) at 390x844 and on Frostmarch at 1920x1080, framed aspect `1.40`, the grid still scrollable, and `.deck-preview-panel` still inside the viewport at both sizes. Screenshots at the top and bottom of the gallery scroll were inspected at both viewports: the first cards render full-size and the last two (Zero Hour, Shatter Seal) are reachable. CSS-only change scoped to a selector used by exactly one element — `grep` confirms `.card-grid` appears on only `#allCardGrid` and `#deckPreviewGrid`, and `mobile-overlap-fixture.html` contains no `.deck-preview-*` markup, so the layout guard's surface is untouched (it could not be executed here: `tests/mobile-overlap/` needs `@playwright/test`, which is not installed in this environment — only the global `playwright` package is).
- Worth flagging, not changed here because it is outside the report: on a phone the gallery only gets **271px** of height because `.deck-preview-stack` is allowed `min(42vh, 320px)` above it, so roughly one card row is visible at a time even now that the rows are correct. Rebalancing that split is a design call.

Original prompt: Update so the first fight is always the same difficulty level, afterwards fights scale based on that calculation then merge and deploy

- July 31, 2026 The Siege expedition's **opening fight is now a fixed yardstick**. Every encounter used to run through `SiegeContentService#generateEnemies`, whose `partyMul = 0.48 + 0.175 * partySize` meant the very first battle moved with the size of the warband you brought: a lone Siegeling met a ~26-31 HP foe while a Marshal's pair met a ~32-40 HP one, on top of a per-foe `rng.nextInt(10)` HP roll and a 6-13 speed roll. So no two runs started at the same difficulty, and the Marshal change shipped earlier today made its opener measurably harder than a solo start. The first battle now comes from a new `generateOpeningEnemies(rng, palette)`: one foe, `OPENING_FIGHT_HP` 30, `OPENING_FIGHT_DAMAGE` 5, `OPENING_FIGHT_SPEED` 8, a single Strike — all pinned in `SiegeTuning`. Element and enemy name still roll, so the fight still looks different run to run; nothing that decides how hard it is does.
- Everything after the opener is untouched and still scales off warband size and depth through `generateEnemies` — the request was to anchor the curve's first point, not to flatten the curve. The constants were chosen to sit mid-band of what a solo warband already rolled at floor 1, so a solo opening plays as it always has (its HP lands at the top of the old 26-31 spread, ~1.5 HP above the old average) and a Marshal's opening comes down to meet it.
- The "is this the first fight" test is `run.getEnemiesDefeated() == 0` plus `battleType == BATTLE`, deliberately reusing existing run state rather than adding a field: map row 0 is always a BATTLE node and must be cleared before any event can ambush you, so no fight can be won before the opener and none can precede it. Reusing the existing counter also means **old checkpoints restore unchanged** — no snapshot format change. Battlegrounds opts out explicitly, since scaling enemies to extracted veteran squads is that mode's entire premise.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, **387/387**, including a new `SiegeOpeningFightTest` (6 cases): a real run's first battle matches the pinned shape exactly; eight consecutive runs produce one and only one opener shape; trimming a run's party to a single Siegeling before the fight generates leaves the opener identical, which is the party-size independence the request asked for; twelve post-opener fights (same node, `enemiesDefeated` set to 1) come back varied rather than pinned, proving the scaling path resumes; `generateEnemies` still grows with warband size (solo vs trio HP); and elites/bosses stay above the opener, so only the opening battle is pinned. Backend-only change — no static assets touched, so no `?v=` bumps.

Original prompt: Merge and deploy (recovery verification)

- July 31, 2026 The backend half of #604 is now genuinely live. #606 (`9d4e02cb`) landed alone on `main` and its Deploy run rebuilt the tip with no competing run; the Cloud Run revision carrying traffic serves the new build about 4.5 minutes after the merge. `/api/siege/roster` now returns `startingParty` on **all 11** knight rows — `2` for both MARSHAL knights (Squire Bob, High Cleric Catherine) and `1` for the other nine — identically through Firebase Hosting and against the Cloud Run URL directly, with the reworded passive text ("Musters an extra Siegeling: choose 2 starting Siegelings instead of 1.") coming from the server rather than the client.
- End-to-end behaviour was exercised against production rather than inferred from the payload: `POST /api/siege/run/new` with Squire Bob and **one** Siegeling is rejected with "Choose exactly 2 Siegelings — more will join along the way.", the same call with the **same id twice** is rejected with "Each Siegeling can only join the warband once.", and with **two distinct** starters it returns a live run whose party is exactly `[Applehead, Squire Bud]` in pick order under knight Squire Bob. That covers both new server-side guards and the muster itself on the deployed instance.
- Full checklist re-run after the recovery, all passing: `adventure.html` serves `adventure.css?v=44` / `adventure.js?v=44`; `card-dashboard.html` serves `dashboard-info.js?v=6` / `siege-class-admin.js?v=3`; `/api/cards/editor` returns `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true` through both Hosting and Cloud Run; `js/config.js` keeps `apiBaseUrl: ''`; `/api/game/options` returns 200 with 4 decks; `/play`, `/siege`, `/home`, `/keep` all 200. The deployed `js/adventure.js?v=44` and `css/adventure.css?v=44` were downloaded and are **byte-identical** to the merged repo files (`diff -q` clean), and the live JS carries `startingPartyNeed` and the `knight-group` availability bands.
- Standing recommendation from the #604/#605 race: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` has no `concurrency` block, so two `main` pushes landing minutes apart can each deploy Cloud Run and Hosting independently, and whichever job finishes last wins per surface — which is how a new frontend ended up talking to an old API with two green workflow runs. A `concurrency: {group: deploy, cancel-in-progress: false}` block would serialize them. Not done here because it is outside what was asked; it is the obvious follow-up.

Original prompt: Merge and deploy

- July 31, 2026 Production deploy of #604 (SiegeKnight availability sort + Marshal two-starter muster). Merged as `334d5249`; Deploy run [30660507125](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/30660507125) completed green on both jobs — Cloud Run 19:48:45-19:52:55Z, Firebase Hosting + Functions 19:53:05-19:55:14Z. Frontend verified live: `adventure.html` serves `adventure.css?v=44` / `adventure.js?v=44`, `card-dashboard.html` serves `dashboard-info.js?v=6` / `siege-class-admin.js?v=3`, `js/config.js` keeps `apiBaseUrl: ''`, `/api/cards/editor` returns `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true` through both Hosting and the Cloud Run URL directly, `/api/game/options` returns 200 with 4 decks, and `/play`, `/siege`, `/home` all 200.
- **The backend half did not take, and the deploy log could not have told us.** Both Cloud Run jobs reported success, but the live roster — through Hosting *and* against the Cloud Run URL directly — carried no `startingParty` key on any of its 11 knights, which is the field this change adds. The cause was a race between two deploy runs: PR #605 (a progress.md-only commit at `9357dd07`, which predates this change) merged three seconds before #604, and its Cloud Run job finished at **19:52:55Z** against #604's **19:52:52Z**. Last writer wins on a Cloud Run service, so the revision holding traffic was built from the *older* commit. Hosting escaped the same fate only by luck of ordering — #604's Firebase job finished at 19:55:14Z, after #605's 19:55:07Z — which is exactly why the site served new assets against an old API. Neither run's log shows anything wrong; only probing the live API for a field the new build introduces catches it.
- The lesson for future deploys: **never let two `main` pushes deploy concurrently**, and when they do, verify the backend by a payload probe rather than by the workflow's own conclusion. A green Cloud Run job proves *a* revision shipped, not that it shipped *yours*. `rerun_workflow_run` and `workflow_dispatch` are both 403 for this integration, so the recovery is a fresh commit on `main` — this entry — landing alone so its Deploy run has no competitor.
- Verification of the recovery is recorded in the next entry once this run completes.

Original prompt: Two things, siegeknights that are available But non purchases should appear near the top of the selection list so you can identify them quickly. Marshall siegeknights should select two starting Siegelings instead of 1

- July 31, 2026 The Siege setup flow now sorts SiegeKnights by how usable they actually are and lets a Marshal knight muster its second Siegeling up front. **Knight list**: `renderKnightStep()` in `adventure.js` previously sorted `selectable` first and then everything else alphabetically, so a knight you own in your collection and could unlock for Siegecoins right now sat buried among knights whose card you do not own at all. Knights are now bucketed into three availability bands — *Ready to deploy* (free starters first, then Siegecoin-unlocked), *Unlock with Siegecoins* (affordable first, then by ascending cost), and *Locked* — each introduced by a `.knight-group` header that spans the grid (`grid-column: 1 / -1`) so the band you can actually play from is always the first thing on screen.
- **Marshal**: the class passive used to be effectively cosmetic. `continueRun` called `joinStagedRecruit` after every battle win regardless of class; the Marshal branch only changed the flavour string, so the "extra Siegeling" was the same recruit every knight already got. The muster moved to warband assembly instead: `SiegeContentService.startingPartySize(knight)` returns `PARTY_SIZE + 1` (capped at `PARTY_MAX`) for a MARSHAL knight, the roster payload carries it per knight as `startingParty`, and `newRun` resolves the knight *before* validating the party so it can require exactly that many starters — plus a new distinct-id guard, since picking two now makes duplicate submissions possible. The frontend reads the same field: `startingPartyNeed()` drives the pick cap, the footer counter, the Begin Expedition gate, and the warband subtitle ("Select 2 starter siegelings — Squire Bob musters an extra one"), and switching to a smaller-muster knight trims the over-full pick list rather than leaving an unstartable run. Squire Bob — the default free starter — hashes to MARSHAL, so most players get the two-Siegeling opening. Adventure pins advanced to `adventure.css?v=44` / `adventure.js?v=44`; the dashboard's class documentation and `siege-class-admin` blurb were reworded to match (`dashboard-info.js?v=6`, `siege-class-admin.js?v=3`).
- Verification: full Maven suite green, **381/381**, including a new `SiegeMarshalStartTest` (Marshal starts at 2 and non-Marshal at 1; a Marshal run fields both chosen Siegelings in pick order; a short *or* duplicated warband is rejected; every roster row advertises `startingParty`). Five existing Siege tests assumed a one-Siegeling start and used Squire Bob — the only knight a signed-out run may select — so they now assemble their warband through a shared `SiegeStarterTestSupport` helper that fills to the knight's muster, with a filtered variant so `SiegeSigilRewardTest`'s "no evolution path" case still fields a warband where *every* member is terminal. The two static-asset guards pinning `adventure.css?v=43`/`adventure.js?v=43` were advanced to 44. A headless-Chromium harness against a stubbed roster passed **24/24** at 390x844 and 1920x1080: band order and membership (affordable unlock above unaffordable, purchasable above unowned), the knight summary advertising "warband of 2", the warband subtitle, 1-of-2 leaving Begin disabled, 2-of-2 arming it, a third pick refused with a toast, and switching to a non-Marshal knight dropping cleanly back to 1/1. The ~20px horizontal overflow the harness reports at 390px was measured on the pre-change tree as well (it comes from `.setup-footer`) — pre-existing, not introduced here, and marginally smaller after this change. Phone captures of both steps were visually inspected.

Original prompt: Make the dark siege lings dark colored siegeling cutouts available from the game, randomize them to not be standard ghost but recolored paper cutout overlay siegelings

- July 31, 2026 Akhar's raiders are now **recoloured Siegelings from the real card catalog** instead of one hard-coded ghost SVG repeated four times. `KeepService.akharsFrontRaiderPool()` samples up to 12 Siegelings that have uploaded art from `cardDefinitionService.getDeckBuilderCatalog()` — the whole catalog, not the player's collection, since Akhar's forces are not drawn from the keeper's own residents — shuffles them with the service `Random`, and exposes them on the snapshot as `akharsFront.raiders` (`id`, `name` as `Shade of <Siegeling>`, `element`, `artUrl`). Cards with no art are skipped, and a catalog with none yields an empty pool so the client keeps its built-in shade rather than rendering broken images.
- A pool is sent rather than a fixed cast because raiders respawn continuously while the player watches: `pickFrontRaiderShade()` draws per spawn and excludes shades already marching, so the four raiders on screen are always four different Siegelings and the wall keeps facing new silhouettes across a visit. The client swaps a raider's art only when its shade actually changes, so the march animation is never restarted by rewriting identical markup, and `render_game_to_text` reports each enemy's `shade`/`shadeId` plus the pool size.
- Visual treatment: a second SVG filter, `#paper-siegling-shadow`, mirrors the existing `#paper-siegling-cutout` structure — same alpha-driven die-cut rim geometry — but floods the rim violet (`#5a3a8f`) over a near-black keyline and runs the art through a true-luminance colour matrix (0.2126/0.7152/0.0722) ramped onto a violet-black gradient, so a Siegeling's own shading survives while its hue does not. The coefficients are scaled so even white art lands at rgb(.19,.09,.38): the first attempt weighted R+G+B equally at 0.20 and bright cards came out mid-violet rather than dark, which the desktop capture caught. A blurred violet outer glow separates a raider from the night sky. A `filter:` chain provides the same read where SVG filters are unsupported, exactly as `.is-paper-cutout` already does. Keep pins advanced to `keep.css?v=48` / `keep.js?v=47`.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, **376/376**, including a new `KeepServiceTest#raiderShadesComeFromRealSiegelingArtAndAreRandomised` covering the empty-pool case for an art-less catalog, the 12-card cap against a 30-card catalog, exclusion of art-less cards, the `Shade of` naming, uniqueness within a pool, and — the point of the feature — that two different seeds produce different twelve. The four-viewport harness rose to **638/638 with zero page errors**, with six new assertions per tier/viewport: every raider carries real card art, the built-in ghost is replaced rather than layered under it, the art is always recoloured (computed `filter` is never `none`), raiders are named as shades, the four on screen are distinct, and `render_game_to_text` names each one. That distinctness assertion caught a real bug: `initializeFrontCombat` built the raiders with `.map()`, so each spawn's dedupe read the *previous* enemies array — the array is only assigned once the map ends — and duplicate shades slipped through on entry. It now pushes incrementally. Mobile and desktop captures at tiers 1, 3, and 4 visually inspected: four distinct violet-black Siegeling cutouts approach the wall, clearly the same die-cut language as the defenders and clearly corrupted.

Original prompt: I want the siege lings to be placed in the interior side of the wall not the far side they should be placed in front of the wall SVG

- July 31, 2026 Corrected the rampart defenders' side of the wall. The previous pass put them *behind* the rampart (`z-index: 4`, under the wall's 5) so merlons occluded their bodies and only heads cleared the parapet — which read as standing on Akhar's side of the fortification, peering over, rather than defending it. They are now back in front of the wall SVG (`z-index: 7`) but stay at wall level rather than the original floating height: `bottom: 31%` in the front view and `27%` on the Keep map, which lands their feet on the walkway line — the crenel floor at viewBox y=64, ~31% of the front-view stage and ~27% of the map stage. Full-bodied Siegelings now stand on the interior walk with the battlements behind them. Muzzle points in `FRONT_DEFENDER_STARTS` moved back up to y≈49-52% to track the standing body instead of the crenel opening.
- The tier-4 gilt crown introduced in the previous pass stays: replacing the full-width `.wall-coping` bar with a per-merlon path generated inside the wall SVG was correct independently of which side the defenders stand on, and now simply renders behind them.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 375/375. Four-viewport harness **542/542 with zero page errors** at 390x844, 844x390, 1920x1080, and 2560x1080 across all four tiers plus the gate-locked case. The four defender guards were inverted to match: defenders must now compute a *higher* `z-index` than the rampart, must sit low enough that their box bottom passes the merlon-top line, must have their feet planted within 10px of the walkway line, and must still rise clear of the merlons. The walkway assertion caught a real 6px overshoot on the first attempt at `bottom: 30%`/`26%`, which is why the shipped values are 31%/27% rather than round numbers. Mobile and desktop captures at tiers 1, 3, and 4 visually inspected against the reported screenshot. Pins stay at `keep.css?v=47` / `keep.js?v=46` — those numbers were minted in the previous commit and have never been published, so they carry this content instead of needing another bump.

Original prompt: Move the singling cutouts down at the wall so they are sort of behind the rampart cutouts shooting between the slots

- July 31, 2026 Akhar's Front defenders now **man** the wall instead of hovering over it. They were pinned at `bottom: 45%` with `z-index: 7` — above the merlon line (~41% of the front-view stage) and drawn in front of the whole rampart, so the Siegeling cutouts floated as free-standing cards over the battlements. They now sit at `bottom: 25%` with `z-index: 4`, below the rampart's `z-index: 5`: the merlons occlude their lower body, their torsos show through the crenel gaps, and their heads and shoulders clear the parapet. The map-view rule moved the same way (`bottom: 22%`, z 4) since that wall sits slightly lower in its box. Projectile muzzle points in `FRONT_DEFENDER_STARTS` dropped from y≈55-58% to y≈43-46% so shots leave at the crenel the defender is firing through rather than starting inside the stonework.
- The tier-4 gilt trim had to change with it. `.wall-coping` was a single full-width bar at `bottom: 43%`, floating *above* the merlon tops and — now that defenders stand in the gaps — ruling a gold line straight across their necks. It is deleted. In its place `renderFrontRampart()` emits a second generated path, `.wall-crown`, tracing the top edge of each merlon (`M{x} 37h32` per crenellation) alongside the wall body it already generates. Because the crown lives inside the wall SVG it inherits the wall's stacking position, so it caps the stonework, never crosses a defender, and scales with the same regenerated grid at any width. Butt caps rather than round ones keep the gilt exactly the width of the merlon it sits on. Keep pins advanced to `keep.css?v=47` / `keep.js?v=46`.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 375/375. The four-viewport harness grew to **542/542 assertions with zero page errors** at 390x844, 844x390, 1920x1080, and 2560x1080 across all four tiers plus the gate-locked case. Four new guards lock the behaviour in: defenders must compute a lower `z-index` than the rampart, every defender box must straddle the merlon-top line (bottom below it, top above it), none may sit entirely below the crenel floor, and each must still clear the merlons by at least 20px so a posted Siegeling can never be swallowed by the wall. The tier-dressing assertions were rewritten off the removed `.wall-coping` onto the crown, checking that only tier 4 strokes it and that it carries exactly one cap per merlon at whatever width the wall was generated for. The harness also gained representative card art (data-URI sprites) because its old initials placeholder renders at the box's bottom edge and would have hidden the very occlusion being tested — the earlier passes were geometrically correct but visually unrepresentative. Mobile and desktop captures at tiers 1, 3, and 4 were visually inspected against the reported screenshot.

Original prompt: Deploy

- July 31, 2026 Production deploy of #595 (rampart upgrade tiers + full-bleed Front). Merged as `f3b42583`; Deploy run [30602572301](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/30602572301) completed green end to end — Cloud Run 03:50:51-03:54:07Z, then Firebase Hosting 03:55:03-03:55:17Z and Functions to 03:56:40Z. Hosting served the new pin about 5.5 minutes after the merge. Post-deploy checklist all passing: `keep.html` serves `keep.css?v=46` / `keep.js?v=45`; `/api/cards/editor` returns `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true` through both Hosting and the Cloud Run URL directly; `js/config.js` keeps `apiBaseUrl: ''`; `/api/game/options` returns 200 with 4 decks; `/play` and `/keep` return 200.

Original prompt: Rampart should be upgradable and slot expandable. Start with 1 slot when unlocked you can purchase or expend resources to upgrade the walls improving their quality and adding up to 4 Siegelings max on the walls

- July 30, 2026 Akhar's Front is now a full-bleed stage: the rampart runs to both edges of the screen instead of floating as a centred island on desktop and landscape. `.front-hotspot` in front view goes to `100vw` at every size (it was `min(1040px, 94vw)` on desktop and `min(1120px, 94vw)` in landscape), the battle box drops its rounded clip and the mobile `inset-inline: 2%`, and only the wall is full-bleed — posts, torches, raiders, and shots live in a centred `min(1120px, 100%)` stage so their percentage geometry keeps its composition on a 21:9 monitor. Those four wrappers are deliberately positioned with `margin-inline: auto` and **no** `z-index` or `transform`, because either would make them stacking contexts and break the raiders-behind-wall / defenders-in-front layering the combat depends on.
- Stretching one 360-unit `preserveAspectRatio="none"` wall across 2560px would have smeared its crenellations and stonework to ~2x, so `renderFrontRampart()` regenerates the path for the width actually rendered: the viewBox grows with the stage and merlons keep the authored 62-unit pitch, so a wide screen gains crenellations rather than stretching the ones it has. Measured pitch is now 185px at 1920 and 194px at 2560 (it would have been 339px stretched), while phones and landscape sit on a 360-unit floor that reproduces today's look exactly. It re-runs on resize, on rotation after the layout settles, and on leaving the Front so the map-sized wall shrinks its viewBox back. Wall dressing moved off percentage widths to `clamp()` plus `aspect-ratio` for the same reason — at 1920 a 5.5% banner had inflated into a 105px billboard.
- Two pre-existing landscape defects surfaced once the stage was full width and are fixed here: the 78vh stage at `top: 56%` pushed its own name plate off the bottom of a short viewport (now 72vh at 50%, measured to clear at 360-430px tall), and a short-screen rule that hides hotspot sublines to de-clutter the Keep grounds was also blanking the Front's `4/4 defenders - Bastion Battlements` line — restored for the front-view plate only, which is bottom-anchored so it grows upward rather than back off screen. Keep pins advanced to `keep.css?v=46` / `keep.js?v=45`.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 375/375. The headless harness grew to four viewports — 390x844, 844x390 landscape, 1920x1080, and 2560x1080 ultrawide — across all four tiers plus a gate-locked case: **478/478 assertions passed with zero page errors**, including new guards that the wall reaches both screen edges and spans at least the full viewport width, that merlons are never smeared (pitch < 230px) with narrow stages keeping the authored viewBox and wide stages growing it instead, that ultrawide keeps stone within 20% of desktop's size, that the three centred-stage wrappers create no stacking context, and that the name plate is fully on screen and carries the tier line at every viewport. Captures at all four viewports for tiers 1 and 4 were visually inspected.

- July 30, 2026 Akhar's Front ramparts are now an upgradable four-tier wall instead of a fixed three-post fortification. The wall's level *is* its post count, so `AKHARS_FRONT_CAPACITY` is gone and `akharsFrontCapacity(state)` returns the level: a newly raised Front opens exactly one post, and each upgrade opens one more to a maximum of four. Three new construction projects carry it — `akhars_front_level_2` "Reinforce the Rampart" (320 timber + 24 stone, 6h), `akhars_front_level_3` "Raise the Battlements" (420 timber + 32 stone + 14 ember ingot, 10h), and `akhars_front_level_4` "Crown the Bastion" (540 timber + 44 stone + 18 ember ingot + 14 frost crystal, 16h). They are ordinary build projects, so the existing material speed-up and Siegecoin instant-purchase paths cover the "purchase or expend resources" half of the request with no new payment code. Gates: every tier needs the Builder's Yard, tier 3 additionally needs a Stonehold keep (hall 4) and tier 4 a Walled Keep (hall 5). Wall quality is real, not cosmetic — the tier multiplies both income components per defender (+0/+10/+20/+35%) and raises the coin bank (120/220/340/480, replacing the flat 360), and the tiers are named Timber Palisade / Stone Rampart / Reinforced Bulwark / Bastion Battlements. The snapshot gained `maxLevel`, `wallName`, `wallBonusPercent`, a level-derived `capacity`, and an `upgrade` block that previews the next tier's posts, bonus, storage, cost, duration, and gate status even while it is locked, so the Front screen can tell the player what raising the walls buys and what it still needs. `PROJECT_IDS` picked up all four Front projects, so the dashboard can retune their cost and duration.
- Migration: Akhar's Front shipped with three fixed posts, so every existing Front is stored at level 1 with up to three assignments. `repairDefaults` promotes such a Keep to the tier matching its highest occupied post (capped at 4) before any normalization runs — without that, deriving capacity from level would have silently evicted posted Siegelings on the first load after deploy. Banked coins above a tier's new capacity are never destroyed; production just stops until the bank is collected.
- Client: `keep.html` now carries a fourth defender slot and a `.front-wall-trim` dressing block, and the rampart SVG paints itself from CSS variables (`--wall-top`/`--wall-base`/`--wall-line`/`--wall-course`/`--wall-cap`) so a tier is a palette swap rather than a second copy of the wall geometry. `keep.css` keys the whole look off `data-akhars-front-level` and the new `data-front-capacity`: tier 1 is dark timber with two lashed plank patches and two torches, tier 2 is bare cut stone with three torches, tier 3 lightens the stone and flies three banners with a full torch line, tier 4 is pale gilt stone with gold banners and a gilded coping course. Defender posts and torch positions are laid out per capacity so one defender stands mid-wall, four spread edge to edge, and no torch is ever planted through a defender; `FRONT_DEFENDER_STARTS` in `keep.js` mirrors those lefts so projectiles leave the defender that fired them at every tier. Original prompt: Fix the firestore transaction issue and then merge and deploy

- July 29, 2026 Transactional reward claims verified in production. PR `#571` (`f00e99b3`) squash-merged to `main` and deployed; live `/home` serves `home.js?v=124` / `home.css?v=117`, `/api/cards/editor` reports `source=FIRESTORE, liveEditingEnabled=true, firestoreAvailable=true`, and `/api/game/options` returns 200. The clearest signal that the new Cloud Run revision is serving is `GET /api/missions/daily`: unauthenticated it now returns `200` with `{"error":"Sign in to use saved decks and history."}` where before this deploy it returned a bare 500, which is a code change carried by this release. The deployed `js/home.js?v=124` (528,251 bytes) contains `claimsInFlight`, `renderMissionTrack`, and `renderKnightPanel`.
- Verification: the one thing the test suite could not cover — `RewardClaimStore`'s actual Firestore API calls, since this environment has no emulator and no `gcloud` — was closed by exercising it against the live database with a throwaway account. Registering an account and calling `POST /api/missions/claim-login` ran a real transaction end to end: gold moved 100 → 200, 10 points banked into the daily track, streak 1, and the response's embedded snapshot reported the post-claim state. A replay was rejected with `Daily login reward already claimed today.`, proving the marker committed durably alongside the payout; a locked chest and an empty Knight track were rejected with their own messages. The actual vulnerability was then re-tested live on a second fresh account by firing two `claim-login` requests simultaneously: one returned the payout, the other returned the already-claimed rejection, and the authoritative wallet read back **200, not 300** — one payout, which is the bug this work exists to prevent. Note this live race exercises the lock and the transaction together, because `--max-instances 1` puts both requests on one instance; the transaction's independent multi-instance guarantee is covered by `DailyMissionTransactionalClaimTest`, which models two instances over one store and was confirmed load-bearing by neutralizing the commit's version check.
- Cleanup miss to correct by hand: the two throwaway accounts were **not** deleted. `POST /api/auth/delete-account` requires `confirmationText: "DELETE"`, which the cleanup call omitted, and the session tokens were discarded in the same step before the failure was noticed — so the accounts cannot be signed back into (their generated passwords were never stored, and `/api/auth/reset-password` needs the server's configured reset code). Two inert rows remain in the live database, each holding 200 Siegecoins and one claimed login reward: `claude-txn-check-1785344324@example.invalid` and `claude-race-check-1785344376@example.invalid`. They are keyed by those email addresses in the `accountUsers`, `playerProgression`, `dailyMissionProgress`, and `authSessions` collections and can be deleted from the Firestore console. They affect no other player's data and appear on no leaderboard that ranks by wins, having played no matches.

Original prompt: Fix the firestore transaction issue and then merge and deploy

- July 29, 2026 Reward claims became genuinely atomic. The striped lock added in the previous entry closed the duplicate-payout window only for callers sharing a JVM, which left `--max-instances 1` in `deploy.yml` silently load-bearing for currency correctness — raise that flag and the duplication returns. Claims now run inside a Firestore transaction over both documents at once. `RewardClaimStore` reads the mission-progress and progression docs through `transaction.get`, hands the decoded entities to the claim body, and commits both writes together; because Firestore tracks every document a transaction read, a concurrent writer to either doc aborts the commit and the body re-runs against a fresh read, so the already-claimed marker and the payout can never diverge. Making that safe required the four claim paths to become pure functions of the two entities they are given: they no longer call `progressStore.save` / `playerProgressionStore.save` themselves (the transaction owns the write), no longer re-read through `getDailySnapshot` (a read after a write is illegal inside a transaction, and the response now reports post-claim state from the in-memory entity via the new `buildSnapshot`), and no longer fetch the card catalog inline — `getDeckBuilderCatalog()` is hoisted to before the transaction, since it can hit Firestore and would otherwise repeat on every optimistic retry. The daily/weekly rollover was likewise split out of `loadProgress` into a pure `applyRollover`, so a claim that crosses midnight carries the reset in its own commit instead of a second write. Everything the body still calls was checked to be pure: `getDeckBuilderMaxCopies()` and `craftCost()` are constants, so `grantCardsWithCap` performs no I/O. The striped lock is kept in front of the transaction — it costs nothing and stops two requests from one player on one instance burning optimistic retries against each other — and remains the sole guard on the fallback path used by unit tests and credential-less local runs, which is sound because those are single-process. Known limitation, unchanged by this work and pre-existing across the codebase: the progression document is written wholesale by several subsystems (pack opens, Siege payouts, Keep rewards), so those remain last-write-wins among themselves; what is now guaranteed is that a claim's marker and its payout commit together or not at all.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 349/349, with a new `DailyMissionTransactionalClaimTest` whose fake store reproduces Firestore's semantics — read at a version, run the body, commit only if nothing else wrote, otherwise re-run against a fresh read. Its four cases model **two instances** (two `DailyMissionService` objects with independent striped locks over one shared store, which is what two Cloud Run instances are) racing the same chest and the same Knight Levels, and assert a single payout each; a third forces three retries and asserts the body pays exactly once rather than a multiple, which is the property that makes retry-safety real; a fourth asserts a rejection inside the transaction surfaces unchanged (`Not enough points for that chest yet.`) with nothing written. The two-instance tests were confirmed load-bearing: neutralizing the commit's version check makes both report `expected: <1> but was: <2>`, and restoring it returns them to a single payout. The plain stores are stubbed to throw in that test, so a body that fell back to a direct read or write fails the build. The four `DailyMissionClaimConcurrencyTest` cases still pass on the fallback path. Not covered: `RewardClaimStore`'s own Firestore API calls against a real database — this environment has no emulator, no `gcloud`, and cannot sign in to production, so the transaction wiring was verified by review (all reads precede all writes; `ApiFuture.get` unwrapping returns the body's `IllegalArgumentException` rather than an `ExecutionException`; a missing document yields `exists()==false` rather than an error) and by the full-context `@SpringBootTest` cases proving the new component wires without a cycle. The claim body's behaviour under transaction semantics — the part this change actually rewrote — is covered by the tests above.

Original prompt: Do a general bug and vulnerability review

- July 29, 2026 Reward-claim duplication fixed. A review of the just-shipped mission reward surface found that every claim path is a read-modify-write spanning two stores — load progress, check the already-claimed marker, save it, then credit the wallet — with nothing serializing the window. Two concurrent requests for the same player both read a pre-claim snapshot, both pass the check, and both pay; the ledger records a single claim while the wallet receives two, so the duplication is invisible in the progress document and repeatable indefinitely. This is not merely adversarial: the claim buttons stayed live during the POST, so an ordinary double-tap on a phone fired two requests. Measured against a fake store that rebuilds a detached entity per read (exactly as the Firestore store's `toEntity` does) and sleeps inside the read to model a network round trip, two concurrent claims paid **chest 300 gold / 80 Remnants instead of 150/40, Knight Level 1300/550 instead of 650/275, mission 300 instead of 150, and login 200 instead of 100** — the Knight case scaling with however many levels were pending. Two of the four paths (mission, login) predate this work; the two new ones (chest, Knight) carry the largest payouts and can also mint cards. All four are now serialized per user with 64 striped locks, the same guard `PlayerProgressionService` already applies to pack opens; Cloud Run runs this service at `--max-instances 1`, so an in-process lock genuinely covers every caller rather than merely narrowing the window. The frontend gained a matching re-entry guard (`claimsInFlight`) so a double-tap no longer spends a wasted round trip. Two smaller findings were fixed alongside: `claimedChests(LIFETIME)` silently returned the *daily* list, a landmine that would have let a future caller read and mark daily chests while believing it was on a lifetime track, and now throws; and `GET /api/missions/daily` returned a bare 500 to a signed-out caller because it called `requireUser` outside the try/catch its sibling claim endpoints use, so it now returns the same `{"error": ...}` envelope the client already handles. Reviewed and found clean: notification rendering escapes card names through `escapeHtml` (no XSS from catalog-controlled text), threshold/period inputs are validated against the catalog so negative or unknown values are rejected, points can only be obtained by claiming missions whose counters the server records, and the save-claimed-then-grant ordering is fail-closed (a failure loses a reward rather than duplicating one). Cache pin advanced to `home.js?v=124`.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 345/345, including a new `DailyMissionClaimConcurrencyTest` with four cases that fire two threads at each claim path through a store modelling Firestore's detached-read behaviour and assert exactly one payout. The test was confirmed to be load-bearing in both directions: neutralizing the locks (giving each caller its own monitor) makes all four cases pay twice with the figures above, and restoring them returns all four to a single payout. A headless run at 390x844 and 1920x1080 double-tapped both claim buttons against a mocked API deliberately slowed to 350ms so the in-flight window was real: exactly one POST per endpoint per viewport reached the network (two total across both viewports) rather than the two per viewport an unguarded button would send, with the ladder and Knight crest still rendering and no horizontal overflow. Not covered: a multi-instance deployment, where an in-process lock would no longer be sufficient and a Firestore transaction or compare-and-set would be required — the `--max-instances 1` setting in `deploy.yml` is what makes the current guard complete, so that flag is now load-bearing for reward correctness and should not be raised without replacing the lock.

Original prompt: Merge and deploy

- July 29, 2026 Mission chest ladder and Knight Level production release: squash-merged PR `#570` (`06eba062`) to `main` after reconciling with `#569` (`c58b9073`), which landed on `main` while this was in review. The only conflict was `progress.md` — both changes prepend a dated entry — and the resolution keeps both, newest first; the code did not overlap at all (`#569` is `keep.*`, this is `home.*` plus the mission services). Four `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` cache-pin assertions were refreshed as part of the merge rather than left red: they pin the exact `home.html`/`home.css` versions this change advances and had already been failing on `main` since `#566` advanced past them, so they were pointed at what is actually shipped — `home.js?v=123`, `home.css?v=117`, `game.js?v=220`, and both card-back counters at 6. That took the suite to a fully green 341/341, the first clean run on this tree in several changes. Two deploys were in flight at once: run 589 for `#569` started at 12:40 and run 590 for this merge at 12:44. Run 590's tree contains `#569`, so the safe ordering was 590 landing last, and that is what happened — `keep.js?v=39` appeared on the host first, then `home.js?v=123`. Had they landed the other way, Hosting would have been left serving a tree without this feature.
- Verification: live `/home` serves `home.js?v=123` and `home.css?v=117`, and `/keep` still serves `#569`'s `keep.css?v=38` / `keep.js?v=39`, so neither deploy clobbered the other. The deployed `js/home.js?v=123` (527,129 bytes) contains `renderMissionTrack`, `renderKnightPanel`, `claimMissionChest`, `claimKnightLevel`, `rewardBundleMarkup`, `data-chest-threshold`, and `data-knight-claim`; the deployed `css/home.css?v=117` (230,848 bytes) contains `.mission-track`, `.mission-chest`, `.knight-panel`, `.knight-crest`, `.mission-points`, and `.reward-bundle`. Hosted `/api/cards/editor` reports `source=FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled=true`, `firestoreAvailable=true`; `/api/game/options` returns 200. Both new endpoints route on the host and reject an unauthenticated caller with `Sign in to use saved decks and history.` rather than granting anything — `POST /api/missions/claim-chest` with a real `{period, threshold}` body and `POST /api/missions/claim-knight` both refuse. Noted but not introduced here: unauthenticated `GET /api/missions/daily` returns a bare 500 because that method calls `requireUser` outside the try/catch its sibling claim endpoints use; that predates this change and was left alone. Not covered: an authenticated production claim, which this environment cannot sign in to perform — the reward math and idempotency were verified by the 9 service tests rather than against a live account.

Original prompt: Daily and weekly reward Hindi contribute points to chest that grant additional coins, remnants and at 100 points grant a random card. Similar to dragon traveler. Lifetime rewards contribute to a global "Knight Level" each level granting coin, remnants and card pulls

- July 29, 2026 Mission rewards gained a second layer. Claiming a mission previously paid its Siegecoins and nothing else, so a player who swept every daily objective got the same shape of reward as one who claimed a single easy one — there was no reason to finish the board. Every mission now also carries `points`, banked on claim into the pool for its period. Daily and weekly points fill a five-rung chest ladder (`MissionRewardTrack`): daily 20/40/60/80/100, weekly 200/400/600/800/1000, each chest paying Siegecoins plus Remnants and the **final** chest of each ladder additionally rolling a random card from the deck-builder catalog. Lifetime points are different in kind — they never reset, so they feed an account-wide **Knight Level** (`KnightLevelTrack`, 30 levels, `100 + (L-1)*50` points to advance) where each level pays `200 + L*50` coins, `75 + L*25` Remnants, and a card pull that becomes 3 on every fifth level. Point budgets were set so a player does not have to sweep the board to fill the bar: daily missions total 115 points plus 10 from the login reward against a 100-point ladder, weekly total 1150 against 1000. Six new lifetime missions (solo wins, spells, traps, siegelings, expedition nodes, 500 matches) were added at the same time, because the six that existed banked only 1650 points and would have capped the Knight track around level 7 with nothing left to chase. Chests and levels are **claimed**, not auto-granted: `POST /api/missions/claim-chest` (period + threshold) and `POST /api/missions/claim-knight`, which pays out every level reached since the last claim in one call. Ledger state lives on `DailyMissionProgressEntity` — `dailyPoints`/`weeklyPoints` and their claimed-threshold lists reset with the counters they sit beside, while `knightPoints` and the `claimedKnightLevel` high-water mark deliberately survive both resets. Card pulls route through `PlayerProgressionService.grantCardsWithCap` so copy caps and duplicate-to-Remnants conversion behave exactly as they do for pack pulls; that injection is `@Lazy` because `PlayerProgressionService` already depends on `DailyMissionService` and the cycle would otherwise fail context refresh (the same pattern `PlayerTitleService` uses). Firestore rows written before this change have no `claimedKnightLevel` field, so the reader defaults it to 1 rather than 0 — level 1 is the floor and treating a missing field as 0 would have paid a phantom level to every existing account. On the hub, the Daily and Weekly tabs gained the chest rail (chests positioned at their share of the max threshold, so the rail stays honest if thresholds are ever retuned unevenly) and the Lifetime tab gained a Knight Level crest with its progress bar and claim button; every mission row now shows the points it banks. Pins advanced to `home.js?v=123` and `home.css?v=117` (the latter in both `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`).
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/home.js` clean; Maven compile clean. Full suite 340 tests with 9 new `DailyMissionServiceTest` cases, all green — points bank into the right pool per period (20 daily / 250 weekly / 300 knight from three claims), the login reward banks its 10, a chest pays coins and Remnants exactly once and rejects a second claim, a locked threshold and an unknown threshold and the lifetime period are all rejected, a day/week rollover clears both point pools and both chest lists while leaving `knightPoints` and `claimedKnightLevel` untouched, `claimKnightLevels` pays levels 2 and 3 together (650 coins / 275 Remnants) and then refuses, the snapshot exposes both tracks with the right unlocked/claimable flags and the card pull on the last rung, and a budget assertion fails the build if either ladder ever becomes unreachable from its period's missions. Four `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` failures in the run (`everyPaintedElementUsesItsDedicatedNotchMedallion`, `profileDashboardTrimsBattlesFavoritesAndSocialTable`, `profileFavoriteElementIconsUseCircularNotchMedallions`, `remainingElementsUseTheirDedicatedCardBacks`) are pre-existing — they fail identically on a stashed tree at this branch point, all pin `home.html`/`home.js` versions from earlier changes, and are untouched by this work. Headless Chromium drove the real `home.html` against a mocked snapshot at 390x844 and 1920x1080: the Daily tab shows 45 points with 5 chests at 20/40/60/80/100 percent, one claimed and one glowing claimable; Weekly shows 500 points with its own ladder; Lifetime shows the Knight crest at level 4 with the unclaimed bundle and a "Claim 2 levels" button; mission rows render their point chips; no horizontal page overflow at either viewport and zero page errors. Clicking the claimable chest posted `{"period":"daily","threshold":40}` to `/api/missions/claim-chest` and the crest button posted to `/api/missions/claim-knight`, at both viewports. Captures were visually inspected. Not covered: a live authenticated session against Firestore, which this environment cannot open — the persistence round-trip was verified by reading the store's own save/load path rather than against the real database.

Original prompt: When you do construction and enter that building add a menu to hud to view construction type, time and timesavers. A nice construction drawn SVG unique to each building would also be nice in the interior so it changes in some way during itv

- July 29, 2026 Construction is now visible from inside the building being worked on. Previously the only signal that a crew was busy was the `#constructionBanner` floating over the grounds; walking into Covenant Hall mid-rank-up, or the Archive mid-restore, showed the finished room with no clock, no project name, and no way to spend a time saver without backing out to Projects. The interior HUD bar gained `#interiorBuildToggle` — a pill naming the project *kind* (Keep rank / Restoration / Expansion / Storage annex / New building), the live remaining time, and a thin progress meter — shown only when a crew is actually running on the room the player is standing in. Tapping it drops `#interiorBuildMenu` from the bar (anchored to `.interior-bar`, not the stage, because the room viewport is only ~250px tall on a phone): project name, `Phase N of 4`, a phase blurb, the progress meter, and a Stage / Time left / Finishes / Crew fact grid, followed by the same material and Siegecoin time savers the Projects panel offers. `timeSaverMarkup` grew an `alwaysOpen` argument so the menu is its own disclosure rather than nesting a second collapse, and `buyConstructionTimeSaver` now re-renders the interior instead of calling `openPanel('projects')` when the purchase came from inside a room — buying time no longer ejects the player from the building they are watching. Alongside it, `#interiorBuildArt` draws a flat work-site SVG unique to each of the ten walkable rooms (hall stone gantry, woodlot saw pit and drying racks, archive shoring and shelving, garden trellis, forge flue, fridge vault lattice, generator armature, quarry derrick, kitchen oven dome, enclave rafters and nests), bottom-anchored on the room floor and capped at `min(64%, 420px)` so the room still reads through it. Each drawing is four `data-phase-min` groups; `renderInteriorConstruction` raises one more group per quarter of the build, so the same room is visibly further along on the next visit rather than only the clock having moved. `constructionTarget` was widened from a hardcoded `_level_2` check to any `_level_N` plus `_storage_annex`, which also fixes the grounds scaffolding for storage-annex projects. State: `interiorBuildOpen`, closed on every room change and on exit, and Escape closes the menu before it closes the room. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=38` / `keep.js?v=39`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` clean; `git diff --check` clean; full `./mvnw test` 333 tests, including the new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#enteringABuildingUnderConstructionShowsItsSiteAndAConstructionHudMenu` (HUD hooks, room resolution, kind labels, live clock hooks, per-room art map, and the stay-in-the-room speed-up path). Four `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` failures remain in the run (`everyPaintedElementUsesItsDedicatedNotchMedallion`, `profileDashboardTrimsBattlesFavoritesAndSocialTable`, `profileFavoriteElementIconsUseCircularNotchMedallions`, `remainingElementsUseTheirDedicatedCardBacks`) — all four are `home.html` cache pins, all four fail identically on a stashed tree at this branch point, and none is touched by this change. Headless Chromium drove the real `keep.html` at 390x844 and 1920x1080 against a snapshot dumped from the real `KeepService` (all six workshops, Enclave built, Hall rank 3, six construction slots): 322/322 checks, zero page or console errors. All ten rooms show the toggle with a kind and a clock, draw art tagged to their own room id with four phase groups and a distinct SVG body (no two rooms share a drawing), raise exactly `phase + 1` groups at 10/35/60/88% progress, open a menu carrying the project name, `Phase N of 4`, all four facts, and two enabled time savers, with no horizontal overflow at either viewport. A room with no crew on it shows neither toggle nor art, and walking into the worked room raises both. The clock ticks in both the toggle and the menu over two seconds without the menu being rebuilt; spending materials from inside leaves the player in `great_hall` with the panel closed and the clock cut; Escape closes the menu and leaves the room open. Captures of all four hall phases and of every room's site at both viewports were visually inspected. Not covered: a live signed-in keep — `/api/keep` requires Firestore account credentials this environment does not have, so the snapshot was generated from `KeepService` directly rather than fetched from a running server.

Original prompt: Update the earth card back

- July 29, 2026 Earth card back replaced with the navy-and-gold artwork supplied with the request. The shipped `card-back-earth.png` was the odd one out of the twelve elemental backs: its frame sat on a mossy olive field and, unlike every sibling, the art was matted on **white**, so the Stone Garden tile on `/decks` drew a white halo around its rounded corners while Blazing Core, Gale Talons, and Frostmarch read as seamless dark tiles. The new source (1024x1536) was resized to the established `520x760` footprint with Lanczos and written as both the PNG and its `.webp` twin — every local raster card asset has one, and `card-binder-visual.js` / `game.js` swap to `.webp` on supporting browsers, so shipping only the PNG would have left the old art live for most players. The new art carries the same near-black matte as the other eleven backs (corner `0,0,0`), which is what removes the halo; the leaf-over-mountain sigil and the gold diamond notch markers are unchanged in meaning, only the field behind them moves from olive to the deep blue the rest of the set uses. Both cache-bust counters that gate this art advanced together — `DECK_ART_ASSET_VERSION` in `game.js` and `ELEMENTAL_CARD_BACK_VERSION` in `home.js`, 5 → 6 — because the two bundles version the same `/img/decks/*` URLs independently and a stale one would keep serving `?v=5` from the service worker's image cache (`cacheFirstImage` matches on the full URL including query, so the bump is what evicts it). The bundles' own pins followed in every page that loads them: `game.js?v=220` in `play.html`, `home.html`, and `card-dashboard.html`, and `home.js?v=122` in `home.html`. No JS logic, CSS, or deck data changed — `scripts/storage-asset-manifest.json` is a record of a past Storage upload and is not read by the app, so it was left alone.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on both `js/game.js` and `js/home.js`. Geometry and matte asserted directly on the written files — PNG and WebP both `520x760`, both corners `0,0,0`, matching `card-back-fire.png`. A headless-Chromium pass at 390x844 and 1920x1080 served the static tree and drew the new earth back beside the fire back at deck-tile size, reading each back through a canvas: both reported `520x760` with corner `0,0,0` at both viewports, and the versioned URLs `card-back-earth.png?v=6` / `.webp?v=6` both returned 200. Captures were visually inspected — the earth tile now matches the fire tile's dark-edged framing with no white border. Not covered: the live `/decks` grid behind auth, which this environment cannot sign into; the change is asset-plus-version only, and the tile markup that consumes these paths was untouched.

Original prompt: In standard battle the end flow against ai should be. End turn pop up message -> ai thinking message -> ai card play animation and placement effect -> if more cards play sequentially with placement effects -> think -> ai send bubble chat emoji to player -> phase change pop (end turn if player turn is next or battle phase is battle phase is next)

- July 28, 2026 Solo AI end-turn presentation flow: ending Setup now waits for any prior Draw playback, announces the player's end turn, shows the AI thinking, reveals every AI placement sequentially with its existing placement effect, pauses to think again, sends a deterministic emoji reaction bubble toward the player, and only then presents the next phase banner. The custom choreography is scoped to standard solo battles; multiplayer and other state diffs retain their existing queue order. After reconciling the Neutral-notch release on `main`, asset pins advanced to `style.css?v=217`, `game.js?v=219`, and `action-queue.js?v=37` while retaining `card-binder-visual.js?v=20`.
- Verification: explicit Node syntax checks, focused `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest`, Maven compile, and `git diff --check` pass. A deterministic mobile queue harness covered the full two-card sequence and an API-backed mobile run covered the real end-turn response without overlapping phase banners.

Original prompt: Implement character first menu with cell selections example. Favorite singeing can be a singular cel that shows your current favorite of a an empty silhouette if none selected. You click on the favorite portrait that opens a cell selection of singeing buttons/cells. That are portraits in a grid like view. You click the new portrait a menu comes up With the paper cutout a little line about the effect and description and an approve or deny button.

- July 29, 2026 The Keeper's Favor became a character-first cell menu. Covenant Hall previously listed every owned Siegeling as a stacked name row and committed the keep-wide honor on the first tap, so a mis-scroll on a phone could silently swap the bonus that drives every station's output. The panel now opens on a single honored cell — the current favorite drawn with the shared paper-cutout treatment, or a Siegeling-shaped silhouette when nobody is honored — with the effect stated beside it. Tapping the cell replaces the card body with `.favor-grid`, an `auto-fill` grid of portrait cells (one per owned Siegeling, each carrying its own bonus and a star on whoever is honored). Tapping a portrait raises `#favorOverlay`, a confirm sheet holding the candidate's paper cutout, one effect line (`+16% keep-wide output`), a description naming who steps down and whether rapport already multiplies the favor, and Approve/Deny buttons; Escape and the backdrop both deny. Approving the Siegeling already honored is the step-down path, so the sheet reverses its wording (`Ends +16%…`, button `Step down`) rather than needing a second control. Nothing is committed until Approve — the honored cell keeps the same footprint in every state so the card beneath a thumb never shifts. The previewed percentage is served, not recomputed: `serializeResident` now emits `favoriteBonusPercent` per resident from the same `favoriteBoostFor` helper `favoriteBoost` uses, so rapport folds in identically and the preview cannot drift from the boost the keep receives (the client's rarity ladder survives only as a fallback for older payloads). The old `data-set-favorite` handler and every `.favorite-choice`/`.favorite-current` rule are gone. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=36` / `keep.js?v=37` — one past the `?v=35`/`?v=36` that the parallel Enclave-collapse change (#559) landed on `main` while this was in review, since both changes edit the same two bundles.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 326/326, including a new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#keepersFavorPicksACharacterCellAndConfirmsBeforeHonoring` (cell + silhouette, portrait grid, confirm overlay with cutout/approve/deny, and an explicit failure if `data-set-favorite` or `.favorite-choice` returns) and a widened `KeepServiceTest#favoriteSieglingGrantsRarityScaledKeepWideBoost` asserting each resident previews its own bonus (Common 5%, Legendary 20%, Common at rapport 1 = 6%, matching the applied boost). `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` clean. Headless Chromium drove the real `keep.html` through `__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__` at 390x844 and 1920x1080: 66/66 checks, zero page or console errors — empty cell shows the silhouette and no legacy list; tapping it opens 5 portrait cells (3 columns at both sizes) all using the paper treatment; picking Legendary Pylord shows the cutout, `+22% keep-wide output`, and a description quoting its rapport, with the favorite still unset; Deny closes the sheet and leaves the grid untouched; Approve commits Frostkin, returns to the honored cell reading `+16% to every station's output…`, and holds the cell height within 0.3px of the empty state; reopening stars the current favorite and its sheet reads `Ends +16% keep-wide output` / `Step down`, which clears back to the silhouette; Escape denies without committing; no horizontal overflow at either viewport. Captures in the session scratchpad (`favor-empty/grid/confirm/honored` at both viewports) were visually inspected. Re-verified after merging `main`: #559 collapsed the Enclave into its own cell grid and touched `closePanel`, the keep pins, and the same regression test, so the conflicts were resolved by keeping both guards (the favor menu closes *and* the Enclave collapses on panel close) and advancing the pins past main's; the two feature CSS namespaces (`.favor-*` / `.enclave-space-*`) do not collide. Post-merge: full suite 326/326 and the 66-check headless run repeated green on the merged tree, plus a focused pass confirming #559's behavior survives the shared `closePanel` resolution — the Enclave opens as 5 space cells, one expands to a single space card, and closing the panel collapses it back to 5 cells with no page errors. Also refreshed three stale `home.js?v=120` pin assertions to the `?v=121` that `#558` actually shipped — they were already failing on this branch's base and are unrelated to this change.

Original prompt: In the keep siegeling enclave make the enclave spots collapsible. The menu should feature buttons of the siegelings opening a single menu for each enclave spot that you can close retrying back to the cell like button menu

- July 29, 2026 Enclave spaces collapse to a cell-like button menu. The Enclave surface (panel and walkable interior alike) rendered all five spaces as stacked `.enclave-slot-card`s, each with a portrait, a rapport meter, and every task that resident offers — on a phone that buried spaces 3-5 under several screens of scrolling, and the assign menu could be open on a card the player had scrolled away from. `enclaveMarkup` now has two states driven by the new `state.enclaveOpenSlot` (-1 = collapsed): collapsed renders a three-column grid of `.enclave-space-cell` buttons, one per space, showing the Siegeling's cutout, name, element, rapport level, and a green badge counting tasks ready to bank (or a dashed "Open space" seat when empty); tapping a cell expands that space alone through the existing `enclaveSlotMarkup`, and the `.enclave-collapse` close button in the card header — `data-enclave-space="-1"` — returns to the grid. Three `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` failures remain in the run (`everyPaintedElementUsesItsDedicatedNotchMedallion`, `profileDashboardTrimsBattlesFavoritesAndSocialTable`, `profileFavoriteElementIconsUseCircularNotchMedallions`) — all three fail identically on a stashed tree at this branch point, are `home.html` profile pins, and are untouched by this change. Headless Chromium against the real `keep.html` via `__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__` (Enclave built, 3 of 5 spaces seated, one task complete) at 390x844 and 1920x1080: entering the Enclave shows 5 cells and zero slot cards or task blocks, with the ready badge reading `1` on the only space that has one; opening space 2 shows exactly 1 card titled "Enclave space 2" for Tidecall with both tasks and a close button and no cells; the portrait still opens the assign menu (4 choices) inside it; closing returns to 5 cells; an empty space opens to its invite seat, choosing a Siegeling seats Galewisp, and collapsing shows them in cell 5. Zero page errors and zero horizontal overflow at both viewports; the grid and expanded captures were visually inspected.

Original prompt: Apply the scaling update to all interiors for Siegelings

Original prompt: Include the neutral notch

Original prompt: Game update exterior notch points stay activated for the duration of the battle even when the attached singling is destroyed. These are call wells, this helps to mitigate being steamrolled by getting wiped. When a siegeling reattaches it still only counts as 1 energy. But the baseline total remains

- July 28, 2026 call-well rules pass started: restoring the dormant per-match perimeter activation maps as the authoritative baseline-energy source, exposing viewer-oriented call-well state to the battle client, and keeping activated exterior points lit after their attached Siegeling is defeated. A reattachment to an established well remains one energy rather than stacking.
- Call-well rules pass complete: `EnergyService` now latches each first elemental perimeter touch into the existing per-match activation map and always includes that one-per-socket baseline in external energy. `GameController` exposes viewer-oriented `playerCallWells` / `enemyCallWells`; the battle renderer uses those maps to keep the point lit while drawing an attachment line only for a card that is still present. The first element remains bound to the well, so a later different-element attachment cannot stack or replace its baseline. - July 27, 2026 Siegling size bands now apply in every room, not just the Enclave. The band shipped in #537 only reached `.enclave-residents` and `.enclave-interior-residents`, so a Gigantic legendary towered over the enclave huts and then shrank back to a stock silhouette the moment it was posted to a workshop — the same creature read as two different sizes depending on where it stood. The `--cutout-scale` ladder (.84 / 1 / 1.24 / 1.62) is now declared once for every surface that draws a Siegeling at world scale: the enclave exterior and interior, the grounds worker at the Woodlot hotspot, the `.lodge-resident` (Woodlot and Covenant Hall), and the `.facility-room-resident` in all six workshops. Every cutout dimension in those rooms consumes the scale — both the `has-overlay-art` silhouette and the initials paper token, at base and narrow-viewport sizes — so the ladder cannot be flattened by a phone media query. No JS change was needed: `setResidentOverlayArt` already stamped `data-size` on every node it paints, which is why the bug was CSS-only. `.shrine-art` was deliberately left alone: `#favoriteShrine` was removed from the grounds in the July 21 change and the Covenant Hall shows the favorite as a panel chooser chip rather than a world-scale cutout, so scaling it would only have dressed up dead CSS. Two collisions the growth introduced were fixed at the same time: on a phone a Gigantic cutout reached the interior next-room arrow in both the workshop rooms and the lodge, so `.facility-room-resident` moves to `right: 22%` and `.lodge-resident` to `left: 46%` on narrow viewports — a fixed nudge for every band rather than a per-band offset, which would make a Siegeling hop sideways when reassigned. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=33` and `keep.js?v=34` (re-advanced past main during the merge below).
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 320/320, including a new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` case that fails the build if any of the five world-scale holders loses a size band, if a room's cutout dimensions stop consuming `var(--cutout-scale)`, or if the dead `.shrine-art` rules are scaled. Headless Chromium walked all seven production rooms via the interior rail at 390x844 and 1920x1080 against a snapshot with a resident of a known band posted to each: 22/22 both as paper tokens and as art cutouts. Desktop cutouts measured 96 px MEDIUM, 119 px LARGE, 155.5 px GIGANTIC (ratios 1.240 and 1.620 against the .84/1/1.24/1.62 ladder); phone measured 78 / 96.7 / 126.4 px with the same ratios. A band renders identically in every workshop (garden and kitchen both 155.5 px; fridge and quarry both 96 px), and the grounds worker carries the Woodlot resident's band. The 42/42 Enclave harness still passes unchanged. The two arrow collisions were found by inspecting the phone captures and confirmed by hit-testing `document.elementFromPoint` at each arrow's centre — the arrows stayed clickable throughout (they paint above the art), so the defect was cosmetic, and after the nudge the bounding boxes no longer intersect at either viewport. Room captures at both sizes were visually inspected.
- Merge with `main` (16 commits, every touched file overlapping): a parallel session had meanwhile scaled `.lodge-resident` and `.facility-room-resident` and added a new world-scale `.hall-favorite-resident` in the Covenant Hall, so most of this branch dissolved into main's version. What survived is the union: main's block plus `.resident-worker > span`, the grounds worker, which main still did not scale — a Siegeling changed height merely by being seen from outside its workshop. The phone arrow-collision nudges also survived, and they are not hypothetical: removing them on the merged tree reproduces the overlap against main's own geometry (garden -28px, woodlot -12px), so main currently ships gigantic residents tucked behind the next-room arrow on a phone. Cache pins were re-advanced past main's (`keep.css?v=32`->33, `keep.js?v=33`->34) since both sides edited the same bundles, and the regression test was widened to cover `.hall-favorite-resident` while keeping the assertion that the orphaned `.shrine-art` rules stay unscaled. Post-merge verification: full suite 324/324, and a headless walk of all seven rooms plus the Covenant Hall at 390x844 and 1920x1080 passed 24/24 — the grounds worker now carries its band (95.5px phone / 118.1px desktop at GIGANTIC), the hall favorite scales (127.2 / 156.3px), every room reports its expected band, and both arrow clearances are positive at both viewports.
