Production may still be faster, but if local starts regress again, investigate the Firebase-backed catalog/override fetch path instead of shrinking the timeout.

- April 3, 2026 desktop hover cleanup: restricted the floating focused-card preview over the enemy half to the mobile layout only. Wide desktop now keeps the existing hand hover/tooltip behavior without the oversized mobile-style preview covering the enemy board, and window resize now re-syncs the floating preview plus the lifted hand card layer immediately.
- Added and iterated on turn flow, placement, energy, and battle rules across the Spring Boot web game.
- Latest change: moved board notch dots to the actual card boundary so edge/corner links render on the rectangle perimeter instead of floating inward.
- April 1, 2026 popup preview follow-up: restored the floating card preview as a full rendered card instead of the old text summary box. Locked hand cards can now still be selected for inspection, the selected-card drawer reuses the same full-card renderer, and both views show notch dots on the real card perimeter. Local Playwright screenshots in `output/web-game/popup-restored-check` confirmed the popup and drawer on both mobile and desktop after restarting Spring Boot from source.
- Added Earth and Wind as first-class elements using the Roblox reference project's names/colors/archetypes (`CreatureData.lua` / `ArenaSystem.lua`), expanded energy tracking/UI to four elements, and switched the starter matchup to Fire/Earth vs Water/Wind.
- Raised Siegling HP floors so all creature cards now start at 10+ HP via the scaled health helper in `src/main/java/com/sieglings/service/CardDefinitionService.java`.
- Reworked internal mixed-element links into combo points. Same-element links are now the only source of normal internal energy; mixed internal points produce combo records like `EARTH+FIRE`, including 3-way and 4-way crossroads.
- Added combo-spell requirements in `SpellCard` plus starter-deck combo spells such as `Emberroot Burst` and `Storm Current`. Live API now exposes combo counts and combo point signatures to the UI.
- Replaced sidebar energy stats with a visual Energy Bucket made of per-energy circles plus combo pie tokens, and added an element color key to the right panel.
- Added a pre-match loadout flow with 8 preset decks (4 single-element, 4 mixed including one four-element deck) and SiegeKnight selection before the game starts. Backend exposes `/api/game/options`, and `new game` now accepts `deckId` + `trainerId`.
- Added trap cards as a full card type. Traps now serialize into the hand/UI, use opponent energy-bucket requirements instead of your own energy, and the AI can spring them during setup. Shared action-card targeting now reads `targetType` metadata instead of guessing from description text.
- Added a one-Siegling-per-turn placement cap for both player and AI. Game state now tracks per-turn placements, the backend blocks second placements with a clear log message, and the frontend stops highlighting placement cells after your first placement resolves.
- Added stronger trap support in deck construction by giving trap cards a light playset presence instead of singleton odds, so elemental and mixed decks draw them more consistently.
- Added elemental weakness bonus damage for Siegling attacks using the Roblox reference chart subset now active in the TCG. Current live matchups: Water gets +1 vs Fire/Earth, Earth gets +1 vs Wind, and Wind gets +1 vs Fire. Weakness bonus is applied only to source-based Siegling damage, not generic spells/traps.
- Adjusted board card layout so creature names sit centered inside the cell while HP/stats/statuses are anchored above the bottom edge, reducing overlap with lower notch points and the stat strip.
- Added selected common battle fallbacks: a few commons now get zero-cost weak poke attacks or zero-cost self-move abilities that reposition them to an open linked point during battle. Verified live that `Flame Sprite` exposes `Flame Sprite Drift` as a 0-energy `SELF` action.
- Added Roblox-backed evolution placement rules for the current TCG lines: `Cacty -> Jacked'ty`, `Pylme -> Bonoblade`, `Breezee -> Gagglestand`, and `Pursula -> Purseus`. Evolution cards now target the occupied precursor cell, and the evolved board instance merges inherited notches with the new card's added links. Live API check confirmed `Player evolves Cacty into Jacked'ty...` and the merged notch set `TOP,RIGHT,BOTTOM,LEFT`.
- Added Shadow and Electric as live TCG elements while keeping Water active. The backend now tracks six elemental energies, the deck builder/startup picker includes `deck_shadow` and `deck_electric`, and the generated catalogs now include Roblox-backed Shadow and Lightning/Electric rosters plus 30 spells / 2 traps per new element.
- Added `Night Regent` and `Volt Shepherd` as SiegeKnight options, updated the UI color system / energy bucket / element key for Shadow and Electric, and fixed `GameService`'s preset fallback so the app no longer depends on a hardcoded deck list index.
- Latest verification: `-DskipTests compile` passes, the app is running again on port 8080 from `spring-boot-16.log`, `/api/game/options` now returns `10` decks and `8` trainers, and a live `deck_shadow` start produced a Shadow opening hand with `shadowEnergy` and `electricEnergy` fields present in the serialized player state.
- Node/npx are not installed in this environment, so the Playwright loop from the web-game skill could not be run locally. Verification is currently manual via the running Spring Boot app at http://localhost:8080.
- Latest manual verification: `-DskipTests compile` passes, the app is running again on port 8080, mono Fire now starts from a 17-card list (`deckSizeAfterDraw: 12` after opening hand), and a live battle log confirmed weakness damage: `Coral Seahorse Strike deals 3 damage to Apple Head (weakness +1)`.
- If further visual tuning is needed, continue adjusting `.board-notches` inset and the `.notch-*` anchor positions in `src/main/resources/static/css/style.css`.
- Added a dedicated compact mobile layout for gameplay. The left panel now renders as concise enemy/player/action summary blocks, the right panel now has mobile-only tabs for `Battle / Card / Log / Key`, the board compresses to a smaller responsive footprint, and the hand switches to a dense 3-column card grid with truncated copy instead of a long scrolling strip.
- Updated markup in `src/main/resources/templates/game.html`, mobile interaction state in `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js`, and responsive styling in `src/main/resources/static/css/style.css`.
- Latest verification: `-DskipTests compile` passes, the Spring Boot app was restarted on port 8080 from `spring-boot-19.log`, and both the local and tunneled HTML now serve the new `summary-block` + `mobile-info-tabs` markup. Playwright visual validation is still blocked because `node`/`npx` are not installed in this environment.
- Firebase/mobile startup bug fix: the hosted app was rebuilding the full deck + SiegeKnight chooser every 2 seconds while an online room was waiting because `startRoomPolling()` called `renderLoadoutOptions()` on every poll. - March 30, 2026 disconnected-energy fix: `EnergyService` now analyzes only the active connected foundation network by reusing `PlacementService.getFoundationSieglings(...)`. This means movement abilities that break reciprocal links now immediately drop the energy gained from those broken links after battle-phase recalculation.
- Added a move regression in `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/EnergyServiceTest.java` that moves a linked Siegling to an isolated point and verifies the broken internal energy disappears after `recalculateEnergy`.

- March 30, 2026 preset deck rebalance: `CardDefinitionService` now builds preset decks with an exact `20 Sieglings / 10 spells / 10 traps` structure instead of dumping the whole element pool. Siegling packages are assembled from full evolution lines only, never exceed 3 copies per card, and distribute the 20-creature target across the deck's included elements.
- Spell and trap packages now use fixed-size repeated bundles that prefer lower-cost, lower-combo, on-theme options first, which makes the preset lists less clogged with expensive setup cards.
- Added `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/CardDefinitionServiceTest.java` to verify every preset deck option builds to 40 cards total, contains the exact `20 / 10 / 10` split, respects the 3-copy limit, and includes full evolution trees whenever any member of a Siegling line appears.

- March 30, 2026 stat-system simplification: Sieglings now expose only `Health`, `Speed`, notches, and abilities. The old printed `Attack` and `Defense` stats were removed from `SieglingCard`, `CardInstance`, API serialization, and the frontend card/board/tooltips.
- Damage now comes from battle abilities and printed attack abilities instead of a permanent attack stat. Temporary offense effects were reshaped into `damage_boost`, while old defense-style buffs were converted into `health_boost` so durability is expressed through Health instead of hidden damage reduction.
- Trainer rosters, generated creatures, and generated spells were updated so their text/effects now reference attack-damage boosts, health boosts, healing, speed, freeze, and destroy effects instead of `Attack/Defense` stat buffs.

- March 31, 2026 placement-anchor update: non-evolution Sieglings can now be placed using either a reciprocal creature-notch link or a compatible perimeter socket on that empty cell. Left-edge cells accept `LEFT`, right-edge cells accept `RIGHT`, and the board's outer row accepts `BOTTOM` for the player side or `TOP` for the enemy side.
- `PlacementService.getFoundationSieglings(...)` now treats externally anchored edge cards as valid foundation seeds too, so cards placed via sockets remain live for energy generation and future expansion instead of becoming dead isolated pieces.
- Frontend placement previews in `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js` now mirror that rule by allowing highlighted legal cells when the selected Siegling can anchor to an open external socket, not only when it can link to a neighboring creature.
- Added `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/PlacementServiceTest.java` coverage for socket-only placement legality and for externally anchored cards remaining in the foundation network. Updated `EnergyServiceTest` so the broken-link movement regression still checks a truly unanchored destination under the new rule.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q test` passes after the placement-anchor update.
- March 31, 2026 manual card-tuning system: Siegling stats, notches, cost, evolution data, and abilities can now be overridden through `src/main/resources/cards/siegling-overrides.json` without hand-rewriting the generated roster. `GeneratedCreatureCatalog` still supplies the baseline cards, and `ManualSieglingCatalog` merges any listed overrides on top.
- Added `CARD_TUNING.md` with the editable JSON shape and an example override, plus `ManualSieglingCatalogTest` coverage proving a hand-authored definition can replace generated rarity, health, speed, notches, cost, and ability fields.
- March 31, 2026 ability effect-key registry: added `AbilityEffectKeys` plus `ABILITY_EFFECT_KEYS.md` so ability definitions and manual override JSON have a single reference for supported effect strings instead of relying on scattered magic values.
- Added `connected_allies_health_boost`, a new source-based effect that gives max Health to allied Sieglings connected to the source card's live reciprocal-link network. `EffectService` now resolves it from the source card, `BattleService` preserves sensible self-targeting when passive creature abilities are converted into active battle abilities, and `EffectServiceTest` covers the linked-vs-isolated ally case.
- March 31, 2026 connected-effect expansion: added `connected_allies_damage_boost` alongside the connected-health effect so link-network abilities can now buff either attack damage or max Health on connected allies only. `ABILITY_EFFECT_KEYS.md` documents both, and `EffectServiceTest` now covers both families.
- March 31, 2026 connected-effect expansion follow-up: added `connected_allies_speed_boost` so connected-network abilities can now buff attack damage, max Health, or Speed on linked allies only. `BattleService` now treats all three connected buff keys as source-based self-target abilities, and `EffectServiceTest` covers the speed variant too.
- March 31, 2026 override JSON ordering pass: reordered `src/main/resources/cards/siegling-overrides.json` by element, then grouped full evolution lines together within each element, with standalone creatures listed after the evo groups for easier manual tuning.
- March 31, 2026 multi-ability override support: Sieglings can now carry an explicit `abilities` array in `siegling-overrides.json`, with each ability defining its own `requiredElement` and `requiredEnergy`. `BattleService` treats that array as the card's explicit battle loadout, preserving per-ability costs instead of overwriting them with rarity defaults, and the frontend card views now render ability lists instead of assuming a single printed effect.

- April 2, 2026 placement-anchor mismatch fix: the frontend was correctly highlighting Emberfox's middle placement because it can reciprocally link to the friendly Pylook above, but the backend still limited non-socket anchor checks to the current foundation cluster. `PlacementService.isConnectedByAnchor(...)` now considers any friendly Siegling already on the board for reciprocal notch anchoring, which keeps server placement legality aligned with the UI's legal-cell highlight behavior.
- Added `PlacementServiceTest.placementCanAnchorOffDisconnectedFriendlySiegling()` to lock in the reported case: a disconnected friendly card with a matching notch can still serve as the legal anchor for a new placement even when it is outside the current foundation network.

- Same pass: added a one-shot `loadoutStartPending` state in `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js` so `Start Match` / `Create Room` / `Join Room` immediately flips into a disabled loading label (`Starting Match...`, `Creating Room...`, etc.) with `aria-busy=true`, preventing rapid duplicate submissions while the request is in flight.

- April 2, 2026 start-match timeout fix: the browser was never reaching mulligan because the frontend's generic `DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` of 10 seconds was aborting `/api/game/new` before the backend finished assembling the opening match state. A raw browser fetch measured the real response at about 18.6 seconds locally, so the request itself was healthy but our own timeout killed it.
- Added `LOADOUT_ACTION_TIMEOUT_MS = 30000` in `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js` and wired it into `newGame()`, `createRoom()`, and `joinRoom()` so the loadout CTA keeps its loading lock long enough for the full match-start response to return.

- July 20, 2026 My Keep elemental-economy expansion: extended the server-authoritative Keep state and Firestore persistence with shared Elemental Essence, a two-level Covenant Storehouse, four two-level production facilities (Covenant Garden, Accord Forge, Frost Fridge, Elemental Generator), independent facility storage/remainders/accrual clocks, and one resident slot per working station. Returning after at least five minutes now materializes production and completed construction into a “While you were away” report covering timber/essence gains, finished projects, lore found, and capped stations.

Original prompt: In the same way add a button that allows you to purchase instead of building projects for significant siegecoin it instantly buys the building or upgrade.

- July 28, 2026 Keep instant project purchases: every eligible build or upgrade in the Projects popup now offers a separate gold-styled Siegecoin action with an inline confirmation. Its server-owned price is deliberately substantial: 250 base coins, plus the project's timber cost, plus two coins per construction minute, plus eight coins per required workshop material. Purchasing skips timber/material costs, construction-team availability, and the timer, while preserving project prerequisites and Keeper-level requirements; the result immediately applies the building or upgrade, grants normal project XP and Enclave task credit, updates completed-project statistics, and leaves any already-running construction untouched. The authenticated `/api/keep/build/purchase` endpoint derives the price and validates and deducts the account's Siegecoins server-side, while build-option snapshots expose the quoted price and affordability. Test-mode simulation and `render_game_to_text` mirror the flow. The combined Keep purchase bundle lands on `keep.css?v=37` and `keep.js?v=38` after rebasing over the Keeper's Favor release.

Original prompt: 2 things, not sure why Generoot is the same size as Droxyl. He is a 3rd level evolution and should be one of the largest on screen. Also when cards evolve the cards in hand appear to change but then revert back to the original card. They should change to new cards from the evolution deck

- August 16, 2026 Two Siege battle bugs from the same screenshot. **Sprite size**: battlefield sprites read `data-size` off `SieglingSize`, which was derived from rarity alone on the theory that "rarity already tracks how far along an evolution line a card sits" — it does not. Generoot is the stage-3 form of squirebud -> floraknight -> generoot but is printed **RARE**, so it landed in the MEDIUM band and stood at `--sprite-scale: 1.19`, no taller than the stage-2 Droxyl beside it. `SieglingSize.defaultFor` now takes the **larger** of the rarity band and the evolution-depth band (base small, stage 2 medium, stage 3+ large), so Generoot resolves to LARGE (1.48) while a base form keeps its rarity band and legendaries stay GIGANTIC. `ManualSieglingCatalog#applyDefaultSizes` already passed the real chain depth, so this is a one-place change; the explicit dashboard `size` pin still wins over both. **Evolution hand cards**: evolving only shuffled the new stage's moves into the *deck* and emitted a `cardUpdate` event carrying cosmetic `previewMoves` — `refreshHandCards` in `adventure.js` morphed the owner's cards to the new art and then re-rendered from the untouched state, which is exactly the "changes then reverts" the player saw. `SiegeContentService#upgradeHandCards` replaces it: every card in hand owned by the evolved unit is rewritten to a move of the new stage (Evolution cards are left alone, since the next stage's unlock is dealt from the deck) and the returned previews are what the cards actually became, so the flip and the hand now agree. `SiegeCombatEngine` calls it from both evolution paths — the played Evolution card and the battle-start Evolution sigil. Instance ids are preserved so the morph animation keeps its DOM nodes. No static asset changed, so no `?v=` bump applies.
- Verification: `./mvnw -Dtest=ManualSieglingCatalogTest,SiegeCardRewardTest,SiegeEvolutionHandTest,SiegeEvolutionSigilTest,SiegeSpriteSizeTest test` green, **30/30**, three of them new or rewritten. `ManualSieglingCatalogTest#aLateStageFormIsNeverSizedBelowItsEvolutionDepth` pins the band rule directly (stage-3 RARE -> LARGE, stage-2 COMMON -> MEDIUM, base RARE still MEDIUM, LEGENDARY still GIGANTIC) and the existing roster test was loosened from "equals the rarity band" to "at least the rarity band, exactly it for base forms", which is the assertion that was encoding the bug. `SiegeEvolutionHandTest` drives a real run end to end: it picks a catalog line whose two stages have distinct move sets, equips an Evolution sigil, starts a battle through `SiegeCombatEngine#startBattle`, and asserts no card left in the opening hand is a precursor-only move — it fails against the previous build, where the hand keeps its base-stage cards. `SiegeCardRewardTest#evolvedPreviewMovesComeFromEvolvedForm` now checks the rewritten hand, not just the preview payload. Confirmed against the reported cards: `generoot` (RARE, depth 2) -> LARGE, `droxyl` (UNCOMMON, depth 1) -> MEDIUM, which is the 1.48 vs 1.19 gap the screenshot was missing.

Original prompt: Hiring a merc throws off the creature info in landscape and portrait

- August 16, 2026 A rented mercenary makes a **fourth** body on `.ally-line` (the warband caps at 3 — `SiegeContentService#PARTY_MAX`), and the unit plates were not built for it. Two independent defects, both reproduced before being fixed. First, `.sprite` had no `min-width:0`: the name line is `white-space:nowrap`, which gives the flex item a min-content width it refuses to shrink past, so four sprites could not fit the row and the outermost plate was pushed **outside** `.battle-stage` — measured at `left:-20px` against a stage starting at 10px, which is exactly the clipped `04/124` in the reported portrait screenshot. The landscape lane already carried `min-width:0`, which is why only portrait lost a plate off the edge. Second, `.sp-tags` (hp / shield / attack buff / status chips) is a `nowrap` flex row with no ellipsis to fall back on, so once the plates narrowed it spilled straight over the neighbouring unit at every viewport — the overwritten plates in the landscape screenshot. `.sprite` now declares `flex:0 1 auto; min-width:0` and `.sp-tags` wraps (`flex-wrap:wrap; column-gap:5px; row-gap:2px`), so the stat row stacks to a second line instead of painting over the next Siegeling. Third, smaller: a rental's server name is literally `"X (Merc)"` (`SiegeContentService#toMercCombatant`), and on a 65px plate that suffix left nothing of the creature — `renderSpriteLine` now badges the role (`.sp-merc`, same treatment the `Shade` prefix already gets) and keeps the name, with the badge hidden on phone-sized plates where the existing `.sp-shade` is hidden too. So the rental stays distinguishable there without spending width, `.sprite.merc .sp-plate` gets a gold border tint. Client-side only — the server name and every battle-log line are untouched. Cache pins: `adventure.css` 56 -> 57 and `adventure.js` 62 -> 63 on `adventure.html` (the only page that includes either). The three exact-literal pin assertions those bumps would have broken (two in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest`, one in `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest`) were converted to `>=` floors, extending the `assetPin` treatment the repo already applies to `game.js`/`style.css`/`action-queue.js`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `adventure.js`; full `./mvnw test` **525/525** green, including a new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#siegeUnitPlatesSurviveAFourthUnitOnTheLine` pinning the three declarations the layout depends on. The behaviour was measured, not eyeballed: `tests/siege-merc-party-layout-check.cjs` renders the reported party (Generoot / Droxyl / Purseus + Shellnaut (Merc), with the shields, attack buffs and evolution gauge from the screenshot) at **390x844, 844x390, 932x430 and 1920x1080** and asserts every plate contains its own content, no two plates on a line overlap, and nothing leaves the `overflow:hidden` stage. **Against the pre-fix CSS it fails 11 assertions** — `sp-tags` spilling 3-16px at every viewport, and in portrait Generoot's plate at `-20..95` against a stage of `10..380`. After the fix all four viewports pass with zero overflow, and the screenshots show four separated plates with the stat row wrapped to two lines on the narrow ones. Rows that clip themselves by ellipsis (`.sp-name`, `.sp-gauge`) are deliberately excluded from the overflow assertion — they contain their own content and were never the defect.

Original prompt: The siegeling cutouts in battlegrounds are missing, make sure the hud is consistent for siege and battlegrounds mode. And images display correctly. See bottom missing art, even in continue. Also make sure siege and battlegrounds save are visually different and you can have one save for both modes displayed here

- August 16, 2026 Three Battlegrounds defects with one cause: the mode was carried as a thin copy of the expedition rather than a first-class one. **Missing art.** A Battlegrounds squad is rebuilt from banked veteran snapshots, and those snapshots store stats, not art — `rebuildMemberCombatant` and `rebuildKnightCombatant` passed a literal `null` for `artUrl`, so every rebuilt Siegeling was served with nothing to draw and fell back to its element glyph on the battlefield, in the map party rail, and on the resume prompt. Both rebuilds now resolve art from the catalog (`sieglingArtUrl` by `sourceCardId`, `knightArtUrl` by knight id), and `serializeCombatant` gained an `artUrlOf` fallback that resolves the display card's art whenever a combatant carries none — that second half is what makes an **already-banked** run render, since its persisted combatants have no art at all. The knight party chip also stopped hardcoding a 🛡️ glyph: it draws `knight.artUrl` and keeps the glyph as the fallback it was meant to be. **HUD parity.** Battlegrounds was folding its tier and boon count into the gold chip's text while Siege showed a bare chip, so the two modes' headers had different shapes. Both now lead with the same `run-slot-badge` element (`🏳️ Siege Expedition` / `⚔️ Battlegrounds · Tier II`) followed by the identical chip order; only the badge differs. **One save per mode.** There was a single account checkpoint document, so starting either mode silently discarded the other's save and the resume prompt could only ever describe one unlabelled run. A new `RunSlot` enum (EXPEDITION for STANDARD/ENDLESS, BATTLEGROUNDS) keys the account checkpoint: `SiegeCheckpointStore` gained `loadAllForUser` plus slot-aware save/load/delete, `activeRun` returns `runs[]` (keeping `run` for the old single-run shape), and every run payload carries `slot`/`slotLabel`. **EXPEDITION deliberately keeps the original unsuffixed document id**, so saves written before slots existed still resume. The resume screen was rebuilt around it: one card per save, each with the mode badge, its own gold/floor/mid-battle chips, its own party strip and its own Continue / Start Over, plus a colour-coded left edge (orange Siege, violet Battlegrounds). Abandoning one save no longer clears a device token pointing at the other and re-reads what is left instead of assuming the roster. Cache pins: `adventure.css` 57 -> 58 and `adventure.js` 63 -> 64 on `adventure.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `adventure.js`; full `./mvnw test` **529/529** green. `SiegeModeSaveSlotTest` is new and drives a **stub catalog on purpose** — the classpath catalog carries no art (art lives in the Firestore overrides), so testing against the live one would compare null to null and pass without proving anything: it asserts a rebuilt BG squad and its veteran knight come back with their card art, that a unit stored with no art still serializes the catalog's, that stored art still wins over the fallback, and that the two slots resolve to different documents with EXPEDITION unchanged. `SiegeRunSaveTest` now also pins that a standard run lands in the EXPEDITION slot. Two `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` guards were updated to the new boot/restart shape and extended to pin the whole-list boot check and the shared mode badge. The client half was measured in headless Chromium by `tests/siege-mode-saves-check.cjs` (new, in-repo) at **390x844 and 1920x1080**, stubbing `/api/siege/run/active` with one save per mode and driving the real boot path: the resume screen renders two cards, labelled `🏳️ Siege Expedition` and `⚔️ Battlegrounds · Tier II`, with different accent colours (`rgb(255,154,60)` vs `rgb(180,125,255)`), the Battlegrounds card carrying its own `⚔ Battle in progress · Round 3` chip, each card with its own Continue and Start Over, and **every party chip drawing its art rather than a glyph** — knight included. It then clicks Continue on each and compares the map HUD: identical child order (`mapMode,mapGold,mapDeckCount,inventoryBtn,extractBtn,mapHint`) with different badges, and the tier no longer inside the gold chip. Zero page errors at both viewports.

- August 16, 2026 (deploy) Shipped the four Siege fixes above to production. PR #712 merged to `main` as `eaacc339`, starting Deploy run [31970861690](https://github.com/SiegelingsGame/SiegelingTCG/actions/runs/31970861690) at 20:33:00Z; **completed success at 20:39:39Z** (both jobs — Cloud Run and Firebase Hosting). Live verification, checked rather than assumed: `/siege` now serves `adventure.css?v=58` and `adventure.js?v=64` (the pins this PR bumped); `/api/cards/editor` answers `source: FIRESTORE`, `liveEditingEnabled: true`, `firestoreAvailable: true` — not the `CLASSPATH_RESOURCE` that signals a stale deploy; `/api/siege/roster` answers 200 in 1.88s. The deployed bundles were fetched and **byte-compared against the merged commit** — `diff` reports live `adventure.js` (197,868 bytes) and live `adventure.css` (146,101 bytes) identical to the repo at `eaacc339`, so every change in this PR is what production is running: `upgradeHandCards`/`isMerc`/`sp-merc`/`runSlotBadgeText`/`resumeOrRoster`/`active.runs` in the JS, and `flex:0 1 auto; min-width:0`, the wrapping `.sp-tags`, `.sprite.merc .sp-plate` and the `.resume-save`/`run-slot-badge` rules in the CSS.
Original prompt: Landscape view of the end battle screen is unusable on mobile. Need to utilize more space you can't see the leveling information

- August 16, 2026 The Siege victory-reward screen (`rewardScreen` / `.epiphany-screen`) was unusable in phone landscape: the layout is a vertical stack (header -> leveling recap -> three choice cards -> Skip), and a ~390px-tall viewport has nowhere to put it, so the recap was either squeezed behind its own scrollbar or dropped entirely (`.epiphany-screen .xp-recap{display:none}` in the `max-width:900px and max-height:520px` query) while the cards still ran off the bottom edge — exactly the reported screenshot. Fix is a sideways split instead of more shrinking: in `@media (orientation:landscape) and (max-width:979px) and (max-height:600px)` the `.epiphany-screen` becomes a CSS grid with a narrow left rail (`minmax(196px,29%)`) carrying the title and the full `xp-recap`, and a wide right column carrying the reward card row over the Skip button (`grid-template-areas: "head cards" / "recap cards" / "recap actions"`). The rail restores `display:block` on the recap (beating both short-viewport queries above it by source order) with `max-height:none` and its own `overflow:auto`, so a full four-member warband is readable without touching the cards; the subtitle and the ✦ flourish are dropped since the rail no longer needs their rows, and the reward grid loses the base `min-height:390px` that was pushing the row below the fold. Portrait, tablet and desktop are untouched — every change is inside the existing phone-landscape query. Cache pin: `adventure.css` 56 -> 57 on `adventure.html` (its only includer).
- Verification: headless Chromium against the real `adventure.html` with the reward screen forced on and a four-member recap plus a three-card spread injected, at **844x390, 926x428, 740x360** (landscape), **390x844** (portrait) and **1920x1080** (desktop). At all three landscape sizes all **4/4 recap rows are visible with no inner scroll**, the recap rail sits left (e.g. 844x390: recap 10-249px, cards 261-834px), the card row ends above the Skip button, and `.epiphany-screen` reports **0px of vertical overflow** — nothing below the fold. Portrait (recap stacked above a swipeable card carousel) and desktop (centered column) render byte-identically to before the change.
Original prompt: Add a menu were you can open and view the cards in your hand bottom left ui might work. Make sure it works on all screens. , standardize the size of all cards so ones with more text don't appear larger than others.

- August 16, 2026 Two Siege battle-hand changes. **(1) Uniform cards.** `.playcard` was width-fixed but height-content-sized, so a card with a longer description stood taller than its neighbours — it broke the fan's arc and read as "this card is bigger". Cards now take a fixed `--playcard-h` alongside `--playcard-w` at every breakpoint (152px base, 104px phone portrait where the description is hidden, 98px phone landscape, `clamp(132px,19vh,170px)` on tablet/desktop landscape where it is shown again), are `display:flex; flex-direction:column`, and the description is the one elastic row (`flex:1 1 auto` + `-webkit-line-clamp:3`) that absorbs the leftover space and clamps its overflow. Card overflow stays `visible` on purpose — the AP cost badge is hung outside the top-left corner and clipping it was the obvious wrong way to do this. **(2) Hand sheet.** The fan only ever shows a few cards at once and hides descriptions entirely on phones, so there was no way to read your whole hand. The bottom-left deck counter (`#deckCounts`) is now a button — restyled as a pill, `aria-haspopup="dialog"` + `aria-expanded` — that opens `#handSheet`, a standard `.unit-modal` listing every card in hand at full size with its description, sorted by the same `sortHandByOwner` order as the fan. It closes on the ✕, on backdrop click, and on Escape; it re-renders on every battle render so draws/plays/end-turn keep it truthful, and it closes itself when the battle ends or the screen changes. Tapping a card in the sheet closes it and hands that card back to the fan **focused** — the same state tapping it in the fan gives it, which is what surfaces the drag hint — and scrolls it to the middle when the fan is scrollable at all (in landscape the whole fan is centered and does not scroll, so the focus highlight is the cue). The card face is now built by one shared `playCardMarkup`/`playCardClass` pair used by both the fan and the sheet, so the two cannot drift. Layout: `repeat(auto-fill,minmax(126px,1fr))` at 176px tall, and a short-landscape query widens the sheet to `min(940px,96vw)` with 114px columns at 142px tall so a full hand still reads in two rows. Cache pins: `adventure.css` 57 -> 58 and `adventure.js` 62 -> 63 on `adventure.html`.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on `adventure.js`. The behavioural check is `tests/siege-hand-sheet-check.cjs`, kept in-repo — it drives a **real expedition against a locally running app** (knight + warband select, walks the map into the first battle) and then asserts at **390x844, 844x390, 1024x768 and 1920x1080**. All four viewports pass all twelve assertions with zero page errors. Card sizes are measured with `offsetWidth/offsetHeight` (the fan's rotate/scale transform makes bounding rects differ by design): every card in a six-card hand reports **one distinct height and one distinct width** per viewport (104x104 portrait, 94x98 phone landscape, 104x146 tablet, 138x170 desktop) while the descriptions in that same hand range from 17 to 41 characters — the exact condition that used to produce different sizes. On the sheet: it starts closed, opens from the counter with `aria-expanded="true"`, lists 6 of 6 hand cards with a description visible on all 6, renders them at a single uniform size, sits fully inside the viewport at every size, closes on Escape, and picking a card closes the sheet with that card focused in the fan (and centered wherever the fan can scroll). Screenshots were reviewed at each viewport; the cost badge overlapping the title in the tighter grid was caught that way and fixed with a title indent.
Original prompt: I completed a run, I found spoutyl and Generoot on that run, since I found spoutly I should have unlocked him as selectable from the start. Generoot is already available through squire bud but I should also earn the evolution of a higher tier siegeling if found as well

- August 16, 2026 Siegelings found on an expedition now become permanent starter picks. Before this, warband-select availability was **purely a static catalog flag** (`SieglingCard.expeditionStarter`, surfaced by `SiegeContentService.isExpeditionStarter`): nothing a run did could ever change it, so meeting Spoutyl on the path and finishing the run left it reading "WATER · locked — find them on the expedition path to recruit them" forever. SiegeKnights already had an account-level unlock (`siegeUnlockedKnights` + `isSiegeKnightUnlocked`); this mirrors that shape for Siegelings. `SiegeRun` gains an insertion-ordered `discoveredSieglingIds`, appended at every path where a Siegeling joins the warband (starting party, staged/post-battle recruits, both broker branches, elite reward recruits — all six share the `deckCardsFor(s, member.getId())` line) **and at both evolution sites** in `SiegeCombatEngine`, because reaching a higher stage is a discovery too. The set rides in the run snapshot so a resume does not forget it. At the end-of-run payout (the block that already banks gold/remnants/lifetime stats) `bankSieglingDiscoveries` resolves each found id through the new `SiegeContentService.baseFormId` — which walks `evolvesFromId` down to the stage-1 root — and banks **both the root and the found form** into a new `PlayerProgressionEntity.siegeUnlockedSieglings` (Firestore payload + reader updated to match). That is the "found a higher tier, earn the line" rule: warband select only ever lists stage-1 cards, so unlocking a stage-2 find without its base would unlock nothing pickable. `PlayerProgressionService` gains `isSiegeSieglingUnlocked` / `unlockSiegeSieglings` (normalized ids, already-unlocked entries skipped rather than rejected — a run routinely re-finds what you own), and `SiegeService.roster` now reports `expeditionStarter` as *catalog flag OR account unlock*. Two deliberate choices, both confirmed with the owner: unlocks bank **when the run ends** (win or lose) rather than at the moment of the find, and the picker stays stage-1 only. The result screen gained a green "🔓 New starter Siegelings" panel listing first-time unlocks by name (a re-found Siegeling says nothing). Also converted four **literal** adventure cache pins in `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` / `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest` to `>=` floors via the existing `assetPin` helper — the same fix the repo already applied to `game.js`/`style.css`; note `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest` was **already red on origin/main** (it pinned `adventure.js?v=60` against a shipped v=62). Cache pins: `adventure.css` 58 -> 59, `adventure.js` 63 -> 64 on `adventure.html`.
- Verification: full `./mvnw test` **529/529 green** (up from 527 with the two static-asset guards fixed). Seven of them are the new `SiegeSieglingUnlockTest`, which drives the real Spring context: `baseFormId` walks a real catalog evolution to a card that `stageOf` reports as stage 1 and is idempotent on a root; banking a discovery flips `isSiegeSieglingUnlocked` and returns the name for the result screen; **finding an evolution unlocks both its stage-1 base and the found form**; re-banking the same find announces nothing and stores no duplicate; discoveries survive a `snapshotRun` round trip; starting a real run via `siegeService.newRun` records its whole warband as discovered (proving the join hook fires rather than assuming it); and `roster()` reports a previously-unlocked Siegeling as `expeditionStarter: true` — that last one stubs `AccountService`/`PlayerProgressionService` through the service's own fields, because the unlock is per-account and this environment has no Firestore credentials. `node --check` clean on `adventure.js`.
- August 16, 2026 (merge) Brought `claude/mobile-landscape-battle-screen-iyuyy1` up to date with `main` after PRs #712/#713 landed the Battlegrounds save-slot and sprite-size work. Four conflicts, all resolved toward main where it had done the same job better: `AdventureLinePuzzleJavaScriptTest` and `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` had **independently converted the same literal adventure cache pins to floors** (main's `adventureJsPin`/`adventureCssPin` helpers replace the `assetPin` calls added on this branch — same guarantee, main's naming); `progress.md` keeps both histories in order; and `adventure.html` needed a real bump rather than a pick, because both sides had independently landed on `adventure.js?v=64` for **different** bundles — shipping either verbatim would have served one branch's JS under the other's URL. Pins therefore advance past both: `adventure.css` 60 and `adventure.js` 65. (Supersedes the 58->59 / 63->64 numbers recorded in this branch's earlier entries.) The semantic merges auto-resolved cleanly: the discovery hook sits alongside main's new `upgradeHandCards` call at both evolution sites, and the hand sheet, uniform card sizing, and starter-unlock code all survived intact.
- Verification: full `./mvnw test` on the merged tree **536/536 green** (this branch's 7 new cases plus main's). `node --check` clean on `adventure.js`. `tests/siege-hand-sheet-check.cjs` was re-run against the merged build — a real expedition driven through main's rewritten resume/boot path into a battle — and passes all 12 assertions at **390x844, 844x390, 1024x768 and 1920x1080** with zero page errors, so main's resume-screen rework did not disturb the hand sheet or the uniform card sizing.

## 2026-08-24 — Hide custom deck controls while decks load

While the Decks page was fetching the card catalog / owned data, the premade grid
showed a spinner but the "Custom / Your saved decks" block below it still rendered
its header, the Create Custom Deck button and saved deck tiles — pressers that do
nothing because the catalog they open the builder against has not arrived. The
loading branches in `renderDecks`/`renderSavedDecks` now share a `decksLoading()`
predicate and hide the whole `.builder-browser` block (via the global `.hidden`
class) until data lands, so only the loading state is visible; `home.js` cache-bust
bumped to v147 in `home.html`.

Verified with `node --check` on `home.js` and a headless-Chromium check at 390x844
serving the statics over `python3 -m http.server`: with no API responses the deck
grid reads "Loading your decks…" and `#decksSection .builder-browser` reports
`isVisible() === false` (it was `true` before the change).

## 2026-08-24 — Hide premade heading while decks load

Follow-up: the "PREMADE / Official battle decks" heading still labelled an empty
row during the deck load. `renderDecks` now also toggles that row head with
`setPremadeHeadVisible()`, so a loading Decks page shows only the page title and
the spinner. `home.js` cache-bust bumped to v148.

Verified headless at 390x844 with no API responses: `#decksSection` visible text
is "DECKS | Premade and custom decks | Loading your decks…", with both the premade
row head and the custom block reporting `isVisible() === false`.

## 2026-08-24 — Actionable deck save/play errors, and no more silently shrunk custom decks

Reported: saving a 30/30 custom deck failed with an unhelpful popup, and "Play Custom"
started a match that lost the deck. Two causes, both fixed.

1. **Silently shrunk decks.** `getBuilderSelectedCards()` in `game.js` dropped any card
   id missing from `gameOptions.cardCatalog` without a word, so a 30-card loadout could
   reach `/api/game/new` as a short list and be rejected as if the deck were empty —
   which reads to the player as the deck vanishing. `startSelectedGame` now checks the
   builder counts and a saved deck's `customDeckCards` with `getUnknownLoadoutCardIds()`
   and refuses to start, naming the missing cards and telling the player to edit the deck.

2. **Unactionable errors.** Every deck rejection was a bare `alert()` (client) or a raw
   `IllegalArgumentException` message / bare 500 (server). Validation errors now throw
   `DeckValidationException` carrying the deck-builder control at fault ("name",
   "trainer", "cards"); `AuthController` returns it as `{error, field}` and catches
   unexpected `RuntimeException`s with a readable message instead of a 500.
   `validateCustomDeckOwnership` canonicalizes card ids (dashboard copy-suffixed ids no
   longer read as unowned) and reports card names and the actual owned count instead of
   a raw id. On the client, `setBuilderIssue()` renders an in-page `.builder-issue`
   callout in the deck builder, switches to the pane/settings that own the problem,
   scrolls it into view and flashes a `.needs-fix` outline on the exact control; editing
   that control clears the issue.

Verified: `./mvnw test` green (552 tests) including two new regressions in
`GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` (`deckSaveFailuresPointAtTheControlToFix`,
`unknownCustomDeckCardsBlockTheStartInsteadOfShrinkingTheDeck`); `node --check` on
`home.js` and `game.js`; headless Chromium at 390x844 confirming the callout renders
with the new CSS (full-width banner, 84px tall) on `home.html`. Cache-bust bumps:
`home.js` v149, `home.css` v135, `game.js` v251. The existing
`failedCatalogFetchNeverEmptiesTheBinder` assertion was updated for the shared
`decksLoading()` predicate introduced earlier today.

## 2026-08-24 — Quick delete for saved custom decks

Saved custom decks could only be created, never removed, so duplicate "Fire and Water"
tiles piled up on the Decks screen. Each custom tile now carries a Delete button beside
Edit that arms on the first tap ("Delete?", red pulse, auto-disarms after 4s) and calls
the existing `POST /api/profile/decks/delete` on the second — no native confirm to
dismiss on a phone, and no single mis-tap destroying a deck. A failed delete renders the
server's reason in a `.builder-issue` banner above the grid instead of an alert, and a
successful one clears `state.selectedDeckId` when the deleted deck was the active
loadout, so the next Play tap can't start a match against a missing deck id.

Verified: headless Chromium at 390x844 driving the real page against stubbed endpoints —
first tap shows "Delete?" with no request sent, second tap posts `{id:"d1"}` and the tile
disappears (2 → 1), the armed state reverts to "Delete" after the 4s timeout, and a
stubbed error response leaves both tiles in place with the banner text shown. Delete tap
target measures 58x34 px, matching the neighbouring Edit button. Plus `node --check`,
and a new `savedDeckTilesDeleteInTwoTapsAndClearTheActiveSelection` regression in
`GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` (76 tests green). Cache-bust: `home.js` v150,
`home.css` v136.

## 2026-08-24 — Deck delete (and save) now update the shared profile cache

Reported: delete "not working on state". The request and the server delete were fine —
`deleteSavedDeck` updated `state.profile` but never wrote `sieglingsAuthProfile` in
localStorage. That cache is what paints the hub before `/api/auth/me` returns and, more
importantly, is the list `game.js` renders the Play page's saved decks from, so a deleted
deck came back on the next load and stayed selectable on Play. `saveCustomDeck` had the
same gap in the other direction (a freshly saved deck missing from the cache).
Both now call `saveCachedAuthProfile(data)` with the server's response.

Verified headless at 390x844 against stubbed endpoints: after the delete the cached
profile lists only the surviving deck id (`d2`); with the new line removed it still
listed the deleted `d1`, confirming the diagnosis. Regression extended in
`GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` (76 green). Cache-bust: `home.js` v151.
